## Chapter 1

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

hello friends

fair warning I've never wrote an atla fic. But these two idiots (sokka and zuko) are my favorites as I'm sure they're probably yours too. this fic is very Zuko centric, you'll notice that pretty quickly. The first few chapters are going to be basically a lengthy "Zuko Alone" where he travels through the earth kingdom meeting various characters both canon and not.

### Text

*“You're just a child.”*

*“Well, you’re just a teenager”*

He’d tried to capture the Avatar, he *had* captured the Avatar. Of course he’d escaped rather quickly, something Zuko would prefer no one ever found out. Of course, as usual the universe did not care in the slightest what Zuko wanted and did the exact opposite. *Zhao* had found out, of all people, that good for nothing, creepy bastard had interrogated *his* crew. Those were his men, his uncle’s men. They were people that despite what anyone said about him, had stood by him. He might not always be the most level headed but he understood loyalty, and for the most part, he men *had* been loyal. Then Zhao, the coward, had fired at his back after Zuko had bested him in their Agni Kai. If this world was at all just Agni would rid him of his bending for the dishonor.

*“Even in exile my nephew is more honorable than you.”*

Zuko was sitting in his quarters, the events of the past few days echoing in his mind. He didn’t feel honorable. Was it because of his failure? He’d lost the Avatar. Capturing him was his only way home. It had been three years since his banishment, sent off to find someone that had been missing for a hundred years. Zuko wasn’t a fool, he knew his father had sent him on a fool's errand, but he had a chance now, he had to try right? He should want to capture the Avatar, not just for himself, but for the success of his nation. He’s the Fire Prince, even in exile he should support his nation, right? Yes. That’s exactly what he *should* do.

Zuko hadn’t found an over a hundred year-old, fully realised Avatar though, he’d found a child. A boy that was probably younger than him when he’d been banished. He’s a kid, a kid who surrendered himself for the safety of a single village, one that many others in his positions would’ve still burned to the ground for the crime of harboring the Avatar. The Fire Lord may be disappointed he hadn’t.

A soft knock echoing against the metal walls pulled his attention, “Prince Zuko, you’ve spent quite a while tucked away in your quarters, perhaps you would join the crew and I for music night?” his uncle’s voice spoke through the door.

“No–” Zuko lets out a sigh, soon finding himself opening the door to his uncle standing out in the hallway, “ –tell the captain to set course toward the Earth Kingdom mainland.”

The dark haired boy didn’t miss the slightly dejected look on his uncle’s face at his refusal to join the crew’s music night. It wasn’t as though Zuko didn’t enjoy music, actually he quite enjoyed the arts overall, something his father didn’t particularly approve of, but he wasn’t looking to become friends with his crew.

“May I ask why you want to head to the Earth Kingdom, nephew?”

“I am… reassessing a few things,” he spoke in a way that made it clear he didn’t plan to elaborate, but the slight smile he received from his uncle was enough to tell him the man wasn’t displeased at the response. Making his uncle proud was something he strived for, even if he’d never admit it. So often making his uncle proud seemed to contradict what he *should* do, even if his uncle rarely said it, he was not all that hard to read, if anything his expressions made more sense than his words ever did.

—-

Zuko and Iroh had split from his crew when they reached a port on the mainland of the Earth Kingdom. There were plenty of rumors of where the Avatar and his traveling companions were, seeming now to be common knowledge, or at least a common rumor that the Avatar has returned.

Then there were the posters, wanted posters, from the Fire Nation of not only the Avatar’s group, but of Zuko. He saw them begin popping up not more than a week after arriving in the Earth Kingdom. It seemed someone, likely Zhao, had told the Fire Lord of his decision not to chase the Avatar.

“You’ll be safer without me uncle, father is not looking to arrest you, he has not branded you a traitor, but if you stay with me–”

“Nephew, please. All that is needed is that we blend in. The Earth Kingdom is a large nation, with many people. I believe as long as we are careful, we will be fine.”

That day they cut their hair, the shorter hair helping them not look so obviously Fire Nation, of course the set of clothes they’d *procured,* helped as well. Though anyone who paid enough attention might have questions about Zuko’s golden eyes. They traveled debating where would be best to hide out in the Earth Kingdom, after traveling for a long while, including through the Great Divide, they ended upon another town, one that the both quickly noticed a Fire Nation presence in. It wasn’t an official colony, not as far as they knew, but Zuko wasn’t exactly comfortable with them venturing into town.

“We should just go around, we can cut through the forest,” the teen suggested.

“We need supplies, I will go, I am less *recognisable,”* his uncle tried to be subtle. It wasn’t entirely true, Iroh was a famous general, but many people overlook a simple old man.

“Fine. I’ll find us a place to camp.” Zuko didn’t give him time to argue, bitter at his failed subtlety, mentioning one of the worst moments of his life. He stalked off into the forest.

—-

If Zuko needed any more proof that the universe hates him then venturing into some random forest and ending up facing off against another teenager, a boy with dual hook swords who did his best to match Zuko’s skills with his broadswords would do it. It was only when he’d managed to disarm the teen of his hook swords that the others waiting in the trees jumped down.

“I’ll remove my swords if you call them off,” he spoke, golden eyes glaring down at the other teen pinned by his swords.

“Fine,” the boy bit out as he did a hand movement that seemed like he was waving the others off, all of them settling from their fighting stances. Zuko pulled back, sheathing his dual blades as promised.

He was tackled a moment later, narrowly avoiding eating the dirt. Someone much larger than him was sitting on his back, his arms pulled behind him and quickly tied.

“What are you doing?” he yelled, thrashing against the weight on his back, though it did no good.

“You really thought I’d trust *you*?” He retrieved his hooked swords, one point in the direction of Zuko’s face.

“You attacked me!”

“Because you’re Fire Nation! Probably sent here to spy on me.”

Zuko could barely believe what he was hearing, yeah sure he figured people might think he’s from the colonies, he doesn’t exactly look like he’s from the Earth Kingdom, but thinking he's a Fire Nation spy? What would be the point? If these kids were that much of an issue they’d just take them out.

“Why would the Fire Nation care about some kids in the forest!?”

“Because we’ve been giving them trouble since they moved into this area. They want us silenced.”

“If they wanted you silenced they would send someone to kill you, not spy on you.”

Zuko was getting really tired of this *conversation*, and it was getting difficult to breath with someone sitting on his back. He’d rather not firebend in this situation, he’s both outnumbered and in a pretty dense part of the forest, that only spells chaos. He knows better than to bend in a densely wooded area if he doesn’t want the whole place going up in flames.

“You expect me to believe you aren’t working for the Fire Nation? You look like you could be their poster boy–” the teen focused on his face, “ –well, except maybe *that*.”

The teen seemed to be focusing on the burn more now, brown eyes narrowing at him. Did he recognize him? The posters looked off with his hair cut and looking slightly older than he was drawn in them. Was he deciding if Zuko could be trusted? That seemed unlikely.

“Get him up, we’re going back to base.”

—-

It was proof enough that they didn’t think he was a firebender when he was thrown in a wooden room, door locked from the outside. It seemed like it was probably a store room usually but had been cleared out. He could just leave, but he didn’t know where they’d taken his swords, and they’d know the second he escaped because there was someone outside the door. He could wait for uncle to find him but that could be a while, the old man was never never quick when it came to shopping.

“So what exactly is the point of this?” Zuko called out to the person at the other side of the door.

“Shut up,” a voice that sounds younger than his own bites back.

“You don’t know then?” he questions.

“I said shut up.”

“Were you there earlier today? When your group decided to capture me?”

“Yes, now be quiet.”

Zuko had gotten an answer, it was a start. He did honestly care much about why he was captured, but he was curious why a bunch of kids thought they were causing enough trouble for the Fire Nation that they’d send a spy.

“You know if I wanted to kill your leader, I’m assuming the guy with the hook swords is your leader, I could have. I stood down, if you were there then you saw that. And if the Fire Nation wanted to spy on you they’d send someone more inconspicuous.”

“His name’s Jet and he’s a good leader. We’re gonna run you and your army of ashmakers out of the valley.”

“You sure you should be telling me that?”

“You aren’t getting out of here so I don’t see the harm.”

That was certainly some confirmation of their plans. He didn’t exactly put it past Jet to kill him, he seemed pretty set on it when they were fighting, but would he actually be able to? Zuko knows he should have a bit more fear at the situation he’s in, but at the very least he’s pretty certain he can get away, he’d just like to be able to find his dao blades before leaving. Still getting out of some rope and a wooden room as a firebender is much easier than when he’s faced earthbenders trying to restrain him. Besides he’s faced someone trying to kill him enough times that it really doesn’t phase him. He lets the conversation die though, it’s best if they think he’s afraid.

—-

*“We were relying on instincts.”*

*“You’ll get yourself killed doing that.”*

Sokka wanted nothing more than the punch that smug look off Jet’s face. They guy was infuriatingly annoying, and his *instincts* told him something was off about this guy.

Later that night they were at the Freedom Fighters hideout, eating with the group. Jet deciding to give some rallying speech.

*“Now, the Fire Nation thinks they don’t have to worry about a couple of kids hiding in the* *trees. Maybe they’re right–” the table booed the comment as Jet’s lips curved into a smirk. “* *–or maybe, they’re dead wrong.”*

Sokka had been pretty dead set on leaving that night, as far as he was concerned it was a waste of time being here, they’d do more good by getting to the North Pole so Aang could learn waterbending. But Jet had brought up a mission, and something told Sokka that he needed to stay for it.

Walking around the hideout, mostly just curious how they structured the whole thing, Sokka noticed one of the few proper rooms –as most of the rooms were more tents on platforms than rooms– always had someone standing guard in front of it. Did they have a prisoner? They didn’t seem like the taking prisoners type. Maybe it was their weapons? Though with how well hidden their base is it seemed a bit pointless to have someone always guarding it. The more likely answer was a prisoner, which Sokka really wasn’t a fan of.

*“He beat and robbed a defenseless old man!”*

*“I want to hear Jet’s side of the story.”*

Sokka was gonna lose it, how could no one else see what was going on. Jet was crazy! He didn’t care about if someone was a soldier or a civilian, that’s a dangerous line to cross. How could Katara and Aang not see that? How could his own sister continuously take Jet’s side?

*“I’m sorry, Katara. Jet’s very smooth but we can’t trust him.”*

*“You know what I think? You’re jealous that he’s a better warrior and a better leader.”*

*“Katara, I’m not jealous of Jet. It’s just that my instincts–”*

*“Well my instincts–”*

“He’s keeping a prisoner, Katara! After attacking an old man, what do you think he’d do to someone he’s keeping a prisoner? You really want to defend that?”

“Where would they even keep a prisoner? And that old man was an assassin! He had a poisoned knife, Sokka!”

“There wasn’t any knife! Jet’s lying, Katara! There’s a store–”

“I’m not listening to this. Jet’s trying to save the forest, and we’re going to help him. Come on, Aang.”

Sokka was going to figure out what Jet was up to, and who he was keeping as a prisoner. It was late in the night when Sokka snuck from their hut, retracing his memory to the storeroom from before. As he was on his way he heard movement, hiding as he watched Freedom Fighters lowering to the forest floor. He struggled with the decision of what to do, should he follow them or take the chance to free their prisoner while they’re gone. He was near enough to the storeroom that he believed he could probably catch up after. As he got there he watched the current guard crumble in front of who from the side looked like a teen with short, scruffy black hair and gold eyes. He was fairly familiar, but Sokka couldn’t exactly pinpoint who the boy was. He didn’t seem to pay Sokka any mind, quickly heading off in the other direction. He tried to shake off the weird moment, remembering he wanted to catch up to the Freedom Fighter to find out what they were planning.

—-

Zuko could hear the sound of multiple people leaving that night, if anytime was gonna be the time to escape it was now. He was pretty sure the person outside the door was still there and from the creaking nearby there was one other person close, he could take down two people, easy. The ropes around his wrist were never tied well, they really needed to work on their knot tying, so it wasn’t hard to get them off even without firebending. He kicked open the door, not giving the guard a moment to think before he jabbed his elbow into the other teen’s necks successfully knocking them out. He immediately saw the water tribe boy from the day he’d failed to capture the Avatar. What was he doing here? He wasn’t with them before. He’d thought he was going crazy when he’d recognised the Avatar’s voice earlier that day, but maybe he wasn’t. Whatever the reason he was here, he wasn’t coming after Zuko right now and he didn’t really care to attack him, he was trying to stay away from anyone who might lead his father or Zhao to finding him, even if not purposely. He turned away, heading off in the direction that he thought that weapons store might be based on his day of eavesdropping. After a few minutes of searching Zuko had his blades back at his side, his bag from before, and a small pack of supplies, the least they could do for attempting to keep him a prisoner. Now he was lowering himself down a large tree to the forest floor.

He knew he should find his uncle, the man was probably worried sick, but if a couple of kids could recognise him as fire nation, how long would it be till someone made the connection between him and the posters? Not many people had a burn scar taking up nearly half their face. His only safety was the way it had been altered for the poster, the fact that it vaguely looked like a hand had been entirely removed. His hair cut hopefully helped, but because of how short it was it didn’t do any favors in covering his scar. His uncle would be safer without him, he didn’t have posters up asking for his head, and his eyes were less notable, still a fire nation amber, but not the distinct gold of Zuko’s, he’d have an easier time going unnoticed.

He’d have to leave his uncle behind, that would be safest. They should’ve done that from the beginning. So he didn’t search for his Uncle in the forest, he avoided the town, and quickly moved on, where to? He didn’t know.

#### Chapter Notes

hi friends

I've decided to update twice a week until I run out of chapters that I'm ahead on (hopefully that won't happen). It'll probably be Wednesdays and Saturdays that I update.

Anyways hope you enjoy the chapter.

### Text

A storm raged through the sky, Zuko hiding out in a fishing town for the night, luckily finding an old woman who allowed him to stay in exchange for helping her move some heavy crates inside her shop so they wouldn’t get rained on. The woman, Min, lives in a small home in the town’s center, with her daughter who had recently fallen ill, and her three young grandchildren, all of whom took to Zuko pretty much immediately. He’d nearly introduced himself as Zuko when she’d asked his name, so now they all thought his name was Zuli, which didn’t really help with the whole hiding that he’s Fire Nation thing, and is generally a woman’s name. No one had mentioned either of those things though, and they still invited him to stay for the night.

Li Yan, the oldest of Min’s grandchildren, though still only six, had been following Zuko around pretty much all day. Zuko would never say he’s good with kids, he didn’t have many great role models in that regard, but he didn’t mind their simple conversations throughout the day as he helped out her grandmother. She’d pretty much followed him around and occasionally tried to help as he carried boxes and bolts of fabric where they were needed. She asked him questions about himself and his travels, though was oddly astute enough to not ask about where he was from or anything much more personal than his favorite tea or his favorite play.

Zuko was sat in the family’s common room, the kitchen, living and dining areas all sharing a space. The storm had been raging for hours, and he was having trouble falling asleep so he’d opted for watching the storm through the front window. His attention was drawn from the window by the sound of creaking boards and a door opening. Li Yan tip-toed out of the room she shared with her younger sisters into the common area, a blanket pulled around her shoulders like a cape.

“What are you doing up?” the young girl questioned when she noticed Zuko. He just gave her a shrug, not really sure how to answer that, he couldn’t very well tell a child he couldn’t sleep because of nightmares.

“Tea?” he asked, almost on instinct, as his uncle always offered tea when he had trouble sleeping, given his uncle offered tea for *every* situation, and Zuko often threw it in his face, metaphorically speaking. He tried not to dwell on the thought of his uncle, how poorly he’d treated him, or how he left without a word. Thoughts like that would do him no good, his uncle is better off without him. He rose from the futon he’d been sitting on, making his way to the small kitchen. Li Yan made her way into the kitchen as well, using a stool to get out the tea from an upper cabinet. Zuko frowned a bit at the spark rocks in his hands, his eyes glancing to Li Yan for a moment to see if she was paying attention, she seemed to be searching the cabinet for the tea she wanted so he took his chance to quickly light the fire without her noticing. He hated using spark rocks, so he avoided doing so unless he couldn’t.

“Do you need help, Li Yan?” Zuko questioned, realising she was now standing on the counter top, still shuffling through the cabinet.

“You said jasmine tea is your favorite,” she commented, not leaving her search. Zuko felt a smile pull at his lips realising that she was looking for his favorite tea, it was sweet, but he wasn’t sure Min would have it in the house.

“Found it!” she cheered, thankfully not too loudly. She jumped back in her excitement, Zuko having to stop her from falling off the counter.

“You’ve got to be careful,” he warned, helping her down from the counter.

She giggled at him as she was steady on the chair, “you act like a big sibling.”

Zuko keeps his expression neutral, at least he hopes he does, as his own younger sister appears in his thoughts. Most memories of her aren’t particularly pleasant, they hadn’t gotten along in years, even before Zuko’s banishment. He was pretty certain he succeeded in schooling his expression when Li Yan didn’t call him out for it.

They spent the next few minutes brewing the tea, while waiting for it to steep they tidied the cabinet Li Yan had been searching through. In the end it definitely wasn’t his uncle’s tea, but Li Yan didn’t seem to mind it, and Zuko thought it was bearable considering his usual failures at making tea. It had made them both a bit calmer, Li Yan admitting she’d been woken up by the storm, and wasn’t very comfortable with thunder. The two sat at the table until Li Yan fell asleep in the middle of talking and Zuko ended up carrying her back to her room, careful not to wake her, before going back to clean up their cups and attempting to lay down himself. He didn’t end up getting much sleep.

—-

“Did you hear? The Avatar was in town yesterday,” a woman commented to Min as the older woman tallied up her purchase.

“I’ve been so busy lately, I’m always behind on the news. Is it true that the Avatar is just a boy?” Min questioned, putting the woman’s fabric in her basket.

“Can’t be more than thirteen, was traveling with two other kids that looked water tribe, helped out that old grumpy fisherman, and ended up getting caught out in that storm.”

Zuko had paused to listen to the conversation, it seemed the trio came out unscathed in the end, he found himself thinking that was a good thing, maybe he really is a traitor… The conversation moved on to a different topic next.

“I heard you have a new helper.” Based on her tone Zuko assumed she had heard something she didn’t like. He’d noticed a few people giving him odd looks the day before, but he’d found that that wasn’t uncommon when you have a large scar taking up much of your face, it was partly covered by his grown out hair, but it wasn’t really possible to hide the burn entirely.

“I assume you mean Zuli?” Min questioned in a calm tone, either ignoring or not noticing the other woman’s.

“Are you going to keep taking in *those* people?”

“I don’t believe I understand what you mean.” Min was giving her the option to back down.

Zuko’s fingers clenched the bottom bolt of fabric in his pile, he could practically feel his fingers turning white at what he knew the woman was implying. He’d noticed that Li Yan and her sisters looked decidedly Fire Nation, Li Yan more so than the younger two. Their mother was clearly Earth Kingdom, along with Min, but Zuko could only assume that the three girls were mixed. Li Yan had distinctly amber eyes, her skin tone similar to Zuko’s, and her hair darker than what was common in the Earth Kingdom. Both her younger sister’s also had amber eyes, but their skin tone was more similar to their mother’s as was their hair. Li Yan looked easily like she could be from the Fire Nation, if Zuko didn’t know her mother and wasn’t in the Earth Kingdom, he would’ve assumed just that.

“You house a bunch of Fire Nation brats and expect the rest of us to be okay with it!”

“Those are my grandchildren you are speaking of. They have no loyalty to the Fire Nation, nor do I. I will not shun a child because of their parentage, they did not choose to be born into this war.”

“We’ve tolerated those three because you are related, but *that boy.* What are you thinking?”

“He is just that, a boy. One who clearly doesn’t have anywhere to go. Would you really ask me to turn away a child who needs help?”

“Please, I saw those swords he wore when he walked into town, for all you know he’s a soldier. Those ashmakers would have a child fight.”

Zuko decided to attempt to stop the conversation by making his presence known, knowing that if he had to listen to much more of this he’d end up lighting something on fire. He walked out from the curtain that led to the back room, gold eye meeting with the glaring woman before he walked past to put away fabric bolts in their proper baskets. She stood watching him as he did his work, glaring all the while.

“Might as well say what you’re thinking, I won’t argue with you. Though you should know that the only nation that doesn’t start conscription at fourteen is the Water Tribe. Both the Fire Nation and the Earth Kingdom draft people starting at fourteen. Of course the Earth Kingdom only drafts men, the Fire Nation will draft anyone as long as they are of age,” Zuko spoke as he finishes putting away the fabrics.

“You spout lies about the Earth Kingdom! Proving you side with them!” She barks the words at him with gritted teeth.

Zuko sighed, he already knew this to be true, he’d come across many people traveling during his short time in the Earth Kingdom, all in an attempt to avoid forced military service. He’d come across both Earth and Fire troupes that had people younger than him among them. This was the reality of a century long war, people forced to fight for their nation whether they want to or not.

“I think it is time you leave,” Min spoke, pressing the basket of fabrics into the woman’s hands. She received an angry huff in return for she stalked out of the shop.

“I’m sorry for–”

“You have nothing to apologise for. People often forget that there is both good and bad on each side of this war. You can not be at war for any amount of time and be untainted by the evils that fester during such, even if you believe yourself on the correct side.”

Zuko found himself agreeing. He of course knew the Fire Nation had started the war, there was no ignoring that fact, but in his nation they were taught that the war was a way to spread their prosperity, that it was a way to share their greatness, the longer he was away, the more he saw of the world, the more he realised that had been a lie. The world does not see their greatness, they see them as an enemy, they are terrified of the Fire Nation. There is much that Zuko still loves about his nation, but there is also much he realised his nation has lost since the start of this war. He’s not sure winning this war is what is best for his nation, and he knows it is not best for the world. He’s decided he wants this war to end, but how, that’s a difficult question. He is not a traitor to his nation, at least not to its people, but he knows that they see him as such, and that will only continue if he goes down this path.

—-

“You must come visit us if you are ever near by,” Min spoke as she handed him a pack full of food and a few new outfits. Zuko nodded in response, a soft smile pulling at his lips, he thinks he might miss Min.

“I’ll miss you, Zuli,” Li Yan mutters as she pulls him into a hug. He carefully pats her on the back, not ever really comfortable with hugs.

“I’ll miss you too, Li Yan. Be good for Min, okay?” He speaks honestly, he would miss her, she was like a non-deranged little sister, instead of one that would see him dead without much thought. Reminded him of a time when he and Azula actually got along, it had been a long while since then.

“I have one more thing for you,” Min said, pulling out a mask, one that Zuko recognised as the Blue Spirit, from his mother’s favorite play. She handed it to him with a soft smile, “I saw it in the market and thought you may like it. I heard you talking about the play with Li Yan.”

“Thank you, Min–” Zuko accepted the mask with a slight bow, “--for everything.”

—-

Zuko had not expected that this is how his night would go. He’d found out that the Avatar was being held at the Pohuai Stronghold, a place that had recently come under the control of Zhao. He knew he couldn’t leave him there, not with *him*. He was already considered a traitor to his nation, there was no changing that, and his conscience would not let him leave a child in Zhao’s clutches. So that’s how he ended up with his swords at the Avatar’s throat as they backed out of the stronghold. He barely had time to see the arrow coming at him before the world went black.

Zuko could hear someone speaking as he woke, it took him a moment to recognise the voice, the Avatar. He sat up, wincing at the pain radiating from his head, he was lucky he wasn’t dead. Zuko silently thanked Min for the mask once again, if not for it he would’ve died tonight.

“Do– been— friends?” Aang was on the wrong side to be talking, and it most definitely didn’t help that Zuko’s head was still pounding.

“Bad hearing on that side, Avatar.” He spoke, really not ready to get up just yet, though he knew they needed to get moving. It wouldn’t be long until they were found by the Yuan Archers, or worse *Zhao*.

“Oh–” Aang was almost immediately on his other side, “--I expected you to be angrier. Also my name’s Aang.”

“I know.”

“Then why do you call me Avatar?”

“Same reason I call the Fire Lord, the Fire Lord. It’s what you are meant to do.”

“I’d rather you call me Aang.”

Zuko looked at the boy as he sat up, eyes narrowing, trying to figure out if it was some sort of joke. It was a sign of respect to call him by his title. He decided it was unlikely that this was some sort of joke or trick, maybe the Air Nomads hadn’t been ones for titles, he wasn’t really sure.

“We should both get out of here before Zhao and his men find us, I don’t think–” Aang tackled him to the ground as an arrow flew straight through where he’d been.

“I think they already have.”

“Get out of here. I’ll hold them off,” Zuko pulled on his mask, his swords at the ready, slashing through an arrow as it came towards him.

“I’m not just–”

“You said your friends are sick. You have to get back to them. They won’t hurt me once they know who I am, so just go!” Zuko knew that what he was saying was a lie, but the Ava- Aang is more important than he is. The world needed Aang, so Zuko needed to buy him time.

Frogs croaking as more started to thaw seemed to shake Aang from his stubbornness, the airbender was almost immediately gone from sight. Zuko only heard a quick apology before the boy left.

He managed to hold off the arrows as he made his way into tree cover, hoping he could lose them in the dense forest. Of course the Yuan Archers weren’t the only ones Zuko had to worry about. The arrows had stopped for the moment but Zuko didn’t fool himself into thinking he was safe, he could hear the sound of soldier’s boots on the ground nearby. He ran through the forest, trying to put as much distance between him and the strong hold. If he could just get to the cave he’d been hiding in he’d be fine.

He was nearing the cave and he couldn’t hear the soldier’s boots any longer, the arrows hadn’t followed him so he wondered if they’d went in search of Aang instead. When he got to the cave he was quick to pack his things, he couldn’t stay here, he knew they’d be searching for the Avatar in the area and that meant he could be found as well, even if they weren’t still searching for him. Quickly his bags were packed, his mask stuffed in the bottom of his backpack, covered in his clothes. He’d changed into a simple green set of clothing, hoping he could make it to the next town and blend in there til things cooled down.

He soon slipped out of the entrance of the cave, braving the cliffside to avoid those in the forest.

### End Notes

## Chapter 3

### Notes

#### Chapter Summary

“What are you doing?”  
“Sitting, you seemed lonely by yourself.”  
“Not really… I’m used to it.”

&#45;- or --

Zuko is shockingly good at making friends.

#### Chapter Notes

I promise Sokka will show up again... eventually, it'll be soon I promise lol.

As always, hope you enjoy to chapter.

### Text

It took him a day to reach the next town, one that after noticing a pretty heavy military presence he passed up on, it was a long while till he found a town without Earth Kingdom soldiers, which after his encounters with some civilians he wasn’t really looking forward to dealing with. Eventually he reached a small village without a military presence, though from the sight of torn red fabric he could tell that that was probably a new thing. The town had clearly been occupied until recently, and walls surrounding it bore markings of earthbending. Zuko had heard about a prison break a little while back, something to do with the Avatar, he’d chosen to ignore the rumors, but a recently taken back village told him it was more than a rumor. He should probably avoid this village too, but he was running out of supplies, he wasn’t sure if he’d make it to the next village before he ran out.

He did his best to cover some of his face with his growing out hair, though he wasn’t sure it would make much of a difference. He made his way into the valley that houses the village, the sight of the recently freed village disappearing behind its wall of earth. He could subtly hear what sounded like earthbending not far off from where he was, somewhere along the outside of the village wall. As he neared the village he saw a boy with long dark brown hair in an earth bending stance, seeming to be practicing in the quickly dimming light. He pulled out of his stance as his gaze landed on Zuko.

“And you are?” the boy asked, an appraising look, green eyes lingered on the swords at his hip before meeting his eyes, or at least tried to through the hair partially covering them. “Li,” Zuko managed not to mess up this time. “Just need to buy some supplies, and then I’ll be on my way.”

“You’re on your own?” the teen questioned, eyebrow raising.

Zuko just nodded, deciding that he didn’t want to go into splitting off from his uncle or coming up with some lie to tell, he’d never been all that good at lying, at least that’s what Azula always said. She was a master at lying though, so maybe he could lie, just not to her. He watched as the teen’s eyes softened, he must have decided the reason Zuko was alone was likely a tragic one. He was sure telling him it wasn’t would be a lie anyone would call.

“My name’s Haru. I’m about to head home for dinner, I’m sure my parents won’t mind one more,” the earthbender, who Zuko now knew was called Haru, offered as he started toward the entrance of the village.

“You really don’t have to–”

“If my parents knew I let some kid just buy food and leave into the night they’d ground me for life.”

He decided to follow Haru, taking him up on his offer for food, and hoping this wouldn’t turn out poorly. He wasn’t sure how much of him Haru really saw of him in the dark. This town was clearly just liberated from Fire Nation rule, he wasn’t sure his parents or the rest of the village would take kindly to his presence.

“Are you sure they’ll be okay with me joining you?” He questioned, not really wanting to walk into someone’s home if they weren’t going to be okay with him being there.

“Why? Because you’re part Fire Nation?” Haru questioned plainly, stopping just as Zuko did, almost as though he expected him to freeze a bit.

“You already noticed.”

“I grew up with Fire Nation soldiers all over town, it is kind of hard not to. But to answer your question: yes, they will be fine with you joining us for dinner. You’re probably younger than me, they wouldn’t turn away a kid just because they’re part Fire Nation.”

Zuko decided it was probably best not to mention that he isn’t just *part* Fire Nation.

“I’ve had some *trouble* in the past. That's why I stopped here, most of the towns around here are full of Earth Kingdom soldiers.”

“Yeah, I can see why they wouldn’t take too kindly to you.” With that comment they continued on to Haru’s home. It wasn’t long before they arrived at the home, clearly built almost entirely with earth bending.

“I’m home! And I brought a guest!” Haru called into the house as he walked in. It wasn’t long before a man appeared in the hallway, Haru’s father, Zuko assumed. Zuko found himself both wanting to sink into himself and stand tall as the older man looked him over, similar to Haru his gaze stayed on the swords at his hip.

“Who is your friend Haru?” the man questioned, his eyes moving to his son.

“Dad, this is Li. Li, this is my dad, Tyro.”

Zuko gave a slight bow in respect, careful not to do the sign of the flame out of habit

“It is nice to meet you Li. I hope Haru invited you for dinner because it is just about ready. Haru you better go wash up, your mother will kill both of us if you track dirt all over the house again. Come on Li, I’ll introduce you to my wife.”

Zuko looked to Haru for a moment, sort of hoping he’d ask for him to join him instead so he wouldn’t have to be alone with the two unknowns that were his parents. Haru gave him no such reprieve, only excusing himself to wash up before disappearing down a short hallway and into a room. Zuko followed Tyro further into the house, soon making it into a kitchen, dining room combination. A woman, who Zuko assumed was Haru’s mother, turned toward them, her eyes quickly going to Zuko, they focused on him for a moment before going to her husband.

“Haru’s guest?”

“Yes, this is Li. Li, this is my wife, Hien.”

“It is nice to meet you,” he again gave a slight bow.

“Aren’t you polite,” she smiled, “it is nice to meet you too Li. I hope you like curry.”

—-

Dinner seemed to go smoothly, mostly Haru and Tyro talking about how Haru’s earthbending practice was that day, the conversation seeming a lot more lighthearted than when his father asked about his progress with firebending. Zuko decided to offer to help clean up, deciding it was the least he could do to thank them for allowing him in their home. That’s how Zuko found himself at the basin full of water alongside Hien.

“You’ll be staying the night, yes?” the woman asked as she dried the dishes after Zuko washed.

“I wouldn’t want to impose anymore than I already have.”

“Nonsense, it's dark out. You can have breakfast with us in the morning, and then we’ll get you some more supplies before you head out.” Hien’s tone did not leave room for argument so Zuko simply nodded in agreement.

Zuko and Haru ended up staying awake in the older boy’s room most of the night. After Zuko had finished helping clean up, the two of them headed to Haru’s room.

“You’re so making me look bad, offering to clean up,” Haru half joked, lightly elbowing him in the side.

“It’s the polite thing to do as a guest,” Zuko reasoned, his father always looked down on it, but his mother had instilled in him her ways, and recently he’d been going back to that, rather than trying to act as his father wanted.

“Is that a colony thing?” Haru questioned.

“More of a ‘my mother’ thing. I think technically it’s a Fire Nation thing, but I wouldn’t really know.”

“Your mother is Fire Nation?” Haru questioned, seemingly surprised. He likely assumed that his mother would be from the Earth Kingdom.

“She was, I have not seen her in many years. My father either, though that has been less time,” Zuko explained, thought decided not to clarify his father’s lineage.

“Was?”

“I don’t actually know what happened to her, but it is more likely than not that she–” he wasn’t sure he could say it aloud.

“I see. Was she–” Haru seemed to debate his question for a moment, “--a soldier? I know that is more common in the Fire Nation.”

“Ah no, she didn’t support the war,” Zuko had learned that much, he’d had time in his lonely travels to go through his memories, to realise that his mother did not agree with his Nation’s war. This realisation had made the likelihood that she was dead much greater. Zuko had to come up with a lie for her to be in the colonies though, so he chose something similar to the truth. “She was an actress, a man high ranking in the Fire Nation wanted to marry her, wasn’t going to give her a choice, so she left.

“Is your father fighting in the war?”

“Yes,” Zuko confirmed, not technically lying, though he knew Haru would make the wrong assumption.

“I hope you get to see him again. I missed my father terribly when he was imprisoned.”

Zuko decided he couldn’t lie about this, it felt wrong to do so. Even if he didn’t tell the whole truth he couldn’t pretend to understand what Haru felt, because he hoped he would never have to see his father again. If he ever had to face him, Zuko wasn’t sure what he would do.

“I would rather not. He is not a *good* man. He is not like your father, Haru.”

Haru stared at him in shock for a long moment, Zuko could understand how it looked, deafening his Fire Nation mother, and having a clear hatred for his Father that Haru was assuming is from the Earth Kingdom.

“I’m sorry to have assumed.”

“No, I just didn’t feel right pretending to be in a similar situation. I’d heard rumors about the prison break a while ago, sounded like quite the feat.”

“We wouldn’t have been able to without Katara, Aang and Sokka,” Haru explained. Zuko gave him a raised brow, a silent question as to who these people are, even though he knew very well who they are.

“Oh, Aang is the Avatar. Katara and Sokka are traveling alongside him.

Haru regaled him with the tale of their prison break, and how they’d liberated their villages and were getting through repairing everything now. Zuko decided not to mention that Katara seemed to have a slight crush on Haru based on his story, but he decided it could also be the other way around considering he’s the one telling it.

—-

In the morning Zuko had breakfast with Haru and his parents, once again helping Hien clean. As he was packing to leave she brought him supplies to carry with him, enough food to hold him over for a while and a new blanket because she saw his while he was packing and decided he’d probably need another as winter continued on. It wasn’t long after that he was back on the road. Their goodbyes had been short, and he’d promised Haru to say hello for him if he came across the Avatar’s group, he didn’t want to tell him that he’d rather avoid them.

He traveled for a while, rarely stopping at villages. He stayed off busy paths as much as he could, mostly in an attempt to avoid running into any soldiers, whether Earth or Fire. It was starting to get late when he decided to move from the path he was on to find a place to set up camp. He made his way through the darkening forest, not expecting to find anyone, until he started to hear the hum of conversation coming from ahead. He couldn’t tell what they were saying, but it was clear there were quite a lot of people. This large of a group of people in the forest, it was probably soldiers. Zuko immediately changed directions only to come face to chest with a rather tall man. He instantly recognised the green army uniform, an earth kingdom soldier. He kept his eyes downcast, hoping to get out of this situation quickly.

“What are you doing out this far?” The soldier questioned, Zuko could feel his gaze but he kept himself from looking up.

“I’m traveling, sir.”

“Where to?”

“Ba Sing Se.”

“Trying to escape to war? Or just ditch military service?”

“Neither. I’m searching for my uncle, we got split up.”

Zuko decided on a half truth, he’d left his uncle behind, and when he’d suggested going to Ba Sing Se Zuko thought it was a terrible idea, but he needed some sort of cover.

“You seem perfectly capable of fighting, unless those broadswords are just for show. You shouldn’t be running off to Ba Sing Se like a little girl,” the man commented, well it was more of a taunt.

“I do not think you would want me in your company, sir.”

“So they are just for show then?”

“No,” his pride had him bite out the word.

“That was quite a bit of confidence for a draft dodger.” Zuko could hear the anger in his tone, in the way he shifted. He needed to get out of this situation, he knew even only seeing part of the man with his downcast gaze that he would snap if pushed, and that he was very likely an earthbender. The way he stood, feet heavily grounded like roots could grow over him at any moment, like the earth with part of him, the stance well trained earthbenders held when they knew a fight was likely.

“I can not be drafted, sir,” he tried to make his tone softer, knowing he’d pushed with his last word.

“You’re short, but you’re certainly not a girl, so why not?”

Zuko realised this was going nowhere, but he wasn’t sure how far he’d get if he ran considering his company was only a thin tree line away, he could see the glow of their fires from here.

“A few reasons,” Zuko raised his golden eyes to the man’s green ones. He watched as the man’s eyes narrowed, they always seemed to notice his eyes first, even before the scar. He could tell when the man noticed the scar, the way it made its way back toward his ear, which was covered by his hair now so that man couldn’t see its damaged state.

“Can you see out of that kid?”

“No, sir.” Zuko gritted his teeth at his lack of tact, asking such a thing.

“Which one’s an ashmaker?”

Zuko did his best to hide his grimace at the *word*.

“My father.”

“He do that?”

*Again* with the lack of tact. Zuko always had a hard time holding his tongue, and this guy seemingly was enjoying this. It was getting hard to keep his cool, he didn’t want to talk about his scar, or how he got it. But he wanted to get out of here, and to do so without possibly getting crushed, he had to get this guy to let him go. He wasn’t stupid, he was on his own, the soldier could have dozens backing him up in seconds. He’d fought earthbenders before, but that was always with an in mind, or with his own backup. Suddenly the ground he walked on felt dangerous, because it could just as easily become a weapon used against him. And he remembered the feeling of earth restraining him, the immovability of it, the seeming impossibility of escape.

“Yes.” He finally responded, internally cursing the shake in his voice.

“What’d you do?”

“Spoke against the war.” Half truths are usually the best option, the real story would give too much away.

The soldier seemed genuinely shocked for a second before he let out a laugh, it took him a long few moments for him to settle down. He brought his gaze back down to Zuko, now much calmer looking, his stance looser, and his eyes softening as if he finally saw a kid instead of a soldier.

“What’s your name kid?”

“Li.”

“Alright Li, need a place to stay for the night?”

“I’ll be fine on my own.”

“I’m sure you would, but we’re headed out in the morning, and I’d rather not have to move camp for just one night because I trusted some halfie kid.”

And that’s how Zuko ended up in an Earth Kingdom military camp for the night.

He wouldn’t call most of them nice, not that he really expected them to be. Luckily it seemed most of the soldiers just avoided him, some gave him glares or would mutter to themselves as he passed. He stayed out of the way the best he could, not wanting to inadvertently cause trouble. He’d been zoning out slightly when he noticed someone standing in front of him, his eyes slowly rose to look at a soldier probably a few years older than him. Zuko waited for him to say something, brows frowing.

“Li, right?”

Zuko just nodded, waiting for the guy to say whatever it was he came over to say. His eyes widened as the brown haired teen plopped down next to him.

“My name’s Sensu.”

He didn’t say anything for a while, trying to figure out what exactly was going on, why some Earth Kingdom soldier would have any interest talking with him. For a moment he remembers another, someone he’d ruined everything with. He forces himself away from the edge, finding brown eyes looking at him instead of green.

“What are you doing?” His tone is maybe harsher than it should be, but it’s quiet enough that no one looks their way.

“Sitting, you seemed lonely by yourself.”

“Not really… I’m used to it.” What was his point? They don’t know each other, he has no need to worry about the possible Fire Nation soldier in their camp. Okay arguably he poses no threat that they know of, as far as they know he’s not a bender, and he’s done nothing but avoid the men since being here. Again the question is, why is Sensu really talking to him? He’ll be gone in the morning, there’s no purpose for it.

Sensu was quiet for a moment, his eyes looking over to camp, watching a few of the soldiers glancing over, they might not have heard Zuko, but Sensu sitting with him wasn’t only odd to him. “Not a fan of us?” he asks, turning back to Zuko.

“Generally I avoid soldiers.”

“Does that extend to Fire Nation soldiers?”

“Yes.” He’d been avoiding any soldiers for months now, it wasn’t worth the risk of them figuring out who he is, or even just realising he’s the boy from the wanted posters that had started popping up.

“You don’t look Earth, why would they have a problem with you?”

“Not everything is about where you are from or what you look like.” If Sensu knew who he was that would sound rather insane coming from him, the Fire Nation Prince of all people saying such things. That was just it though, being a Prince, even a Prince of the nation that started this war, couldn’t save him. For all he supposedly was, here he was sitting next to an Earth Kingdom soldier, likely one wrong word from capture.

“What is that supposed to mean?” Sensu doesn’t sound angry, and Zuko has always been very attuned to anger, if anything he sounds confused and maybe a bit surprised.

“I’ve pissed off some powerful people, got myself branded a traitor.”

“By the Fire Nation? What’d you do?” He doesn’t make a big deal of the possible realisation. Zuko’s not sure if he thinks he’s from the colonies, or just managed to get pulled into this war on the *wrong side* but he only asks a simple question instead of freaking out. Zuko’s not sure he can answer it.

He just shrugs in response, he’d done a lot, though his most heinous treachery was as the Blue Spirit, not himself. He was pretty sure the reason he had posters up were mainly because of his run in with Zhao, he wasn’t sure his father even cared enough to call for his head just yet. He’d originally believed it to be his father who called for the posters to be put up, but if that was the case he’d be having a lot harder time avoiding the Fire Nation. Zhao on the other hand couldn’t out right say he was searching for *Prince* Zuko, it had to be discrete, making his avoiding them a bit easier.

“Powerful men often have fragile egos,” Sensu commented.

“I’d be careful who you say that around.”

“It won’t matter soon.” It was the resigned tone of someone who believed they were going to die, Zuko had only heard it a few times before, from a crew member when they’d gotten in a fight with an Earth battalion and his lower half had been crushed by a rock, and from his mother, the night she left.

“What do you mean?” The group seemed relatively safe in the middle of nowhere currently.

“There’s a struggle for Omashu, that’s where we are headed next. Ego’s do not matter when someone is shooting fire at you.”

“If Omashu is taken, Ba Sing Se will be the last major city left. Gaoling is too far south to be of help.”

“That is why we need to go before it is too late.”

Zuko just nodded. The Earth Kingdom had been losing ground for a while, but if the Fire Nation got Omashu under control, it wouldn’t be long before this war was over, maybe even before the comet.

Nothing much of interest happened overnight after his conversation with Sensu. The other soldiers continued to ignore him and he slipped away from camp pretty easily in the morning. He chose not to look for Sensu to say goodbye, it wasn’t worth it, but if nothing else he hoped he would be alright.

### End Notes

Quick note: just in case, Sensu is Lee's brother, from the "Zuko Alone" episode.

## Chapter 4

### Notes

#### Chapter Summary

Zuko learns new information for someone not entirely unlike him, but also absolutely nothing like him. But there is nothing to do with it due to his current status as a traitor. He once again finds help from an Earth Kingdom family, maybe his luck isn't so bad...

### Text

“Well this is unexpected,” a man’s voice shook Zuko from his half sleep. His eyes shot open looking at the man in front of him. “What are you doing sleeping in the forest, boy?”

“I’m traveling…” Zuko looked up at the man standing a few feet from him. He was older, and looked vaguely familiar, he was definitely Fire Nation, so maybe him being familiar isn’t a good thing. He quickly got to his feet, “What do you want?”

“Why didn't you stop in town?”

“I don’t make a habit of going into occupied towns.”

That man gave him a quizzical look, probably because he recognised Zuko as Fire Nation. It was then that he realised who the man was, he’d seen him on wanted posters both here and in the homeland, he was a deserter, the first to do so without ending up dead or captured soon after. Zuko knew that wasn’t entirely true, there were plenty of people who avoided service, vanished into the Earth Kingdom, but Jeong Jeong was the loudest about his leaving, so he got called the first. After figuring this out he felt a lot less stressed, knowing he wouldn’t turn him in.

“What are you doing so close to an occupied town Jeong Jeong?”

“My reputation precedes me, have you also escaped my boy?”

“You could say that.” Zuko wasn’t exactly enjoying the way Jeong Jeong avoided his questions, but he wouldn’t push. He’d heard of the man’s mastery over firebending, and also of his temper, he wasn’t looking for a fight.

“You sound Bulkan. Shocking accent to hear from a deserter.” Jeong Jeong did seem vaguely suspicious of him, and calling him out on his accent being that of someone who grew up in Hari Bulkan, the Fire Nation’s capital, only reaffirmed that.

“Traveling tends to change your view of the world, and my mother never supported the war, it took me much too long to understand why,” Zuko explained, trying to keep his answers vague but with enough truth to trick someone who was once feared by enemies and comrades alike.

“Less time than me it seems, that burn isn’t new. What else is to be expected, cursed fire, only made to destroy.”

“Fire is not a curse,” he argued, eyes narrowing.

“You say that despite the damage it has done to you?”

Zuko winced slightly at the lack of consideration or tact, what was with people throwing the worst moment of his life in his face over and over again? They acted like he wasn’t reminded every time he looked in the mirror.

“You sound like you fear your own element.” Zuko knew that fear, because for a long while he’d feared his own bending as well. After what had happened to him it was difficult to even but in the same room as fire let alone wield it. For several months he relied entirely on his swords, his fire only escaping him with his anger, his lack of control only worsening his fear. His uncle spent nearly a year getting him comfortable with his bending again, getting his control back.

“To fear fire is correct, child.”

“Fearing fire as a firebender only leads to more harm. You are more likely to lose control.”

“You sound like General Iroh.”

Zuko went silent, he knows his uncle? Did they serve together? Zuko didn’t always agree with his uncle, but he’d been the one to help him with his firebending after everything happened. For a while he could barely look at fire, he hated it, but over time he became more comfortable with it, and ended up more skilled in his bending than before. Zuko knew it was probably best to keep his cover.

“You know the Dragon of the West?”

“Yes. I have for a number of years, I met him after his son died. A shame what happened, he was too young, but they need the war to continue, and to have him in line, well–”

Golden eyes narrowed with silent question, what was Jeog Jeong insinuating, what would be wrong with Lu Ten becoming Fire Lord? Zuko had been close with Lu Ten when he was young, he had been someone he could look up to, even if his father was never a fan of his cousin. He’d been inspired to take up swordsmanship by his cousin, having watched him practice with Master Piandao when he was young, and starting his own training as soon as he could. Very few of Zuko’s beliefs have went unchanged in the past three years, but the fact that Lu Ten would’ve been a much more fitting Crown Prince was certainly one of the few.

“What is that supposed to mean?”

“The Fire Nation would risk quite a lot, having a Crown Prince whose lineage was not certain. While the Fire Nation has never required marriage to make a child legitimate, with the current state of the world, with this war, having a Prince with unknown loyalties was dangerous. Of course the current Crowned Prince is a known traitor so I suppose getting rid of Prince Lu Ten did not do much.”

“Do you believe Prince Lu Ten would've followed the same path?”

“Maybe with a little more tact, Prince Zuko.” Jeong Jeong commented, revealing his knowledge of who Zuko is.

“You aren’t really one to talk. And thirteen year-olds rarely have tact.”

The comment earned a light chuckle from Jeong Jeong. He nodded in agreement before finally motioning Zuko to follow him.

“You can stay the night, but I’ll be moving on early in the morning so I expect you to as well.”

—-

Zuko wasn’t sad to leave Jeong Jeong, while they both may be traitors they didn’t feel the same on much besides the need for the war to end. He had wished to ask him more questions, what exactly he believed happened to Lu Ten, but he was cagey around the subject at best, aggressive against answering at worst. In the end he only knew a few things, that Lu Ten’s mother was an unknown and that he was not killed in battle.

He was back on his travels early in the morning, searching for a non-occupied town to get supplies from. Zuko was forced to take a busier path than he usually did, as the one in the forest had been flooded by recent rain. He’d started to see people more often, though no one paid him much mind. As he walked the path he felt a presence behind him, he turned back for a moment to find a boy his age, maybe a little older, along with a woman that was likely his mother, who had another boy who couldn’t be older than four on her shoulders.

“Hey,” he greeted with a slightly strained smile, Zuko assumed he was in pain considering they’d probably been walking a while and the teen was using a walking stick to support himself. Zuko just gave him a nod before continuing on. They continued to walk in the same direction, time ticking on with Zuko just slightly ahead of the small family. Zuko’s gaze snapped back to the group as he heard the youngest begin crying.

“We should stop for a rest.”

“He’s hungry ma, not tired. If we stop it’ll be even longer before we get more food.”

“I know that, but we can’t just keep going, *you* can’t just keep going,” she urged, Zuko assumed she probably made a gesture toward the boy’s injury or walking stick as she did.

“I’m fine ma,” the teenage voice argued weakly. Zuko could hear the pain in his voice, but he obviously wanted to keep moving despite it.

Zuko found that he had stopped as they slowed. He had enough for all of them to share a meal, but it would be the last of his rations. He looked to the child wailing, making himself nearly fall from his mother’s shoulders as he cried.

“I was about to stop to eat, you can join me, if you want.”

The woman looked at him, her eyes soft as she looked him over, not seeming at all suspicious of him. “That is very kind but we could not take your food.”

“I’m offering.”

“I’m not gonna pass up food,” the teen spoke with a bright smile.

“Junpei! Please excuse my son,” the woman spoke quickly, giving her son a disapproving look.

“It is fine. Admittedly it is for a selfish reason, I’ll need to pass through a town ahead that is being used as a base for the Earth Kingdom military, I am not sure I will be granted passage on my own.”

Truthfully Zuko had planned to avoid the town, hoping he could make it to the next one without running out of supplies, but that seemed unlikely, especially since Jeong Jeong hadn’t exactly been helpful in that regard, instead giving him a pai sho tile, the white lotus, and a vague explanation about how it would help him find aid if he needed it. Usually one military filled town would lead to others surrounding it, especially this close to occupied territory, it would be a while before he finds another town. He needs to be able to get supplies in town, and that would be pretty difficult if he was turned away.

“I’ll assume you’ve been given some trouble before.”

“I generally avoid either military due to the assumptions that will be made, but yes I’ve had some issues in the past.” If there was something traveling the Earth Kingdom had taught him, it was *tact*, unlike some people, *Jeong Joeng*, and how to negotiate, sort of.

“Alright, as long as we aren’t just taking your food I’m fine with it.”

They found a small clearing just off the path to create a small fire, enough to cook the rice and vegetables Zuko had left. Sayuri, he’d learned the woman’s name was, had offered to take over the cooking, something he definitely didn’t mind as he wasn’t exactly a good cook, only recently needing to. Zuko sat with Junpei and his little brother, who was entertaining himself by drawing in the dirt with a stick he’d found.

“So Li, where are you headed?” Junpei questions, sitting next to him as they wait for the food to be done.

“My uncle and I were supposed to head to Ba Sing Se, but we got split up a while back.” He kept with a basic story, close enough to the truth that he wouldn’t find himself in too deep of a lie, but vague enough it would reveal anything too specific.

“So you’ll be going there then?”

“Not sure, honestly I think he’s better off without me.” Truthfully Zuko didn’t currently have much of a plan, he didn’t know where he was going, he just knew he couldn’t stick around in one place for too long. He needed to keep moving until he could manage to get lost in the Earth Kingdom, somewhere he was unlikely to be found. His uncle could still return home, honestly Zuko hoped he had, that he was safe in the Fire Nation. The man had done enough for him.

“I doubt he would think that.” It was a sweet thing to say, but Spirits was it far from the truth of his family.

“He’d be the first of my family not to. I’ll just bring him trouble.” Zuko didn’t miss Junpei’s shocked expression, honestly he was shocked at just how bluntly he’d said it, he was clearly in need of some decent rest.

“You’re being too hard on yourself Li. I mean I’m pretty much nothing but trouble for ma and she still cares about me.” And clearly Junpei was referring to his injury, one Zuko was unsure of its permanence, if it was like his own.

“Our families are very different.”

“I think you should find your uncle. He’s probably worried about you.” Junpei gave a soft smile, clearly trying to calm Zuko, but his deep brown eyes showed something too close to pity and it made Zuko’s stomach clench in annoyance.

—-

Soon enough they were back on the path, making their way to the village-base. It was a few hours before it came into view, the sky becoming increasingly dark with clouds. As they neared the village Zuko took note of the watchtowers along the wall. There were guards posted at the gate, Zuko had fallen back, walking beside Junpei as Sayuri took the lead with Junta on her shoulders. The guard came to attention as they neared. rising from their Pai Sho game.

“What is your business here?” One of the guards asked, his gaze mainly focused on Sayuri. Zuko mostly kept his features hidden under his scruffy hair, feeling the gaze of the other guard going over each of them.

“My sons and I are looking for a place to stay during the storm, and hopefully to buy supplies,” Sayuri responded calmly, not skipping a beat as the claimed Zuko as her son as well.

“Names please,” the guard talking to Sayuri requested as he took out a ledger, likely one to record people going in and out of town.

“My name is Sayuri. My oldest is Junpei, then Sanyu–” she gestured to Zuko as she said the name, understandably deciding not to use Li, if him not looking like her or his *siblings* wasn’t suspicious enough, having probably the most common name in the Earth Kingdom while the other two had what seemed like thought out ones would likely seem odd, “--lastly Junta.” He could feel someone’s eyes on him, an incredibly annoying feeling.

“Neither of your sons in the military?”

“No sir. Junpei was in an accident as a young boy and has had trouble walking since. Sanyu was burned when our village was attacked and lost sight on his left side.”

“And yet your son carries broadswords,” the other guard spoke.

“For self defense,” she assured.

“It is dangerous to carry a weapon you can’t properly wield,” the guard’s tone becoming more harsh. He seemed to shift toward Zuko, and he had to keep himself from reaching for his swords as to not seem ready for a fight. Again he recognised the stance of an earthbender, he didn’t want a fight, it wouldn’t be fair to those helping him. To his surprise, Sayuri shifted herself to be more in front of him.

“He hasn’t had to use them, just having them seems to keep people from bothering us much.” Just then thunder boomed across the sky, lightning cracking only a short moment later.

“As long as they are kept sheathed you are permitted to bring them in, if they are drawn there will be consequences,” the original guard spoke, his warning strict but not a threat.

As they were walking into town after being permitted entry he heard the two guards speaking to each other.

“Damned halfie.” The one who asked about Zuko’s swords grumbles.

“Leave him be. It’s not his fault his mother is a whore.” That other counters, a harsh contempt in his tone.

Zuko was quick to stop Junpei from doing something stupid, the teen went to struggle against his grip, attempting to pull his arm away until his mother spoke.

“Stop, Junpei.”

“But Ma–”

“It is not worth it. Don’t make Sanyu have to stop you from doing something rash.” Zuko looked at her with surprise, he expected her to be angry as well. “I expect he has heard much worse before.”

Zuko didn’t like the pitying looks he received. He didn’t really *allow* himself to be all that bothered by people throwing slurs and nasty remarks at him, most of the time they weren’t correct and even if they were he didn’t think he could say much against them. Zuko didn’t believe others deserved it, but he was the son of the man forcing this war to continue, if anyone should take on other’s ire he believed it should be him.

They managed to find an inn further into town. The place seemed pretty empty aside from a few soldiers sitting at the bar inside the inn’s dining area. They made their way up to the counter, a young woman sitting there silently looking over their party before speaking. “Welcome in, what can I do for you?”

Zuko tuned out most of the conversion, luckily playing the part of someone’s son meant he didn’t have to do much talking if he didn’t want to. He looked at the woman at the desk, at his second glance he noticed something unexpected. The girl looked Fire Nation, save for her eye color, green, but her features were definitely Fire. It was unexpected considering the guard’s reaction to him, maybe they hadn’t realised? He didn’t plan to mention it, not wanting to cause unnecessary trouble for her, maybe people really did just look over people if they didn’t have a more distinct feature of gold or amber eyes.

“Your room will be up the stairs and down the hall on the right.”

—-

They didn’t go out to get supplies that night, the heavy rain keeping them inside the rest of the day. Instead they did their shopping the next morning, getting supplies before they quickly headed back to the inn to pack. They wanted to leave quickly, deciding it was best not to tempt too much time in town, Zuko was glad for it considering he didn’t want to be recognised from his wanted posters. While his posters may be Fire Nation it didn’t mean someone from the Earth Kingdom wouldn’t try to turn him in, the reward was enough of a reason. Beyond the reward he had to worry about the Earth Kingdom military believing he’d have crucial information, which wouldn’t be entirely untrue.

They managed to leave town without an issue, the guards on their exit much friendlier. They traveled for a long while, Zuko carrying Junta on his shoulders to allow Sayuri a break from having to carry the boy.

“Did you hear that?” Junpei spoke up, from Sayuri’s attention being pulled to the left Zuko assumed she heard something too. Zuko handed Junta to Sayuri so he’d be able to fight if necessary. He finally heard what the others had as the footsteps neared, he recognised the sound, metal boots on hard earth.

“You need to hide,” Zuko whispered, rushing the trio into the trees, away from the sound of footsteps, “Just stay moving in the treeline.”

“Li, what are you doing?” Sayuri questioned in a tone that reminded him of when he would be caught doing something he wasn’t supposed to by his mother.

“I’m just going to make sure they aren’t following. I’ll catch up,” Zuko was pretty sure that statement was a lie but he said it as though it was a promise.

“We should go ma, I trust Li,” Junpei backed him up, for whatever reason having faith in his words. Zuko could feel the guilt rising up at his comment but he didn’t say anything. The trio began moving a moment later, no one saying goodbye, likely because they only thought it would be a few minutes, Zuko knew better mouthing *goodbye* as they walked away into the trees.

“She said he was with three others, a woman and her sons.” A woman’s voice spoke, sounding like they were on the path now.

“Are you sure she knows what she’s talking about?” Another spoke, his voice exasperated.

“She swears it was him.”

“Fine. We’ll search the area, but if your stupid half-breed girlfriend is wrong–”

“Would you shut it already? She’s smarter than *you*.”

“Whatever. I just want to find him, Admiral Zhao’s getting unbearable.”

“Seriously, he’s probably more obsessed with finding Prince Zuko than he is with the Avatar…” she groaned in annoyance.

Zuko had to keep them from finding his three traveling companions, they might need him alive, but those three? They weren’t so *lucky.* He readied himself to run, he wasn’t looking to get caught, just to distract them for long enough that the others could get away. He *accidentally* stepped on a branch to snap it, knowing the sudden sound in the otherwise silent forest would get their attention. The moment he did so he ran, knowing they would be right on his tail. Sayuri, Junpei, and Junta had continued parallel to the path, so he was running as far from it as he could. He allowed his steps to be heavy, loud, leading them away. There were heavy metal clad footstep behind him, they were never far behind but they couldn’t catch up to him just yet. He knew he could take down the two soldiers, he was lucky only the two had come to check out their lead, it made his escape easier. He saw a small clearing in the trees, he’d stop there, make his stand. He entered the clearing, turning to where he’d come from, drawing his swords just as the two soldiers broke through the treeline.

“The *traitor prince*,” the man sneered, not waiting a moment to shoot fire at him. Zuko easily broke through it, sending fire between the two to split them from each other. He quickly ran toward the man, swords at the ready, he easily dispersed the fire shot at him, closing it on the man. He dunked under a stream of fire shot through the wall that separated the two soldiers, as he dunked down he swept a flame covered blade at the man’s ankles throwing him off balance. The wall split just as he brought the hilt of one of his swords down into the crook of the man’s neck causing him to crumple, knocked out. Zuko turned his attention to the woman, “the woman from the inn?” he questioned, confirming who it was that she had been speaking about. He didn’t get a response, the soldier shooting rapid fire at him, daggers of flames one after the other coming at him as he tried to close in.

“I’m not what Admiral Zhao or the Fire Lord claim I am. This war is hurting the Fire Nation, hurting its people, we’re destroying ourselves along with the rest of the world,” Zuko spoke as he tried to find an opening while fending off the fire daggers. He again didn’t get a response beyond further attacks. He sent himself over the flames, feeling fire lick at his heels for a moment as he neared her. He held a flaming sword at her throat as he landed. “Stand down. I don’t want to hurt my own people.”

“We’re no longer your people.”

He felt flames hit his torso, nearly doubling over in pain he managed to steel himself long enough to use the hilt of the sword not to her throat to knock the soldier out. He stumbled back as she fell, his tunic burned, his torso searing and red. He forced himself to move, quickly scooping up his bag from where he had dropped it early and making his way back into the trees, not paying much mind other than getting away before either of them woke up.

Zuko wasn’t sure how long he’d walked for but eventually he happened upon a river. He quickly pulled off the remains of his shirt before beginning to clean his wound. He rummaged through his bag looking for bandages and a new shirt. He was quick to wrap his torso and throw on his shirt, not wanting to waste time, he had to get further away.

He didn’t get far, running into a familiar face.

“Li?”

“Sensu?”
