## Chapter 5

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

hey, so I very much just spaced on posting last week. I'm editing the next chapter now and hopefully I'll post that later today or tomorrow to kinda make up for the lack of chapters last week.

### Text

“What are you doing here? I figured you’d be nearly to Ba Sing Se by now.”

“Ran into some trouble,” Zuko admitted.

“What kind of trouble?”

“Well, I’m pretty sure my stomach and eye will have matching scars,” he grumbled out, teeth clenched in pain, now that he stopped walking he was thinking about it, focusing on it.

“Sensu! What’s taking so long?” A man’s voice called through the trees as footsteps neared. His eyes went directly to Zuko as he neared them.

Zuko’s eyes widened as he noticed the man’s amber eyes, his hand going to his swords on instinct. He could feel pain shoot through his body as he tensed. He didn’t draw his swords as his vision was growing blurry, he hadn’t thought the burn was this bad. He tried to blink away the dark, the fact that the two started talking lessened his concerns, but the lack of tension made his body tired.

“Who’s he?”

“This is Li, we’ve met before, a bit north, before we joined up with your company.”

“They as cheery with him as they are with us?”

“Pretty much.”

Zuko didn’t catch anymore of the conversation as the world went dark.

—-

He couldn’t get his eyes to open as he woke, too heavy, instead his eyelids forced him to stay in darkness as he listened to what was going on around him. He could feel the cot below him, his swords weren’t at his hip anymore, neither of his bags on him either. Where were his things? Where was Sensu or that other man?

“When’s he gonna wake up?” That was Sensu’s voice, a sort of relief rushed through him with that fact.

“Soon, he likely passed out from the pain, though the malnutrition probably doesn’t help. You say you know this boy?” He didn’t recognise this voice, but they sounded older.

“Sort of, we ran into him up north, Aigou didn’t trust that he wouldn’t disclose our position so he stayed a night at our camp, left without saying… anything,” Sensu’s voice explained, sounding bitter? sad maybe? at Zuko’s lack of saying anything before he left the last time. Zuko couldn’t figure out the exact emotion behind it.

“Of course he didn’t, he barely trusts me,” the older man didn’t sound like he was the biggest fan of Aigou.

Finally Zuko managed to force his eyes open, immediately seeing two people staring at him. He pressed his lips together, tilting his head slightly, eyes narrowing as he looked at the man next to Sensu. He was older, probably around uncle’s age, and definitely part Fire.

“Glad to see you’re awake, Li. My name’s Yaozu. You seem to have gotten yourself quite the burn,” He seemed to make a point to gesture to Zuko bandaged torso. “You did not clean it very well.”

“I was in a rush. I’m not exactly on the Fire Nation’s good side.”

“Most who are not entirely Fire seem to not be.”

Something about the way he said it felt leading, like the old man knew more than what he was saying, but Zuko doubted he knew exactly who he was considering he hadn’t been turned over to whoever was in charge. Zuko looked around the tent for a brief moment, it was only the three of them. It was a risk but keeping up big lies was never his strong suit, and Sensu had probably risked quite a lot bringing him back here, he knows what would happen if a Fire Nation soldier brought some Earth Kingdom kid to their camp because he was hurt, even if they were part Fire, both would be lucky if they left alive.

“That is not the issue.”

He watched as realisation came to Sensu, his eyes widening slightly, mouth agape. Yaozu did not seem nearly as surprised, or really at all, just as Zuko had assumed, he was expecting the response.

“I would assume you’re the one on the posters?”

Zuko nods, he doesn’t focus on Yaozu though, instead looking at Sensu, urging him not to go running, “the Fire Nation considers me a traitor, though most of the posters are probably because I angered Admiral Zhao.”

“How does a boy anger an Admiral?”

“He’s got anger issues,” he commented vaguely, trying to stall till he could decide what exactly he should tell them.

“Surely that’s not the reason. Did you serve under him, make him look bad by deserting?” Yaozu didn’t seem like he was judging if that was the case, but Sensu looked like he might throw up, probably because helping some random Fire Nation teenager was a lot different than a Fire Nation soldier, even if they had deserted.

Zuko nearly choked at the idea, shuttering slightly.

“I’d sooner jump into a volcano than serve under him. I didn’t desert. I never served in the military, not formally at least, I left the Fire Nation when I was thirteen.”

“Not formally?”

“Someone under fourteen can not legally serve in the military,” Zuko answered, effectively putting an end to that line of questioning for the moment. Yaozu relented, while Sensu was looking increasingly like he might be ill.

“Back to the Admiral, what did you do to anger him?” Yaozu seemed to understand he wasn’t getting more of an answer on the military question.

“Several things, but I’m pretty sure beating him in an Agni Kai in front of his crew pushed him over the edge.”

“An Agni Kai? That is a fire duel, yes?”

“You’re a firebender?” Sensu questioned, his words almost a whisper. He looked like he was facing the gallows, which arguably if any of this left this tent he very well might be.

“Not a very good one, but yes.” Zuko couldn’t help but think of all the times he’d been told that, how weak of a firebender he is. How Agni had cursed him, being born on the Winter Solstice. How for a while it was believed he wouldn’t even be a bender. And how his lack of bending power was shameful, considering who he was.

“You beat an Admiral,” Sensu argued, his voice a more normal volume.

“I’m better with my swords, and he was a commander at the time.”

“That’s uncommon; for a firebender to use a weapon,” Yaozu commented.

“It is seen as weak, not being able to win by fire alone,” Zuko furthered the explanation, mostly for Sensu’s benefit, it seemed Yaozu already knew.

The room fell silent for a long while, Sensu seemed to be very deep in thought, while Yaozu seemed to want to say something but kept stopping himself. He looked at Sensu, his lips pressed together in thought, he soon turned to Zuko again.

“Your name is not on the wanted posters.”

“Naming me would dishonor my family. Having a traitor as family is nearly as bad as being one yourself,” Zuko knew that was the only reason someone’s name wouldn’t be on the poster in the past, his father actually preferred to shame the families of deserters, unless it was his own. Zuko’s name was left off for two reasons, as Prince of the Fire Nation he knew many state secrets, even after being banished he still kept knowledgeable of much, and he knew the weaknesses of his nation better than anyone, putting on the posters that he’s the Prince could lead to the Earth Kingdom capturing him for information. Second was more of a personal reason to the person who probably wanted him captured more than the Fire Lord, Admiral Zhao wants the glory of capturing the traitor Prince himself. Many, even in the Fire Nation, no longer know what the prince looks like, the last official portrait being released on his thirteenth birthday, before he’d been scared and banished, a random traitor is a less enticing capture than the traitor Prince, the lack of name gives Zhao a better chance of being the one to catch him. Or he’s right that his father doesn’t care about his capture and the posters are entirely from Zhao, obsessed of his own accord.

“I assume your family is a noble one?”

“In title, sure.” Zuko commented bitterly, not bothering to deny it, of course that was sort of a lie, but it was as close as he could say to the truth.

“You’re a Fire Nation Noble?” Sensu questioned, mouth once again agape in shock. “But you aren’t– I don’t know, evil..?” The boy looked pretty bewildered by the whole situation, but at least he wasn’t running off to turn him in.

“Sensu–” Yaozu started in a sort of warning tone.

“It’s fine. I understand the surprise. From personal experience, most are just that.”

Zuko had once believed those who served under his father were noble, he used to believe his father, while harsh, was the absolute, he was to be respected, revered. Even after his father burned him, banished him, he still wanted his approval, wanted to do what he asked. It was only after that day at the South Pole that it really hit him, just how young he’d been when his father had decided he was a lost cause, had chosen to burn him and send him away before he’d even had time to heal. It took the realisation that he’d been a child, and that while it may have been the first time his father had permanently scared him, it wasn't the only time he’d been left with wounds after disappointing him. On top of all that, how many of his own people had watched what his father did to him? How many of them cheered it, smiled as a child was burned, scared, and disabled in front of them. He’d done grave, permanent damage and all of them just watched. Zuko knew them standing up for him would mean more than the mutilation he faced, he at least had the protection of being the Crown Prince, but he wished anyone had tried. Instead he remembers seeing many grin with sickening glee as his father’s fiery hand came toward him while he cried, only his uncle had the decency to look away.

He was a child. The Av– Aang is a child. He was ashamed he hadn’t left the moment he’d learned that fact.

“You don’t have to say that, it’s still your home,” Yaozu tried to assure.

“It *is* my home, but the people currently in control, the Fire Lord and all those who serve closely under him need to be stopped.” Zuko watched the slightly bit of shock come over both of them, “what? Did you think the posters are lying when they call me a traitor? If this war is to end they need to be taken down.”

“Heard we picked up a Fire brat lurking around our camp,” the man spoke, green eyes moving to Zuko, “what were you up to?” his tone was pretty jovial, but there was something off about it, dangerous.

“Running from Fire Nation soldiers,” Zuko retorted.

“That so?” He looked to Sensu for confirmation.

“I found Li by the river, they burned him pretty bad, gnarly looking, I’m shocked he stayed up as long as he did.”

“It is not the first time I’ve been burned…” he commented, Zuko was honestly kind of disappointed he’d passed out at all, it really wasn’t *that* bad.

“Wait, is this the same kid? When we were a bit north, weren’t you going to Ba Sing Sa?”

“That was the plan…”

A head popped into the tent, “General Fong is looking for you three. The ships are nearing and he wants everyone there to greet them.”

“What are we supposed to do about…” The soldier gestured back to Zuko.

“Bring him I guess? He didn’t say.” The man was gone after that.

“Don’t let him out of your sight,” the tall soldier directed Sensu, before leaving as well.

“I guess that means I’m getting up,” Zuko groaned as he sat up on the cot.

—-

Zuko stood at the back of the large group gathered near the small docks at the river, he couldn’t see well enough over the crowd to see who it was that was coming. Sensu on the other hand was taller than Zuko and had an easier time looking over the crowd.

“Who exactly are we waiting for?”

“Reenforments.”

Zuko watched as Sensu’s expression turned to surprise, apparently finally getting to see who was there. Zuko could vaguely hear the creaking of boats, the sound of footsteps on wooden docks, finally he caught a glimpse of blue through the crowd. Water Tribe? He understood Sensu’s surprise, the battle for Omashu would be a land battle, something that the Water Tribes often avoided. It was recently that the Southern Water Tribe had rejoined the war, but they more often took down ships than fought on land.

Zuko could hear muttering among the soldiers, much of which didn’t sound particularly kind. He’d been told a lot about the Water Tribes, until recently he believed much of it, even after being at a Southern Village, he’d not gotten rid of much of his beliefs. It was only recently that he wouldn’t blindly agree with those who seemed to harbor distrust or disdain for their reinforcements.

“Chief Hakoda I presume,” someone’s voice echoed over the crowd.

—-

Zuko and Sensu were quick to go back to the medical tent when people started to disperse, mostly in an attempt to avoid more confrontation with others. Yaozu let them know that he’d be back later, having a few people to check on and inventory to do for them medical supplies. They were left to their own devices, only directed that it was probably best to stay in the tent since it was unlikely anyone would come to bother them.

“So you’re really from the Fire Nation then?” Sensu questioned.

Zuko nodded, not particularly surprised that the older teen was still questioning that fact. It had to be shocking, even for Sensu who seemed pretty passive when it came to those who were both Earth and Fire, to meet someone from the Fire Nation who didn’t immediately attack him.

“You told Aigou your–” he paused, as if trying to decide whether this question was a good idea. Zuko generally did not think so, but Sensu, well he was different somehow. He didn’t bother to wait for the rest of the question, knowing he was going to answer anyway.

“Yeah, he did this” —he gestures to the left side of his face— “he claimed it was a lesson in respect. I left the Fire Nation soon after,” Zuko decided not to mention he was in fact banished from the Fire Nation. “To be clear, that is not a *normal* punishment. *Noble* families, *my* family functions differently.”

“*Functions differently,”* the brown haired boy repeats his words, but with a bitter tone. “It wasn’t the only time,” he says definitively, “he’d burned you before.”

“Nothing permanent, not until that day, not from him.”

“You said you’re a noble, wouldn’t someone– I don’t know– get their hand chopped off from that or something?” Sensu asked, in a tone that seemed like he wasn’t entirely processing this conversation.

Zuko almost wanted to laugh, because he wasn’t *just* some noble, because Sensu was right, you would think scaring the Crown Prince, hell even just trying to, would be grounds for at the least some punishment. That wasn’t the truth though, that wasn’t Zuko’s experience.

“My father didn’t care as long as I could still appear at functions.” He rolled his sleeves, faded scars peppering his skin, most old enough that they nearly bended in with his skin. “Mostly they’re from my tutors or from training accidents. Fire is admittedly a dangerous element to learn. Though this” —he pulls the collar of his shirt to show his shoulder, a faded gash across his collar— “is actually from sword training.” Zuko smiled, trying to lighten it up a bit, as he actually liked his dao blades.

“Spirits, Li.” Sensu wasn’t looking at his scared shoulder or arms though, he looked at Zuko, so intently that Zuko found that he couldn’t pull himself away from the boy’s warm brown eyes. The moment hit him like a pile of bricks, and he hated that he even realised it but Angi damnit, he was leaving soon. *Spirits he’s cute,* Zuko couldn’t help but think to himself. *Back* *it up Zuko, you are not falling for another one.*

Their conversation turned lighter after that, Sensu steering it that way and Zuko allowing it because despite the fact that some indiscernible force made Zuko want to tell the other boy everything, he knew he couldn’t. So instead they talked about their travels. Zuko got to talk about Li Yan and Min, who he actually rather missed he realised as he spoke about them. Sensu talked about his little brother, who happened to share a name with *Li*, though spelt differently.

Hours passed and the sun had nearly set before Yaozu had come back to tell them to get some food.

Sensu dragged Zuko from the medical tent to get food with him, though it was more likely because he wasn’t allowed to be left alone than for any other reason. No one had clarified and Zuko wasn’t sure, but it seemed like he wasn’t supposed to leave, and Zuko wasn’t looking for another nation’s military to be after him. The two got in line for food behind two Water Tribesmen, the two of them glancing back at the new presence behind them. Sensu’s face seemed more surprised than necessary considering they already knew the Water Tribe was around, but then Zuko took a closer look at the men in front of him, they looked slightly older but he recognised them from his lessons on current world leaders, something his mother had pushed for. The shorter of the two was Chief Hakoda, the other their fleet’s second in command, Bato.

He noticed Hakoda’s gaze on him, preparing himself for some sort of comment. He really was hoping to avoid the man, partially out of a guilt he wasn’t ready to address, but secondly because he wasn’t sure how to answer people’s questions about who he is. He watched Hakoda’s expression go from a sort of confusion to something Zuko couldn’t exactly discern.

“How old are you, son?” The tall man questioned.

It was now Zuko’s turn to be confused, because that was certainly not the question he expected. The man next to him, Bato, didn’t look at all surprised by the choice of question, seeming to share the same expression as Hakoda.

“I’m sixteen, sir.” For a moment he was worried he was supposed to address the man differently, he wasn’t sure what was correct, he’d only ever dealt with one other nation’s leader, and his father would’ve expected kneeling, Zuko at least knew that wasn’t necessary.

“I thought you were fifteen?” Sensu commented, Zuko’s not entirely sure where the other had gotten that idea, they’d met after the Winter Solstice, though with everything that had been going on maybe he’d forgotten at the time and had told the other he was still fifteen.

“My birthday was on the Solstice.”

Sensu looked like he wanted to say something, Zuku tried not to think it might be a comment about the irony, he would probably think that, but it wasn’t only ironic, it was shameful, because he’s the Fire Nation’s Prince and born on the Winter Solstice. Sensu wouldn’t know of the rumors that the reason his firebending was not as strong as the rest of his family was because of his birthday.

“He’s barely older than Sokka,” Bato gasped out. He regarded Zuko with a sort of nervousness? No. With concern, a look too close to pity for Zuko’s preference.

“Sokka is my son, he’s fifteen. He was too young to join us when we left to fight,” Hakoda explained, he sounded calmer but Zuko could see in the way his brows knitted togethers and the faint wrinkles on his face deppened that he felt the same on the matter as his fellow tribesman.

“In the Earth Kingdom you can join the military at fourteen, though many wait to do their years of service until a bit later. I did, I only joined on a few months ago.” Sensu explained. Zuko looked at the Earth teen, a narrowed gaze at him, did he really think that two leaders of another nation wouldn’t know something like that? After seeing Sensu glance at him Zuko realised the explanation wasn’t meant for them.

“Yes, I’ve heard a bit about the Earth Kingdoms rules of military service,” Hokoda commented, his gaze on Zuko gave him an idea of which rule he was thinking of. Because of the colonies and the nearly one hundred years of both Earth and Fire people being in the Earth Kingdom, both nations have rules on who can serve in the military. No one born in the colonies or who is *too much* Fire, may serve in the Earth Kingdom military. What *too much* meant, Zuko wasn’t sure. For the Fire Nation military, anyone born in the colonies is required to serve for at least a year as a proof of loyalty, though no one born with Earth Bending in the colonies may serve combat wise. Zuko knew a bit more detail on the rules than that, but that was a general point, and with the look Hakoda was giving him, he’d noticed just how Fire Zuko is. Sensu seemed to notice the look too, but before either could say anything Zuko heard a voice he recognised from before.

“What’s he still doing in my camp?” General Fong spoke, eyes focused on Zuko.

“He’s hurt, General.”

“What’s your point? It isn’t my problem that those ashmakers attack their own. I told you he could be patched up then I wanted him gone, he’s up, meaning he can leave.”

Sensu went to speak again but Zuko stepped in front of him, “I was only sticking around because I didn’t know I *could* leave.” He could feel a slight venom to his tone, something about General Fong set him off.

“Watch your tone,” the man warned, stepping forward slightly.

Zuko could hear what he thought was Hakoda speaking, but he was on his left side now and wasn’t talking loud enough for him to fully hear. “--not– just– kid– hurt–”

“For all we know he could be a spy,” Fong retorted loudly to whatever it was Hakoda had said. Hakoda stepped into Zuko’s view, hand up slightly to keep him from saying something. He wasn’t exactly a fan of being told to be quiet, but he wasn’t stupid enough not to understand his situation, so he held his tongue.

“I very much doubt the boy is a spy. And if he was truly attacked should we not learn why?” Hakoda reasoned.

Admittedly Zuko wasn’t a big fan of the idea of them finding out the real reason they were after him, but he didn’t mind giving a half truth if it meant getting out of here sooner. Zuko noticed Yaozu standing off to the side, he walked up to the question.

“I believe I can answer that,” he spoke as he unrolled a wanted poster showing Zuko as he was last time he’d encountered Zhao. His hair hadn’t been cut yet, at least not the phoenix tail, and he looked slightly younger though it hadn’t been all that long. The poster was probably based on his last royal portrait which would make sense why he looked younger. He noticed two changes to the poster though, the offer had gone up, and it directed to bring him to Zhao specifically. He couldn’t remember doing anything that would’ve caused that, there wouldn’t be new posters from yesterday. Was Zhao getting desperate? Why, though? Zuko doubted his father cared much about his capture, so why would Zhao?

Bato whistled a bit, “damn, how’d you manage that, kid?” Both of the Water Tribesmen looked somewhat amused, maybe even impressed by his wanted poster, meanwhile Fong looked increasingly like he might lose it.

“Nothing that warrants that reward, pretty sure Zhao’s just obsessed at this point…” He’d suspected his posters had always been coming from Zhao, but this proved it.

There were two very different reactions to that, Bato and Hakoda suddenly looked like they might be sick, but Zuko would never miss the anger that seemed to be under the expression, he’d always had to be good at detecting anger. Fong on the other hand was glaring at him, and unlike the other two men, looked angry at Zuko specifically, more than angry, he looked ready to attack.

“Want to explain why a Fire Nation Commander would even know who you are?”

“I don’t think you need to know that since I’m leaving. If he’s getting that desperate you don’t want me around,” Zuko decided it was best not to correct that Zhao is an admiral not a commander.

“I’m not letting a kid go off on their own with the Fire Nation after them,” Hakoda spoke up.

Zuko was quiet for a moment, he focused on Hakoda, thinking back to his lesson he remembered something, the Southern Watertribe Chieftain has two children, the son he’d mentioned before and a daughter. Aang had been rescued by two kids from that Southern Watertribe village. Haru had mentioned a girl called Katara, being with Sokka and Aang, likely Hakoda’s daughter. He quickly made a possibly stupid decision, not that he was ever known for making good decisions.

“Maybe don’t waste your concern on me, frankly I would suggest being more worried that there are similar posters for your children, considering they are traveling with the Avatar,” Zuko tried his best to make it not sound like a threat, but even then it certainly didn’t sound good.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

It took more arguing than he’d like, and a vague explanation as to how he knew Hakoda’s children were traveling with the Avatar, but he was finally leaving the Earth Kingdom camp. Of course General Fong had insisted he couldn’t know where the camp was, apparently still not trustworthy enough, so Zuko had been led away blindfolded on an ostrich-horse. Finally though he was back on his way, his swords at his hip and both his bags. There was one thing Bato had mentioned in all the arguing it took to get them to let him leave, Aang and his two companions were headed to the Northern Water Tribe, that info had brought upon a memory. Zhao was insufferable in many ways, but his obsession with destroying the moon was one Zuko could remember him mentioning even when he was a kid. There were rumors about how connected the North was with the spirit world, if Zhao thought there was a way to use that he would. A way to get rid of waterbending, that was Zhao’s plan, even Zuko’s grandfather had told him it was a stupid plan, that there wasn’t a way to destroy the moon, Zuko still remembered overhearing the arguing from the war council as a kid. Everyone was in a bad mood afterwards, he’d hidden away with his mother to avoid everyone that day, even Azula had joined them in their mother’s room so as to not deal with anyone’s anger.

Zuko shook off the memories of that day, of his mother. It was decided, he had to find a way to get to the North Pole, and there was really only one guaranteed way. He could practically hear his uncle scolding him for not thinking things through, but it was the easiest way to get to the north quickly. Zuko set his travels north.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“Fire or Earth?” The woman questions, looking between him and his map that he’d been staring at for ages.

“Does it have to be or?” he responded, not meaning for it to be a joke but he got a laugh from both her and the two identically looking girls that had peaked their head out of the back of their cart.

“Good answer. Where are you headed, kid?” She’s still sitting in the front of the cart, keeping her advantage of height. She’s not foolish, she doesn’t entirely trust him.

“As far north as I can go.” He needed to get closer to the north western ports controlled by the Fire Nation, they’d be the most likely to have a ship headed from the North Pole.

“I can get you to Poyang, that’s where I stop.”

Zuko referenced the map, finding Poyang lake well past the Great Divide. It was an easy answer. He rolled up his map, slipping it into his bag as the woman gestured for him to climb up next to her. The moment he sat down apparently was the two girls’ cue that they could start talking to him.

“Hi I’m Himari–” “Akahana.” They introduce themselves. Himari was much louder, leaning against the front of the wagon where he and who he assumed was their mother sat. Akahana wasn’t as far out, turned toward the conversation, but quieter and kept her distance a bit.

“Zu*li,”* he tripped up, having not realised how immediately he let his guard down with them. It had been a while since he’d seen amber eyes that weren’t those of attackers, but instead people that immediately saw him as kin.

“Rare name, both for a boy and for someone this far into the Earth Kingdom,” their mother comments. “I’m Nara.”

“All flower names,” Zuko notes, trying to get the topic off of his fake name.

“It’s a family tradition,” Himari says, with what he can only call a proud smile.

“That’s a nice tradition. Is your family from Honoka?” And he knows mentioning a Fire Nation town is risky, but he’s rather certain he’s right, he recognises the mother’s accent as similar to that of the Hanabusa family, the nobility of Honoka.

“Not the colonies then,” she laughs slightly, “ditch your company after seeing what it’s like out here?” And it seemed they were both willing to risk a lot, because what the comment was implying was enough to get her killed.

Zuko chooses to lie, at least somewhat, knowing banishment was rare enough that a Fire Nation citizen would be suspicious. The Fire Nation is not known for their mercy, and even within the Nation people know execution is much more likely than banishment unless you are nobility. He’s not looking to lose his ride by scaring her with the possibility of who his family might be. “My mother was against the war, grew up with her and my father disagreeing on that. I was shipped out early, Navy under Commander Zhao.” He watched her face scrunch in disgust, apparently his reputation known even to deserters. “I left a few months ago, and have been traveling the Earth Kingdom since.”

“Wait…” Akahana gasps, suddenly disappearing into the cart, only to appear again a few moments later. She holds a parchment up, showing her sister who gasps too.

“What are you two doing?” Nara questions, not looking back, but definitely doing the mom voice to get their attention.

“He’s the boy from the posters,” Akahana explains. Zuko catches sight of the poster, it’s one of the older ones where he looks more similar to his royal portrait than how he looks now, but he supposes his scar isn’t exactly common.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

It was nearly a week later that he got his chance, that he had a way to get far enough north in a short enough time, he’s lucky considering the time he’s wasted, though he had trouble really considering it a waste.

“A friend of mine is heading north tomorrow, I know it is late notice, but if you still want to go it might be your only chance for a while.” Nara relays the information to him before she leaves for her work at the Beifong Family’s vacation home that morning.

Zuko knows Nara doesn’t want him to go, she’s made it clear that she thinks it’s best he leaves the fighting to others. He can’t explain everything he knows though, what he plans to do, it wouldn’t do anything but make her more worried. He knows he has to do this though, he has to warn them about Zhao, about what he’s planning. He can’t risk waiting any longer.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

The cart took him far, much quicker than he could walk, even with the week break, he got farther that day than he could have with his injury in the week. He once again knew he’d have to find a way to repay Nara for everything she’d done for him.

It wasn’t long before he came across the town he was looking for, a small Fire Nation colony that served more as a restocking post for the military than an actual town, the part that would serve his purpose was the docks he needed to get on a navy ship, hopefully one heading north.

He waited till the dead of night, frankly getting into town was a joke, dressed in black he mostly blended in with the night. Once in town he wasn’t far from the docks, this would be the hard part, if he wasn’t lucky he’d end up having to check each ship. He was checking for any preparation meaning they’d be heading north, the easiest place to check was the uniform stores, it wasn’t far from the ship entrance and wouldn’t be guarded. There wasn’t heavy security at the docks, they likely trusted that no one would be stupid enough to steal from one while in a colony. The ships were all quiet, most of the crews probably in town. He made quick work of sneaking onto the first ship, he knew the guards' patterns for navy ships so it wasn’t difficult to avoid the few on the ship, making his way through the halls to the uniform stores. A quick glance at the thin material told him they weren’t going north, so he was rapidly onto the next.

Two hours later and his fifth ship he heard more commotion, voices on deck. He couldn’t make out what they were saying, but he could tell vaguely where they were, he couldn’t make his way up one of the gangplanks with where they were standing, they’d either hear or see him. He frowned looking at one of the ships cannons, judging if he could make the jump. Would he make too much noise? He shook off any doubts, this was the last ship, if they weren’t heading north he’d have to come up with a different plan. Zuko took a few steps back, giving himself more room before he’d have to jump. He took a shaky breath, he’d had to get up there.

Run. Run. Run. Jump.

His fingers gripped the edge of the cannon, he quickly pulled himself up it, there was less room to get a running start, but he was much closer to the deck now. He could only vaguely hear the voices still so he decided it was safe to continue. His jump just barely allowed him to reach the deck, he held the edge of the deck, finally letting out a breath he didn’t realise he’d been holding. After a quick glance told him no one was around he pulled himself up the railings onto the deck.

He made his way across the deck quickly, only once he got below did he realise the voices had stopped. He couldn’t focus on the likelihood that they’d heard him though, instead rapidly making his way to the uniform stores once again. This time he found what he was looking for, thick material uniforms, made for colder climates.

There were steps coming down the hallway, he could hear each door opening, they’d definitely realised he was here. Zuko looked for a place to hide, his gaze going to the vent in the wall. He made quick work of getting it open, carefully he backed his way in so he could close the cover behind himself. He continued to back his way through without too much of an issue, luckily vents in Fire Nation ships were often rather large due to the natural heat of firebenders, and the heat from burning coal, ventilation was an important thing. Sadly they were only so large that he still had to crawl on his stomach to get around, he wasn’t actually planning to get off the ship, his original plan being to disguise himself as a soldier, that would have to wait till at least after they left port, when they would likely have checked all of the ship for him, but he could probably keep himself hidden in the vents til than.

Once in a portion of the vents that was far enough from any opening that he wouldn’t be seen he pulled his bags and swords off, he wasn’t able to sit up in the vent so Zuko just layed back against the bag that held his clothes and he prepared himself for the likely long while he’d be in here.

—-

Hours passed and Zuko was woken by the sound of many footsteps above, likely those of the crew on deck. He couldn’t hear voices necessarily but he could hear the rumble of many voices at a distance, almost immediately it went silent, Zuko had a feeling he knew why, they never found who’d snuck onto the ship.

It wasn’t long before he started to hear footsteps all around him, doors opening, the screech of furniture before moved across metal floors, unintelligible yelling in the distance. This went on for what felt like hours before finally things began to quiet down. Had they decided he wasn’t aboard? After two searches that seemed likely.

—-

When the ship finally left the dock and Zuko could feel the boat moving, he calmed a bit. He found his eyes drooping, his burn in pain, likely from all the exertion. He’d had a week to rest, but it hadn’t been enough for the extent of the injury, especially not since he’d started traveling so soon after it. He let out a shaky breath as he carefully shifted to try to relieve some of the pain. Eventually he fell into darkness, allowing sleep to overtake him, it was better than being in pain.

He wasn’t sure how long he'd been asleep, but from the obvious change in the temperature of the boat he could tell they were much farther north now. If the weather hadn’t told him the creak of the boat would, the recognisable sound of a metal ship in cold water. His stomach was another sign, the pain reminding him he needed to eat. Zuko dug through his bag, pulling out some of the rations he could eat without them having to be cooked. Hakoda had insisted he take some blubbered seal jerky, something he wasn’t sure he was excited to try, but was definitely grateful to have right now. It took a few bites to get used to the saltiness of the food, after getting past that it actually was pretty good. He drank a bit of the water he still had, careful to save it so he wouldn’t run out. He could tell from the lack of movement in the ship that it was likely nighttime. If he was going to disguise himself now was the time.

Zuko pulled his bags back on, his sword slung over his back now too. He slowly crawled through the vents, finding his way back to the uniform stores. Once to the original vent he’d pulled himself into he got out into the room, mindful to close the vent behind himself after hiding his things a bit aways inside. He then set out to find himself a uniform that fit, luckily with them being organised it didn’t take long. He pulled the clothes one over his black clothes, slightly mourning the fact that he didn’t have his white set as if he had to vanish among the snowy tundra his black clothing would not be useful.

Everything was going smoothly, and that meant something just had to go wrong, because that’s when the door opened…

## Chapter 6

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

as promised, another chapter since my dumbass forgot last week.

### Text

“*Do an activity together.* Sokka, you really said that?” Blue eyes glower at Sokka incredulously, her brows knitted together. Letting out an exasperated sigh she finishes packing her bag. “I don’t have time for this, I have to get to training,” Katara comments, her tone more rushed, though she does look back at Sokka with a sort of pitying look that he can’t figure out the reason for before shaking her head and walking out the door.

Later that day Sokka finds himself among Northern Water Tribe warriors, he’d happened upon them sparring. One of them, who seemed to be the sort of leader of the group, Aoto, invited him to join. Seeing as they were a non-bending group he readily joined in. They were practicing hand to hand, something Sokka wasn’t exactly very trained at. The closest he got was his short amount of training with the Kyoshi warriors, which after he managed to actually win a few matches, he was even more grateful for.

“Your fighting style isn’t Water Tribe, well at least not entirely,” Aoto commented as he took a seat next to Sokka. They were taking a break for lunch, which ended up being a caribou-bear stew. He breathed in the scent before eating, something he’d been doing a lot of in the short time they’d been here, their foods weren’t exactly the same as home, but they were definitely much more similar than he’d been eating recently, and that fact comforted him greatly.

He wouldn’t mention it to Katara or Aang, but he did feel homesick, not all of the time, but enough. Sokka wanted to travel, yes. *Tui and La,* he had been willing to go to war with his father, still would, but that didn’t mean he didn’t miss his home. Being in the North, well it helped a little, but it also somehow made it worse, because it wasn’t *actually* home.

“I did some training in the Earth Kingdom, picked up a few things,” Sokka explained semi-vaguely before starting in on his stew. The man nodded with a light smile before starting his own meal.

Over the meal Sokka learned a few things about Aoto. The man worked as a carver usually, training only for the possibility of an attack. He worked mainly with bone to create anything from weapons to jewelry. He also had two daughters, younger than Sokka or Katara, as well as another baby on the way. His wife works as a healer, as she’s a waterbender, Sokka wasn’t sure if he should ask if she planned to start training for battle as well now, though maybe not, considering she’s pregnant. He wasn’t sure it would be an option otherwise, because Katara was still the only girl being trained to fight. He also learned that Aoto smiles, like all the time, and it doesn’t seem to just be a recent thing, or a way to try to make Sokka comfortable because the corners of his eyes have deep wrinkles, beyond what would be average for someone his age. The man told stories of his younger years, ones it seemed the other warriors had probably heard many times before because of their exasperated reactions. Sokka didn’t mind at all, Aoto reminded him of sitting around the table or fire with his father and Bato when he was younger, the man being similar in age, maybe slightly older. It was a sort of bittersweet happiness to be reminded of the times before.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

*Han*. The guy couldn’t be more annoying, he was infuriating! Obnoxious, loud, overbearing, and his *stupid* laugh. Sokka had a hard time concentrating when all he wanted to do was scream at the older teen. How was *he* gonna be Yue’s husband? She didn’t deserve to have to listen to his egotistical bullshit all– “You alright there, Sokka?”

Isamu, a friend of Han. Surprisingly okay though. He managed to snap Sokka out of his bubbling fury, giving a knowing look as a slight eye roll toward Han, “he’s been worse since you started training with us. I think he’s nervous because some of the guys saw you and Yue together.”

“Well tell him she already turned me down, no need to be *so–”* he just gestured to Han, who was currently acting like he was holding court with a group of young warriors, Sokka supposed he should technically count as that, but he had no interest in kissing up to Han. He earned a chuckle from Isamu for his comment, which was enough to continue to make him *alright* even with having Han as a friend.

“She actually turned you down?”

Sokka gave a sort of nebulous shrug, cause he didn’t really know what was going on there, “I guess. I mean she’s betrothed, and we’ll probably leave soon enough anyways. Doesn’t really matter.”

“Not like she *chose* Han. But yeah, you won’t be around forever, *though* I guess she probably knows that too,” Isamu notes, clearly trying to make some sort of point as he raises an eyebrow as he finishes the comment.

Sokka does end up taking the advice, once he understood it, well sort of, he got that he should at least talk to Yue again. It was good he did, because without that ride on Appa, the black snow would’ve been too late a warning.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Sokka had fought to be on this mission, but now that he was here, he sort of wished he would’ve been taken off to watch after Yue like Han had been. He wanted to prove himself though, and he knew the most about the Fire Nation. Him and two Northern Tribesmen were disguised as Fire Nation soldiers, tasked with looking for Admiral Zhao who was leading this siege. Plan: take Zhao out, bring down their Admiral to destabilize their forces. Sokka would argue that three warriors would not be enough, but the ship would have few people on it, most already moving on the city’s walls, and they needed to be stealthy, find Zhao, take him out, leave. If it went well maybe Sokka would suggest setting off whatever blasting jelly might be in the storage rooms.

“We should split up, this ship is too big to cover like this,” Aoto, the oldest of the trio, suggested.

The man was maybe a little older than Sokka’s father, his dark wavy hair had started to gray, he had wrinkles around his eyes, probably from smiling, as he actually seemed to do that a lot in the short time Sokka had known him. Despite his age he took Sokka’s opinion into consideration, and even looked to him now. Sokka knew out of the three he actually was the one with the most experience dealing with the Fire Nation. The North had removed themselves from the war long ago, long before Sokka was born, and frankly he’s pretty sure before Aoto was born. The man was trained, he was a warrior, but not in practice. He worked as a carver when not training, until now it had been decades since the North had seen a fight, so a man even his father’s senior looked at him for agreement before making the decision.

“We should stay together,” Sokka urges, having a bad feeling about splitting up. But they weren’t the only two on this mission.

“Sokka he’s right, this will take too long, we got disguises because of you, we’ll be alright,” Isamu countered, who Sokka had decided he didn’t particularly like from the jump as he was friends with Han, though the guy wasn’t actually all that bad, just a bad choice in friends. He was a few years older than him, enough that he seemed to look at Sokka as a kid, despite also still being a teenager himself. He’d only realised this because despite his friendship with Han, Isamu had ventured to treat him similarly to how Sokka did Katara, a younger sibling. To be fair, recently Sokka had stepped back from that a bit, he’s still the big brother of course, but he knows Katara could fend for herself now. Isamu did not seem entirely convinced of this in Sokka’s case, but maybe he was wrong considering he’s agreeing to splitting up.

Sokka really wasn’t for splitting up, it meant they didn’t have back up, they would be outnumbered easily if found out, but he wasn’t really a leader here, despite his experience, he’s still the youngest here, and doesn’t have any actual training. He knew Aoto would eventually relent if he pushed it, but they didn’t really have time. Things in the Southern Water Tribe were usually solved by a vote, and they essentially just had one, so despite his reservations, he agreed.

“Fine. We meet back on the main deck in a candle mark, any of us aren’t here, leave.” Sokka knew they couldn’t wait around if someone didn’t return, because it likely meant they’d been found.

Both of them nodded in agreement before they all split.

Sokka knew this wasn’t the right plan, but he didn’t know it would go this horribly. He’d swept through his area of the ship before he made it to the top deck, no closer to finding Admiral Zhao. He’d been the farthest from the top deck so the others should already be back. He came upon a sight he hadn’t thought of, he’d found Admiral Zhao. At his feet were the charred remains of Isamu, if it wasn’t for Aoto currently forced to his knee by two Fire Nation Soldiers, he wouldn’t even be able to tell. The man’s usually happy expression was hard, blue eyes devoid of the spark they usually seemed to have. Sokka froze at the scene, and immediately Zhao seemed to know he didn’t belong. He felt his arms grabbed, pulled behind him as his helmet was torn off. Forced to kneel as well, though he wasn’t as close to Zhao as Aoto. Still the man’s amber eyes lit up in a way that made his stomach want to cave in on itself.

“How unexpected, someone *actually* useful,” Zhao’s lips pulled into a sickening smirk, as if the smell of burnt flesh wasn’t enough to make him ill already. “No Avatar with you today? Foolish, to think someone *like you* could best me alone.”

Sokka wanted to yell that he wasn’t alone, that the other warriors were capable, but considering one laid burned to the point that he was unrecognisable, and the other was also captured, well that wouldn’t be very true. Zhao seemed to read his thoughts, as he chuckled.

“Well I only need one of you–” Zhao’s hand lit a blaze, and then the flames were surrounding Aoto. Sokka’s pleas went ignored as he screamed for him to stop, and soon his screams were deafened by Aoto. The man was shouting out in pain as fire rained down on him, not relenting as the guards that he originally held the warrior had joined in the *murder*.

Tears filled his eyes as Aoto’s scream stopped, Sokka knew when that meant. The only thing left of either of the North Water Tribe warriors was the smell of burnt flesh and unrecognisable corpses. As the fire rescinded, Zhao simply let out a sigh, as though murdering two men had been some boring chore that he finally finished. Sokka seethed with anger but he couldn’t bring himself to fight, he barely felt like he was in his body. His limbs didn’t seem to want to move, like he wasn’t in control. But the truth was, Sokka was just terrified. He’d frozen the second he was Isamu and that was the end of it, he’d failed.

“This has wasted enough of my time, throw him below, I’m sure he’d love to meet our other *guest*.”

—-

Sokka found himself thrown into a cell below deck. They’d forced him out of his Fire Nation uniform, and taken his boomerang that had been tucked into it, leaving him in his Water Tribe clothes. He didn’t feel the cold of the metal floor, lying that with tears blurring his vision. He felt the way his heart raced, beating violently like it wanted to tear from his chest. His breaths were shallow, he couldn’t seem to get any air into his lungs, they burned like acid. He belatedly realised he had started sobbing, gasping for breath as his tears echoed off the metal floor. He could hear footsteps above, the creaking of the ship’s hull, the wave crashing against metal.

He’d watch Aoto *burn*. Zhao had burned him to death, like it was *nothing.* He barely had a reaction, he didn’t care. Sokka only survived because in all Zhao’s chasing them he apparently knew who Sokka was, and somehow that saved him. It didn’t matter though, because two warriors had lost their lives for a failed mission. Zhao was off the ship, even if he managed to get out of here he would already be at the walls. Aoto and Isamu were *murdered* and there was no point, nothing was even accomplished. They died for nothing. And Sokka was alive. Sokka was alive, but he couldn’t even pick himself up off the frozen metal. Sokka was alive, but the mission had failed. Sokka was alive, but he was in a cell. Sokka was alive, but he shouldn’t be.

Eventually his breaths slowed, maybe he’d even fallen asleep, honestly he wasn’t sure. He hadn’t exactly been all there, or he’d been too much there, he didn’t know which. But he knew he had to get off the ground because laying here wouldn’t fix anything. He wasn’t sure how long he just laid on the hard metal floor before he finally managed to push himself from it. He rubbed his eyes clear of tears, trying to get an idea of where he was. There was only a small hallway before him, and to the left stairs that led up, probably to the deck. He could only remember getting thrown onto the floor of the cell, not being dragged here, because they’d of had to drag him, since his legs weren’t listening after– *can’t think about it*.

He looked around the cell, only to finally notice someone in the far corner of it. They were… sleeping? He hoped they were just sleeping. He brought his focus to the person on the ground, because he *needed* something to focus on before he lost himself to the sight of flesh melting from someone’s body. They hadn’t moved since he’d got there, well at least since he’d noticed them. He really wasn’t sure if he could handle a third dead body today. His eyes focused on their bare back, there was more dried blood than skin visible. Sokka cringed at the sight, he couldn’t even tell what caused all the damage, but it looked new, a few days at most.

“Hello?”

They still didn’t move, they weren’t actually dead right? There *was* a lot of blood. Sokka carefully rose, his legs feeling like they might give up on him any second. He walked toward the person laying on the ground, *slowly*. Even as he got close he couldn’t really see them, face covered in shaggy black hair. Carefully kneeling down, he looked over their back, so caked in blood that it was hard to tell what wounds were under the red substance. They only wore a pair of shorts, though they looked like they had probably been pants originally. Chains linked their ankles, loose enough to walk, but not to run. Their legs were covered in bruises and burns but nothing looked broken at least. He could see the rise and fall of their breathing, so definitely alive. Thank the Spirits, Sokka *really* didn’t want them to be dead. He debated trying to wake them again, should he just let them rest? They were probably in pain.

Sokka managed pretty much no time waiting, as the second he wasn’t focused on the person in front of him his mind went to– *no.* He let out a soft sigh before he lightly went to shake them awake. They flinched immediately at his hand on their shoulder, and then it was a blur. Sokka hadn’t expected it, but before he could blink he was laid out, the previously maybe dead person over him. He recognised him, but he couldn’t find himself able to say anything, probably because the other teen had his chain hands around his throat.

He stared up at gold eyes, too stunned to move, or gather the will to push him off. He just laid there, looking into gold eyes that didn’t seem to be looking at him. They were cold, angry, they looked nearly void of life, as though maybe he was just a walking corpse. It was only a moment, a long one, but still, when something like recognition and then immediately after relief flashed through his golden eyes. He removed his hands from Sokka’s throat before settling back on his legs before managing to get himself up. He teetered back toward the corner of the cell, which wasn’t exactly far since there had been where he was laying. He let himself fall into the corner, wincing as he hit the ground.

Sokka slowly got himself into a seated position, slowly pulling himself from his stupor before he turned his attention to the firebender.

“Sorry.”

### End Notes

## Chapter 7

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

I know it is still April 1st, but no joke chapter today.

No big preamble today, hope you enjoy the chapter.

### Text

Sokka couldn’t help but notice the burn that wrapped around his side, but the moment his eyes went to it the boy shifted his legs so that they were in front of his torso. He didn’t miss the way the firebender’s face scrunched up in pain when he moved, likely stretching the wounds on his back. The fact that he’d *attacked*, he didn’t really have a better choice of descriptive despite the word feeling incorrect, *pounced?* That seemed more correct, it probably hadn’t helped when he’d pounced on Sokka, *still weird.* In any case, his wounds had to hurt, they clearly did, and he was probably laying the way he had been to help it, but Sokka doubted he’d turn his back on him, even if it meant alleviating some pain. Sokka was pretty sure he’d have opened some of them with his moving around, but it wouldn't be like he could tell, even if he could see the firebender’s back.

He looked so different, but also not at all? Sokka could tell he was angry, but in a different way than at the Southern Water Tribe, it was not the forceful sort of anger he’d shown when capturing Aang, the kind that was put on to instill fear. It wasn’t the calculating anger from when he escaped Jet’s hideout either, where he made the decision to flee rather than burn the place to the ground, Sokka was under no illusion that he couldn’t have. But no, this anger was cold, not in the analytical sense, there wasn’t any planning going on, no, he looked *empty*, but it was such a way that the only thing that would fill that emptiness would be unbridled rage. It was like a wolf caught in a trap. And that look, it sent twisting chills through his body, yanking at him and pushing through at the same time, it was hard to breathe, how could gold look so cold?

“Sokka, right?”

His voice broke Sokka out of his first set of thoughts only to throw him straight into another, rough and strained like he hadn’t drank in days or spoken properly in Spirits knows how long. How did he know his name? Sokka didn’t realise he hadn’t responded until the firebender just moved on to something else.

“Bato wanted you to know he made it back to the fleet safely.”

Sokka couldn’t help but act like a fish out of water, looking like he was gasping for breaths, which to be fair he was close to as panic continued to set in. He’d met Bato? Bato was with the fleet? Were they okay? Had he attacked them? Had Zhao attacked them? Suddenly his mind was completely off of what happened only minutes before —it could have been hours for all he knew—, which was likely for the best as if he thought about what happened he might just *break*.

“What did you do?” Sokka could hear the way his voice shook, fear overtaking rage. But he’d said Bato wanted him to know he was okay? They’d talked. He’d talked to Bato, to the fleet, maybe saw his dad? “When?” he asked before the firebender had a chance to answer his first question.

“I didn’t attack them if that’s what you’re thinking. Chief Hakoda’s fleet was coming to help the Earth Kingdom forces defending Omashu.”

“When?” Sokka urged again.

“A month maybe? I’m not exactly sure, I got caught… a little over a week after I left the Earth Kingdom camp. It was probably over a month ago. Hard to tell time down here.”

Sokka just stared at him for a minute, trying to figure out if what he was saying was a lie. *Defending Omashu*, that meant the Fire Nation was trying to take over one of the last major cities. The fleet was helping with that? A land battle, something rare for the South Water Tribe, especially recently. And to work with the Earth Kingdom, just as uncommon, they were on the same side technically but that didn’t mean the countries were friends. But if Omashu was really being attacked… Why was *he* there though, at an Earth Kingdom camp? He’d been captaining a Fire Navy ship only a few months ago. But Sokka had seen the posters, wanted posters for him, and he had been captive for at least a few weeks. But would an Earth Kingdom camp even take that risk? Why would they bother?

“You alright there?”

“Just trying to figure out if you’re lying,” Sokka admitted, he didn’t have the brain power for this right now.

“Well my sister always said I was a horrible liar, so you tell me.”

And frankly he sounded more tired than Sokka, distant, like he was only sort of here. He really didn’t like the idea that this had been the outcome of captivity by the Fire Nation, though Sokka had a feeling that said state might not be due to the captor being Fire so much as being Zhao.

Answer: No. Sokka didn’t think he was lying.

“What were you doing in an Earth Kingdom camp?”

“Wasn’t really planned… a friend of mine helped me out, after I got burned.”

“You’re *friends* with an Earth Kingdom soldier?” Sokka clarified, since that seemed to be the explanation.

The firebender went to respond but suddenly the cell was filled with the echo of coughs. Sokka was left unsure what to do as he didn’t exactly have water to offer him. It took a while but eventually he got his coughing to stop, though Sokka could almost hear how his throat probably stung as he tried to take in breaths. He blinked the tears that had welled in his eyes away, quickly wiping them away. *Fuck*, he heard him croak out under his breath. He ended up just opting to nod in what Sokka assumed was his response to the earlier question.

“You okay?”

Again only a nod. He leaned back as he tried to steady his breathing again after the coughing fit. He hissed in pain when his back hit the cold metal wall of the cell, quickly going back to sitting up so his broken skin didn’t touch anything. His eyes blinked heavily, Sokka noticing how he’d kept his eyes closed for an extended period of time, seemingly without noticing. The more he looked at the other boy the more he could see how tired he looked, as though he’d barely been sleeping, maybe he hadn’t been. Sokka decided he could ask more questions about the firebender and about his apparent friendship with some unexpected people later, right now he needed to figure out if there was any way out of here.

“Any plans of escape?”

He was only met with a bitter, weak laugh.

“You’ve tried?”

This time a nod and a hand held up with three fingers shown.

“Three times. Okay. So you know how to get out of the cell?”

He shook his head, though then he paused. Sokka waited a minute as it seemed like he was thinking. There was a nod instead now. “Zhao… keys,” he managed to get out.

“He’s not on the ship.”

And that comment seemed to light something, golden eyes suddenly focused, looking anxiously in blue ones as if trying to figure out if Sokka was lying. “Two locks, one’s– fire,” his voice barely went above a whisper and kept cutting out, but he gestured first to the cell door and then up the stairs. It took Sokka a long moment but he thought he figured out what he meant.

“Like the Fire Sage temple? It is unlocked with firebending?” Sokka questioned, trying to clarify. Again he got a nod, though also a quirked eyebrow as if asking how he knew that.

“Went there with Aang and Katara on the Winter Solstice,” Sokka explained, and based on the simple look of understanding it didn’t seem he needed to explain who either of those people are. “Can you get it open?”

“Yes.” His voice came out quiet but confident, so Sokka decided to trust the answer.

“He’s attacking Agna Qel’a, the Northern Water Tribe capital, we need a way out of the cell without the keys,” Sokka decided to clarify, just in case he didn’t know, plenty of people didn’t seem to know anything about the Water Tribes. Gold eyes glowered at him, as if annoyed he’d said anything, it was a look similar to one he’d seen from Katara, when she tells him he’s being a know-it-all. Sokka put up his hands in a mock surrender, but as he did so the firebender flinched back, hitting into the wall.

“Shit, are you okay? I didn’t mean to scare–”

“I’m fine. I wasn’t–” he bit out in a hoarse tone, eyes evading Sokka’s now.

Sokka was quiet now, slowly lowering his hands. He carefully scooted himself back slightly, giving the other boy some space, hoping it would lessen whatever stress he’d just caused.

Suddenly the boat jerked, as though a raging storm had appeared. There was no sound of rain or snow beating down on the metal ship though, no thunder, but a moment later the boat rattled, the metal screeching as it hit something.

“–hit another boat,” the firebender seemed to be thinking out loud.

“What do you mean we hit another boat?!” Sokka yelled, the idea to stay calm quickly vanished. They were trapped on the lower part of a ship when they’d crashed? What if the boat sank? Would they just be left here? Were they–

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Zuko levied the water tribesman with a unsure gaze, trying to decide what to do. He was yanked from any further thought as the boat seemed to crash over a large wave. Neither could see what was going on, but if Zuko had to guess, this was waterbending, but more powerful than something he’d ever witnessed. He’d seen boats moved with waterbending, but this was an immense force, metal screaming and screeching as the boat was crashed into with aggressive water.

“Something or someone is causing massive wav–” the boat was crashed into from the left, sending the two of them tumbling into the wall; Sokka crashed into him. He closed his eyes, trying to stave off crying out in pain, barely hearing Sokka blurt out *sorry!* The boat didn’t steady, keeping them against the wall, though Sokka managed to at least move next to him rather than being stuck having crashed against Zuko.

“We need to get out of here, the boat can’t withstand this.”

“How?” He nearly rolled his eyes, they were in a cell, how were they supposed to get out of here?

“Melt a bar?” Sokka reasoned, gesturing between Zuko and the bars of the cell.

“I can barely keep a flame going right now and you expect me to melt metal?” Zuko would’ve yelled, but his voice didn’t let him get much above a whisper.

“Well if this ship goes down we’re either drowning or freezing to death so I suggest you use the power of friendship”

“I don’t have any friends.”

“I’m Sokka, and you are?” he asked, putting his hand out.

Zuko just looked at him bewildered, not understanding what the boy was trying to do here.

“We’re becoming friends, if you don’t give me your name I’m just gonna call you jerkbender”

“Zuko.”

Sokka grabbed Zuko’s arm, going to use his other hand to get Zuko to grip his, but the other did so before he could, accepting the Water Tribe greeting. Sokka smiled a goofy sort of grin, “now we’re friends. So, the power of friendship, you don’t have to melt it, heat it enough to bend it.”

They were thrown the opposite way by another wave crashing against the ship, the sudden movement sending them into the wall, Zuko groaning in pain as he hit the bars. He struggled to right himself, Sokka helped pull him up from the left of him, he flinched at the sudden touch, not being able to see him. Zuko turned to see Sokka giving him a questioning eyebrow raise, which Zuko chose to ignore before focusing on the bar near him. *Fuck it*, he gripped the bar with his hands, immediately he could feel his skin burning against the cold, it quickly went from the sting of walking in from the cold to actual burning. He closed his eyes, trying his best to ignore the feeling as his skin burned. He couldn’t keep a flame going enough to weaken the bar so this would have to do, plus he needed to save enough fire power for the door above too.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“Shit,” Sokka muttered, as he heard water dripping, the ship was taking on water, he could hear a slight sloshing noise somewhere nearby. Zuko didn’t seem to hear anything, but he also didn’t seem to notice the smell of his own flesh burning. He tried to blink away the vision of burned bodies, Aoto screaming as he was surrounded by flames, the sight of flesh melting– *stop.* Sokka looked at Zuko's hands, cringing at the smell he knew was coming from them burning, before he could get pulled back into his thoughts he noticed the bar was bending, “it's working! We just have to put pressure on the bar to bend it enough to get through!”

Zuko didn’t say anything as he started to bend the bar, Sokka pushing against him until the metal was pushed just far enough they should be able to squeeze through. Sokka watched as Zuko pulled his hands away, the palms bright red, skin already beginning the peel and bubble from the burns. It was only then that he noticed the tears rolling down the others face, which he promptly wiped away with the back of his hands so as to not upset the burns.

“You might burn your back going through, so I suggest being careful,” Zuko noted before he moved to slide his way between the wall and the bent bar. He only slightly winced as his back hit the bar while he slid sideways through. Sokka followed quickly, luckily his jacket protecting him from the hot metal for the short time it took to get through the bars.

“Now we hope no one is up top,” Sokka notes, as he looks up the stairs.

“It's Zhao’s personal ship, you said he left, most of his men would have gone into Agna Qel'a with him.”

Sokka found himself a bit surprised hearing him say Anga Qel’a like a Water Tribesmen, rather than the butchering of it he’d heard a few times people mistook him for a northerner in the Earth Kingdom. He only managed a nod in response before Zuko started his way up the stairs. The cells were just below deck, the stairs leading up to a metal latch rather than a proper door, the latch looks nearly just part of the deck from above.

“I don’t think anyone’s above. I can unlock it if I can manage the fire power, stand back.”

Sokka decided it was best to listen, staying several steps further down, he watched as Zuko bawled a fist, punching up toward what looked like a shallow hole in the ceiling door. Fire shot from his hand, the sound of metal gears clicking and rotating followed. Zuko stumbled back, Sokka barely quick enough to keep them both from tumbling down the metal steps. The firebender didn’t speak as he slowly steadied himself, the latch had opened, the dark sky above them, water beginning to flow into the space.

They were quickly on the deserted deck, Sokka forcing Zuko up the steps before more water pushed them back. They made it onto a water filled deck. The boat tilted at an odd angle, but the water seemed calm now. Blue eyes searched the surrounding waters for any other boats, in the dark he saw no one around, but he also couldn’t see Agna Qel’a.

“We need to get off the boat, I could hear it taking on water somewhere near us when we were below.”

“The lifeboats are on the opposite side of the deck, I’ll be right there.”

Zuko ran toward a door that likely led to the captain’s quarters before Sokka could respond.

—-

The two of them were lucky there hadn’t been many people on the boat because there were multiple lifeboats still hooked to the side of the ship. Sokka quickly *stole* extra supplies from one of the other lifeboats, not knowing how long it would take them to find land. The boats were clearly meant for many more people than just two teenagers, so there was plenty of room for the extra supplies.

“Get in the boat!” Zuko came running, now wearing a shirt and actual pants rather than the torn to bits pants and no shirt from before. His chains from before were gone, probably having found the key in Zhao’s quarters. He held two bags, as well as what looked like a sword and… boomerang! It had been hidden under his Fire Nation disguise, he was almost sure it had been tossed overboard, but here it was! As well as a group of soldiers appearing around the corner behind Zuko. *Shit*, Sokka now understood why he yelled to get in the boat. He hit the lever to start lowering it before jumping in, Zuko not far behind. Sokka watched as Zuko narrowly dodged a fire blast sent his way. He dropped down from the ship, landing in front of Sokka with a notable jerk in pain as he hit the small boat. The boat then hit the water, causing another jolt, Sokka immediately started rowing, trying to get them out of view in the cover of darkness.

“He went over the side! The Prince is escaping with the Water Tribe prisoner!”

Blue eyes shot to golden ones, he didn’t stop rowing, he couldn’t. He kept rowing as he watched the *prince’s* face pale, golden eyes staring back at him, wide and uncertain, seemingly awaiting the other’s reaction while also already expecting the worst. Sokka pulled his eyes away, focusing on rowing the boat as far as he could.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

once again we are at the end of a chapter, I do hope you enjoyed it. as always I greatly appreciate comments, kudos, etc.

until next time (probably Wednesday)
