## Chapter 8

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

This chapter is literally just them talking, so hopefully ya'll don't mind that. Also I'm a believer in letting Zuko say fuck because you can't tell me he wouldn't if that show was higher rated.

Into the chapter we go.

### Text

Sokka wasn’t sure how long he’d rowed for, but considering the sun was now coming over the horizon and there was no sign of a ship, it had apparently been a while. He and *Prince* Zuko hadn’t spoken a word since getting into the boat. He finally got a chance to look at the bandaging on the firebender’s hands, seeming actually pretty decent. He'd somewhat watched him doing it earlier, but hadn’t fully been paying attention. He supposed it made sense for him to know how to treat a burn.

“So um… *Prince*, huh?”

Zuko looked up from the bottom of the boat, which he’d found exceptionally interesting for a while now. He let out a sigh, seeming to realise this wasn’t a conversation he could avoid.

“*Banished* Prince, technically.” Sokka noticed his voice sounded much less hoarse, he vaguely remembered Zuko having offered him water earlier but he’d ignored him, still reeling over the whole *prince* thing.

“So, the Jerk Lord is your dad?”

“Regrettably,” Zuko responded with a sort of drawl, and Sokka almost laughed at the fact that he didn’t correct him saying *Jerk Lord.*

“Damn that has to suck,” Sokka commented, realising only after that, that probably wasn’t the expected response, “wait were you banished because of Aang escaping?” He was referencing what happened at the South Pole, but Zuko took a minute to answer as if trying to figure out what exactly he was talking about.

“No, I uh… was thirteen when I was banished.” His words more stilted, as he tried not to fiddle with his hands too much, likely because of pain.

“Thirteen!?” Sokka yelled, his voice cracking at the sudden pitch change, he coughed slightly, trying to cover it. Then something occurred to him, “if you were banished why were you looking for the Avatar? Why did you have a ship?”

“The ship was my uncle’s doing,” he paused, a sour look appearing, “the Fire Lord said that if I captured the Avatar that I could come home. It took me too long to realise that he’d only said that because everyone believed the Avatar was dead. After–” Zuko shifted slightly, sitting up straighter before he fell into a bow, “I am sorry for my actions at Wolf Cove. I should not have threatened any of you.”

“There’s no need for the bowing!” Sokka exclaims, his cheeks flushing at the unexpected action, he really hadn’t expected Zuko to apologise, being that he’s a *prince*, and not just any prince, the *Fire Nation Prince*. Even before knowing that he didn’t really expect an apology, not like firebenders usually give those out. “No one got hurt, and you kept your promise not to attack the South Pole, even after we broke Aang out. Obviously it's not *fine*, but it's fine, you know?” Sokka awkwardly tried to explain.

Zuko slowly raised from the bow, he continued his point after a slight nod, “I couldn’t stop thinking about how young Aang is, I mean he’s just a kid. And then everything fell apart, my ship was damaged so I went to the closest port” –he took a deep breath, eyes that had begun to glaze over slightly jumping back into focus, he let out the breath– “Zhao was there, he wanted information about how my ship got damaged, I lied.”

“Can I ask, why were you banished?”

“Disrespect.”

Sokka waited for him to elaborate, but after a few long moments it was clear Zuko wasn’t going to say more without prompting. “There’s got to be more to it than that! If I got banished for disrespect I would’ve been banished well before thirteen!”

“Ultimately that was the reason.”

“I’m looking for specifics here Zuko. Trying to figure out if I actually trust you, you know, considering your father is kind of the most evil guy in the world.” Sokka tried to reason, in a somewhat dramatic sort of way, not as if what he said wasn’t true though.

Zuko levied him with a sort of exasperated look, like he wanted nothing more than for this conversation to be over. The longer he looked at Zuko the more he realised he wasn’t annoyed, no his eyes were still empty, like how they were on the boat, except there was no rage or anger, there was fear. Sokka almost wanted to relent, to end the conversation and switch to something else, but he needed to know, know that Zuko isn’t like his father, and isn't like the man who killed Sokka’s mother.

“It was my first time in a council meeting, I begged my uncle to let me sit in, he warned me, told me I shouldn’t say anything, I didn’t listen. Some general, I don’t even remember his name anymore, but his plan, I couldn’t just sit there. He wanted to throw untrained people onto the front lines, a distraction for the Earth Kingdom, they were a sacrifice. Everyone knew they’d be killed, the group they were sending, they were newly conscripted, all of them were no older than sixteen. I was an idiot, thinking saying something would change anything, I stood up, told him his plan was inhuman, that they were sending people to their deaths for no reason. It was seen as disrespectful, and because of that the dispute would be handled through an Agni Kai. I thought I’d be dueling the general, I had spoken against his plan– No one said anything, I didn’t know he’d be the one I was facing until I was already there. I couldn’t fight him, I didn’t *want* to fight him. Despite everything he’d already done, at the time I still wanted to make him proud. And beyond the reluctance because of him being my father, he’s the Fire Lord, even in an Agni Kai, attacking him could be grounds for treason. So I refused to fight, a disrespect to him and to the sanctity of the duel, he–” Zuko’s fingers went to his facial scar.

Sokka’s eyes had already grown ten times since the beginning of Zuko’s explanation, but then the realisation of what the Fire Lord, Zuko’s *father* had done to him. Sokka’s father wouldn’t so much as raise his voice at him unless he was in danger, to think a parent could permanently scar their child. He thought he might throw up over the side of the boat, luckily he managed to keep it in.

“Anyways, I was an idiot, I was banished after, I’m not sure he would recognis swe me now, I never saw him after the Agni Kai. I woke up on the ship.” Zuko pulled fingers through his hair, wincing as it stretched the wounds across his torso when he lifted his arms. Sokka decided this was the perfect distraction, because they really needed to get away from the conversation.

“You never actually cleaned those did you?”

“They’ll be fine.”

“You’re gonna get an infection you idiot. Let me help, turn around.”

“If I take off my shirt they're gonna pull open.”

“You should’ve thought about that before. I know I saw some alcohol for cleaning wounds in one of these…”

“It’s the smaller dark red bag, there’s one of every life raft,” Zuko informed, resigned to the fact that this was going to happen. He pulled off the jacket that he had on, then turned before he started to struggle pulling off the shirt. Sokka couldn’t tell how much blood was on it due to the shirt being dark red, but at Zuko’s wincing he assumed a decent amount was stuck to the shirt and his skin. Sokka said a silent apology before he quickly yanked up the shirt. Zuko yelled at the newly dried blood ripped from his skin.

“A little warning!” he shouted, tensed up and slightly shaking, whether the shaking was from the cold, the pain, or shock Sokka wasn’t sure. Zuko held the jacket to his front, likely trying to ward off some of the cold. His grip was that of the Unagi, likely white knuckled under the bandages, his grip never loosening as Sokka carefully cleaned his wounds. After a slight yelp when the alcohol touched one of his wounds the first time, he only occasionally flinched. Cleaning only revealed more wounds, many looking at least somewhat fresh, probably from his time as Zhao’s prisoner, he was lucky these didn’t get infected. Sokka could see older wounds too, almost all of them looked like burn scars, none as bad as his front or his eye, most nearly blending with the skin, but still there. He couldn’t help but scowl at the sight, sure he thought the Fire Nation was evil, of course he did, they destroyed most of his tribe, they killed his mother, but he’d assumed they weren’t cruel to each other. Zuko’s story had put a dent in that idea, but he’s a prince, there couldn’t be many people who could get away with scaring a prince, before now were these all his father? How had the burn across his torso happened? It was newer, a few months at most. Then there were burns that were newer, bright red tough skin, Sokka decided not to bring them up, nearly all of them were smaller, they didn’t look like firebending from an attack, Sokka had seen what that looked like, traveling through the Earth Kingdom, no, they were concentrated, many looking like a slash of a blade, but the ones around his upper arms and back, they looked like hand prints. He didn’t say anything, carefully applying burn salve before going to hand it to him so he could put it on the burn that marred his front.

“Here, burn salve, before we wrap your torso,” Sokka spoke, trying to hand Zuko the jar. There was a moment of hesitation, long enough to feel odd, before Zuko turned to the left and then took the salve. It only dawned on him then, that Zuko likely couldn't see out of that eye.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“I’m not leaving without him,” Katara spoke, as she waited at the docks of Agna Qel’a for more bodies to be pulled from the waters, “I can’t leave without knowing, Aang.”

Aang only nodded, he couldn’t bring himself to say anything, Sokka had been on one of those ships, a ship he’d destroyed or drowned. If Sokka was dead, he killed him. Aang had killed Sokka. There were hundreds dead because of him, both Fire Nation and Water Tribe soldiers got swept up in his waterbending. How many would never be found? How many were too far down in the icy depth of the polar ocean for them to bend to the surface? Would Sokka be one of them? Would they never find him?

“I’m going to stay here for a while longer, you have to go though Aang. Find an earthbending teacher, you don’t have time to sit around here with me.”

“I’m not leaving you.”

“If you don’t, more people will die.”

“I have till the end of summer, I still have to work on waterbending, I can’t leave yet.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;

Zuko couldn’t row for a long time, his hands very much wrecked from having to heat up the metal bars without enough strength to keep a steady flame. He tried to ignore it as he rowed, while Sokka finally got some rest.

Gold eyes looked to the night sky, for once thankful he’d focused mainly on naval skills as he studied the stars to figure out where they were. The Earth Kingdom was the best choice, part of the northern areas were Fire Nation colonies, they’d have to head southeast to avoid them, they’d probably end up near the northern air temple. Luckily they’d seem to have been rowing pretty directly south, though it might not be luck, Sokka likely knew how to navigate using the stars as well.

As he rowed he tried to keep his mind busy, knowing otherwise he’d focus on the pain he was in, on what had happened on that ship. He found his mind drifting to Sokka, more specifically if he was technically a prince as well. Zuko knew more about the Northern Tribe than the Southern Tribe, he wasn’t sure if they had royalty. Hakoda was of course their Chief, but whether that meant Sokka would also become Chief of the Southern Water Tribe he wasn’t sure. He knew of the Northern Water Tribe Princess, Yue, she was only a few months younger than Zuko. He knew it was likely that the Southern Water Tribe didn’t have the same customs partially because until recently they had been much more affected by the war, they didn’t have water benders to build a city like Agna Qel’a, not anymore.

It was a few hours laters when he heard Sokka let out a loud yawn as he finally woke up, the sun was just barely over the horizon now. After a few long minutes of silence he looked back to find blue eyes staring at him.

“You know staring is rude,” Zuko commented, not that he wasn’t used to people staring at him, it happened pretty often, even before he’d been burned, but that didn’t mean he wasn’t self conscious about it.

“Sorry, *your highness.”* Sokka retorted as he got up. Zuko chose not to respond to the taunt, there were a few moments of silence before Sokka spoke again. “You eat anything?”

“No” Zuko stopped rowing, only to reach for the bag behind him that he knew would have rations, mainly dried fish and rice with a small pot and clean water to make it with, it relied on a fire bender that wasn’t somewhat out of commission to keep a flame going though. “You’ll have to make do with dried fish, I’ll need at least another day before I can keep a fire going for rice, we’ll probably be to land by then though.”

“You even know which direction you’re rowing?” Sokka questioned as he took the bag from Zuko, pulling out a portion of dried fish for both of them.

“Southeast, toward Earth Kingdom mainland. We’ll end up somewhere around the Northern Air Temple.”

Sokka hummed in response, “I wish we could go back toward the North Pole. But we’ll be going into a ship graveyard if we do that, and this time of year we’ll be lucky if we don’t get crushed by waves during a storm,” he seemed to be thinking out loud. “Katara and Aang are going to be looking for me, I can’t just let them think I’ve…”

“You can send them a message once we reach a town, they’ll be coming to the Earth Kingdom soon enough anyways, doesn’t he still need an earthbending teacher?” It was only logical to go to the Earth Kingdom, they could easily go their separate ways there, Sokka could wait for his sister and Aang while Zuko moved on. Going north would probably take longer considering how far out to sea they had been pushed, plus it would be cold, they might freeze before getting back, if they weren’t crushed by pieces of Fire Navy vessels.

“How do you know that?” Sokka glared, though considering he was stuffing his face with dried fish currently it wasn’t all that menacing.

“Our wanted posters tend to be next to each other, they haven’t added a fourth to your group, plus the Avatar is meant to learn the elements in order.”

“Speaking of, how’d you go from defending rooky soldiers to a full blown traitor? I mean what you did wasn’t against your country, not really.” Sokka spoke between mouthfuls, his eyes not so much focused on Zuko anymore as the vast ocean around them.

“I wouldn’t consider myself a traitor.” Zuko kept his tone calm, taking small bites of his dried fish to hopefully keep himself from throwing it up after having not eaten much for however long it had been on that ship.

“So what, you still want the Fire Nation to win? Rule the world or whatever?” Sokka louring at him, though he still hadn’t stopped eating so Zuko didn’t take it too seriously. The conversation wasn’t exactly avoidable considering Sokka now knows who he is.

“I just want what is best for my people,” he reasoned, not really sure how much Sokka actually cared about hearing his answer or thoughts on the war.

“And what exactly does that mean? Because most of your people want me dead, or at the very least bowing down to your father.”

“My *people* are being fed lies. Many have never met someone not from the Fire Nation or colonies off the battlefield. In school they are taught lies about the past, about how the war started, told that is was necessary, that our actions were not that of aggressors but a way to share our greatness with the world, and very few get to see the world outside being sent to fight a battle they are convinced is just. My nation is being destroyed by this war–” And Zuko knew from the way Sokka’s posture changed, up right, his shoulders back, as if he was trying to make himself larger, more intimidating. His eyes proper daggers rather than a vague questioning glare from before.

“*Your nation!?* Do you even know what has happened to the South Pole, why the North abandoned us, how our home has been destroyed?” His voice echoed over the water.

“That wasn’t– I’m saying that there is no one coming out of this war better, there is no nation improved by it,” Zuko tried to reason, even knowing this conversation wasn’t going to be one of reason, because Zuko had seen that look before, ironically when Sokka’s father had defended him, it was the same glare General Fong had given him.

“So what if it *was* improving the Fire Nation it would be fine?”

“For Agni’s sake! Are you *trying* to disagree with me? I’m saying I don’t agree with this war, isn’t that what you want? The *enemy prince”* –he put a heavy emphasis on those words– “is telling you he doesn’t want this war to continue and you are taking issue with that?”

“Well because frankly I don’t care what is best for *your* nation of jerkbenders! Your war is the reason my mother is *gone*, it is the reason my sister had to go across the world to find a waterbending teacher, the reason my father left.”

Zuko didn’t say anything in return for a long moment, waiting for Sokka breathing to slow a bit as he wasn’t looking to get thrown from the life raft. It was hard to decide what to say, it seemed a bit like Sokka wanted an argument, he wanted *someone* to put the blame on, and Zuko was a good target, a great one even. Of course Zuko had never been particularly apt at holding his tongue or his temper. He let out a harsh breath, should he try to relate? Should he just stay silent? What was the right choice here?

“Aren’t you going to say anything?” Sokka bit, blue eyes stayed focused on gold, not letting him look away. It wasn’t that glare anymore, it was one much more meaningful. He looked… lost. His expression might be angry but he’d seen that look before, he saw it when his eyes reflected back at him in the water.

“Anything I say is only going to anger you more.”

“No, I want to hear it, what have you *truly* lost in this war?”

And from Sokka’s tone Zuko knew he wanted an argument, he was trying the egg him on. And he decided to allow it, because Zuko was fine with being the bad guy, he was fine having anger taken out on him. Sokka would only throw more words at him, so why not just get out what he’d been holding in, if it was going to be a screaming match anyways it wasn’t like he had to hold his tongue. He wanted a retort, so Zuko would give him one.

“That’s a joke right? You’re asking me what *I’ve* lost to this war. You’re *looking* at me right now and asking that? Fine. Beyond what you can see from my *fucking* face, what I *know* you saw yesterday. What do you think happens to people who speak out against the war, like my cousin had? You think it matters if they are in the royal family? You know what? You are right, I’m lucky, at least I’m still alive. My uncle can’t return home anymore, because he sided with a traitor. My little sister, she’s been *broken* for years, my father favors her, and despite where I am, I’d say being favored is worse. My nation is imploding and the world has no idea” –Zuko kept their eye contact as he spoke– “And you aren’t the only one who’s lost a mother.”

Sokka stayed quiet, blue eyes narrowing and widening back and forth, probably trying to figure out if what he was saying were lies. He didn’t yell back again, but still looked like he might do something stupid like throw him overboard, and Zuko never had much self preservation so he spoke again.

“What do you want me to say Sokka? Do you want to hear that I want my country to lose this war? Do you want to hear that I want my father dead? What exactly is it that you want?”

And Zuko could tell Sokka decided that those questions weren’t lies, because his shoulders relaxed and he finally ate his last few bites of fish before speaking.

“I want you to hand me the oars so I can start rowing us southeast. I have friends at the Northern Air Temple, they’ll help us.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

It was well into the night before either of them spoke again. It was Sokka who started the conversation.

“How long were you on Zhao’s ship?”

Zuko didn’t want to talk about this, he didn’t want to think about his time on the ship, about being tortured, not even for information, just because Zhao enjoyed having power over him, enjoyed seeing him afraid. He didn’t want to think about burning hands holding him down as he screamed. He didn’t want to think about laying on the metal floor in a pool of his own blood. He didn’t want to think in general. Because thinking meant being back there.

“A few weeks? I wasn’t captured by him.”

“You wanna explain how?”

“No.”

“Okay…” Sokka let out a sigh, clearly annoyed, but Zuko decided to ignore it. “Look, I’m sorry about before, I’m just– it’s hard to wrap my head around. I mean you’re the Fire Lord’s son, he’s like the most evil guy on the planet, but you’re… well you aren’t what I expected. I shouldn’t have gotten mad at you, it’s not your fault who your dad is.”

“I’m not mad about earlier, Sokka. I just don’t want to talk about what happened, I wasn’t even mad then, you just seem like you wanted to argue and I was gonna let you. You have every right to hate my dad, I do. After what I did at Wolf’s Cove, I wouldn’t blame you if you hated me too.”

“I don’t hate you…” Sokka muttered, taking the rows out of Zuko’s hands to take over. “Do you hate me?”

“No.”

“Okay. Good. That's Good.”

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

So like it's a little heavy... but like not that bad...

&#45; Niko ٩(◦&#96;꒳´◦)۶

## Chapter 9

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

hey friends, quick note before the chapter

I couldn't find if the mechanist had a name and I didn't really want to just refer to him as "the mechanist" so I went with Tek, still the same character though. Siew is any entirely original character, obviously many of the characters in this story are cause I wanted the same vibe as the show where they meet different people as they travel.

Also finally hit over 30000 words!! my current file is over 50000 though and not complete so we still have a while lol

### Text

They finally saw land as the sun peaked in the sky the next day. It wasn’t much longer before they reached it, and as they neared the shore Zuko noticed people *flying*. Sokka didn’t seem nearly as surprised by the sight, how was he not shocked? They were *flying.*

Zuko had been to the Northern Air Temple before, not long after his banishment, the Air temples had been the first places he searched for the Avatar. There weren’t *flying* people the last time he was here. As he watched the far away people soar through the air he noticed the difference, they weren’t bending, they weren’t airbenders. Of course it would’ve been rather astounding for airbenders to suddenly reappear at the temple, but for a slight moment he had let himself believe that. Zuko hadn’t seen Aang fly more than a few feet before, but there was something different about the way they glided through the air.

By the time they reached the shore there were people there to meet them, an older man stood at the front of the group. He had dark brown hair, what he missed on the top of his head was made up for by facial hair. He looked to Sokka first, his face scrunched up with questions, likely wondering where the rest of Sokka’s group was.

“Sokka my boy, what are you doing here? Not that it isn’t nice to see you, but you were meant to be heading to the North Pole weren’t you?” It was only then that the man really seemed to notice Zuko, “And you brought… a friend,” it sounded more like a question than a statement.

“Things got a bit messy up north, I need to send a message to Katara and Aang to let them know I’m alright.” Sokka jumped out of the boat into the shallow water, Zuko followed the action, the two of them pulling the boat up. “This is Zu–”

“Li. Zuli sir, but just Li is fine,” Zuko cut off, earning a slight glare from Sokka.

“Tek,” the man introduced himself. His nervousness was clear, as was that of those behind him.

“He’s a friend. Guy’s got more wanted posters than I do,” Sokka joked slightly, and somehow the comment actually seemed to calm the group a bit.

Tek nodded, “grab your things, we’ll get you two a place to stay and some food. Teo will be glad to see you.”

—-

Zuko had to stop himself from jumping as a boy landed near where he and Sokka stood, his glider was clipped off his wheelchair –at least that was what he assumed it was, they look different in the Fire Nation and he hadn’t seen any in the Earth Kingdom– before he rolled himself over to them.

“Hey Sokka, didn’t expect you to be back here so soon, where’s Katara and Aang?”

“Back in Agna Qel’a, we got split up in the battle.”

The boy just nodded, not responding to Sokka as his focus was now on Zuko, “so you’re the Fire Nation guy Milo mentioned.”

“We’ve barely been here ten minutes…” Zuko commented, surprised that somehow he’d heard he was here already. “You can just call me Li.”

“Alright Li, I’m Teo. So how’d you meet Sokka?”

“He was captured.”

“By you?” Zuko held back a laugh as Teo gave a look that clearly meant he doubted Sokka was capable of that.

“No! By the Fire Nation.”

“But he’s…” Teo just sort of gestured to Zuko to make his point.

“Yeah, I know. They’re not exactly fans of him,” Sokka joked slightly.

“Well any enemy of the Fire Nation is a friend of mine. Come on, I’ll show you guys to your room.”

Zuko decided not to go into that he wasn’t actually an enemy to his nation, it was probably best just to go along until he and Sokka went their separate ways. It wasn’t long before they reached the room the two would be staying in.

“Figured you wouldn’t mind sharing, plus we don’t really have the room otherwise,” Teo looked between the two of them, pursing his lips slightly before he dropped his thinking face a moment later, “I’ll leave you two to get sorted, see you both at dinner.”

“Any idea what that face was about?”

“What face?” Sokka questioned, apparently having not noticed Teo staring at them like he was trying to solve a puzzle even though he’d definitely been looking at him.

“Nevermind.”

—-

Zuko was laying on his cot, not far from Sokka who, unlike Zuko, had decided to partially unpack, not that he really had anything that was actually his to unpack. His jacket was thrown on his cot along with boomerang. He’d gone through the medical pack to see what they had left, he should probably ask Tek if they had someone to check Zuko over, considering the stubborn idiot probably wouldn’t ask himself. At the very least he needed to have his bandages changed. Sokka looked at Zuko, his eyes were closed, but his face was still tense, so he was likely still awake.

“You should get their medic to treat your wounds.”

“I’m fine,” Zuko didn’t even bother to open his eyes when he spoke.

“You are pretty far from *fine* Zuko.”

“I don’t need someone to treat them, I can do it myself.”

“You’ve got wounds up and down your back and burns that haven’t been treated properly, just let them check you out.”

“No.”

Sokka couldn’t understand why he was being so stubborn, they were safe right now, he could get treated. Did he just not trust them? Why? Sokka somehow doubted it was because of some issue with them not being Fire Nation. Was it just because he didn’t know them? Zuko trusted him though, well maybe not *trusted,* but he doesn’t hate him, and Sokka was pretty sure escaping the Fire Nation Naval ship together should give him some points.

“Would you do it if I went with you?”

“Why would that make a difference?” Zuko’s tone was still dry and uninterested, but he’d opened his eyes now, glancing at Sokka from where he laid on the cot, his eyes looked heavy, like he may fall asleep any moment.

“Because you don’t trust them.”

Golden eyes narrowed into a glare at him, much more awake and he looked *pissed,* like Sokka had called him a blubbering hog-monkey. Zuko rose from the bed after a moment and Sokka was almost certain he was gonna get punched. Of course he didn’t miss how Zuko winced as he moved.

“Fine. Let's get this over with.”

Sokka was aware of what he was doing, at least somewhat. He couldn’t stop himself, it was a good distraction, a way to keep him from thinking about his failed mission, that he’d gotten two people killed. Both Imasu and Aoto left family behind, more family than Sokka has, more people would miss them than him and yet, he’s the one still here. He shouldn’t have let them split up, he knew it was a bad idea, that they’d be safer together. They trusted him and he failed. He didn’t even know what happened to Agna Qel’a, if their failure, his failure, meant more people died. Did he get more people killed? Zuko was an easy distraction, he was injured and needed someone to make sure he wasn’t an absolute idiot about getting treatment. He was also the first guy around his age that didn’t seem to have a *thing* for his sister, though maybe that wouldn’t be the case if Katara was actually here. Something about that thought made his stomach knot up, probably because he’s the Fire Nation’s Prince, though Sokka found that he didn’t feel protectiveness at the thought, as much as something confusing that he couldn’t quite decipher.

Soon they happened upon someone to ask directions from, Sokka did that talking as they looked at Zuko like he might explode at any moment. He found anger bubbling in him at the reaction, they didn’t even know him, Sokka actually had reasons not to like him and yet…

“Hello?” Sokka called into the room as he knocked on the frame of the doorway.

The woman who’d been attending to a boy probably a few years younger than himself turned to the doorway. She looked between the two of them, before after a moment her eyes softened, “Come on in boys.”

The kid was asleep, with a damp cloth on his forehead, probably running a fever. The medic had them sit a few beds away while she went to put a few things away in a stormroom off the main room they were in. Soon she was back at their set of beds.

“Alright, so what seems to be the problem?” She seemed to be more focused on Zuko, though considering he looked a bit like death that could be expected, Sokka didn’t want to assume it was out of fear.

Of course the stubborn jerk didn’t respond, golden eyes flickering to Sokka as if he expected him to answer. Sokka held his stare for a long moment before letting out an exasperated sigh. Fine, but he wasn’t going to sure coat the issues then.

“We were captive on a Fire Navy ship,” Sokka started, though the woman didn’t look shocked, Tek or someone else had probably already told her, because she hadn’t seemed surprised that they showed up in the first place.

“Tek filled me in, I was planning on coming to see you both after I was done here. If I remember correctly, your name is Sokka, you were with the Avatar and your sister, Katara last time you were here.” She continued before Sokka spoke again, “I assume you met aboard the ship. If you could let me know what happened,” she urged. Her eyes went to Sokka while she spoke now, making Sokka relax a bit, as she didn’t seem bothered by Zuko being Fire Nation.

“I’m *fine*,” –read: I’m not going to talk about my trauma because I’m bad at feelings– “I was only there a few hours before something caused the ship to take on water and the two of us escaped. Zu*li* had been there for weeks though, his back is covered in wounds, and he’d got a pretty nasty burn going around his side. We did the best we could on the lifeboat but there wasn’t much to work with and they hadn’t looked to be treated while he was there,” Sokka had left out the word he was thinking of using, *tortured*. Zuko had been tortured, by his own people. *People* was probably incorrect, as it was likely mainly a singular person, but as every person on that boat let it happen, Sokka was going to include them in his disgust.

“And your hands?” She directed her question to Zuko.

“Burned them when we were escaping,” Zuko filled in, his voice quieter than usual, more stilted.

“Mmh,” she nodded, “I’ll need you to take off your shirt so I can see the wounds.”

Sokka nodded as a sort of encouragement, they couldn’t let him stay barely treated. Golden eyes glowered at him for a long moment before he pulled off his shirt, wincing as the action pulled on his wounds, but it was done before either Sokka or the medic could try to help. His torso was still covered in bandages, and around an injury on his upper arm. Small amounts of blood had seeped through, Sokka was glad to see it hadn’t been as much as he feared, especially with the strain of having to row the lifeboat.

Siew, they learned her name as she’d spoken about a million different things while treating Zuko’s wounds, seeming to sense that the both of them needed to be distracted. She only brought the focus back to what she was doing when she got to the burns on his arm and upper back.

“Are there burns like this anywhere else?” And Sokka couldn’t figure out how she managed to keep her voice calm, because he could see the anger in her eyes, and he was glad Zuko was turned away from her because he’d probably hate the look of pity that seemed to be hidden with it.

“No,” he answered simply. Siew didn’t question him, but Sokka nearly did as the answer seemed too fast, and Zuko offered different information almost immediately. “I– my ankles were chained, they have the same wounds as my wrists,” his voice was quiet, so much so that Sokka had struggled to hear what he’d said. Siew didn’t seem to have a problem figuring out what he said though, she was closer.

“I’ll take a look at them in a moment,” she spoke, her voice still just as calm.

By the time they were nearly done in the medic’s room it was apparently time for food, as Teo ended up coming to find them.

“Hi, Siew!” Teo greeted as he came into the room, earning a slight glare and a meaningful look at the still sleeping boy a few beds from them. “Sorry,” he whispered. His gaze went to Siew, who was currently finishing wrapping Zuko’s ankles. It was for the best that he already had his shirt back on, considering Teo was similar to Aang in that he’d probably ask too many questions.

“If they’re okay to go, food’s ready,” the boy explained.

“Yes, they’re fine to go” –she turned to Zuko– “if you need anything please stop in, okay?”

Zuko didn’t seem to notice the way she slightly deflated when his only response was a nod, to be fair he hadn’t really talked much, beyond answering what he had to, Sokka had engaged a bit more through out, even talking about Katara’s healing abilities which Siew found very interesting, or at least acted like she did. Siew brightened though as Zuko mumbled a quiet ‘thank you’ that even Sokka barely could hear.

As they left the infirmary Teo started talking about what had been going on since Sokka was last here, he found himself barely listening, instead watching Zuko, who obviously wasn’t listening either, but every once in a while turned toward them slightly, as if he was at least trying to. Sokka managed to catch his eye as he looked over; subtly motioning for him to move to the other side of Teo, next to him so he didn’t have to keep turning to them to figure out what was going on. Zuko did end up moving, earning a slightly confused glance from Teo, but it was only a moment before a look of realisation flashed in his eyes and he kept talking.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Neither of them were long for the world after dinner, but Sokka still had to give his letter to Tek to send to Aang and Katara. He hadn’t asked Zuko to come along, but it didn’t seem he was planning on walking back to their room on his own, and Sokka didn’t mind the company, frankly he *really* didn’t want to be alone right now.

Tek was squirreled away in his workshop, now working on weapons for the Earth Kingdom military. Sokka knocked on the door, walking in when the man called out.

“You get food?”

“I’ll get some in a bit,” Tek wiped grease from his hand on his apron as he moved from his work bench. “Did you bring your letter?” Holding out his now somewhat clean hand for Sokka’s letter, which he quickly pulled from where it was folded in his tunic.

“Yeah, thanks for sending it for me.”

“Of course. How bout you Li, anyone you want to contact.”

“No.” There was a slight pause before Zuko seemed to decide the one word answer may seem rude, “thank you for the offer.” Sokka couldn’t help but notice the way Zuko had to stop himself from giving a polite bow in the moment. For someone who clearly had problems with authority he seemed rather formal in the situation with someone who could be considered an authority.

“Sorry we couldn’t get you your own rooms, but you are welcome here to stay for as long as you please.”

“I’m planning on heading to Omashu, it's far, but Aang will probably want to meet up with King Bumi.”

“When you’re ready to head out I’ll get you some supplies. I should get back to this, you boys have a good night.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“Omashu might not be safe to go to anymore,” Zuko commented as they got to their room. Sokka just waited for him to explain, sitting down on his cot while he waited for Zuko to get the memo. “I mentioned it, back on the ship. There’s a fight for Omashu.”

“That’s where Aang and Katara are going, I already said I would in my letter.”

“For all we know Omashu has already been taken over.”

“I’m still going.”

“Fine, you’re an idiot though.”

*Well you’re one to talk,* Sokka nearly commented. Of all the people to tell him something he’s doing is stupid, the guy thought it was a good idea to stowaway on a Fire Navy ship! It's not like Sokka was expecting him to come, despite what he’d said in the cell, they aren’t exactly friends, they are barely able to tolerate each other. Why does Zuko care so much anyway?

“Well you don’t have to come with me!” Sokka found himself biting out.

“I’m not leaving you to run to your possible death on your own, not after your father defended me. Anyways, I might as well, hopefully I can find out if Sensu is okay.”

“My dad did what,” Sokka questioned, his eyes wide.

“At the Earth Kingdom camp. General Fong wasn’t exactly the biggest fan having me there, frankly he looked a bit like he’d kill me given half a chance, probably should be glad Sensu didn’t leave my side while I was there” –Zuko shook his head a bit, probably trying to make sure he stayed on topic– “Not the point, Fong made this whole scene, yelling about me being there. I’d been talking with your dad and Bato before it happened. They tried to get him to let me stay until my burn, the one around my side, healed. They couldn’t get him to let me stay, but Fong was going to keep my stuff, including my swords, and they convinced him not to do that, so it’s the least I can do.”

“So you are going to travel across the Earth Kingdom with me, possibly into a battle at best, or a Fire Nation occupied Omashu at worst, because my dad and Bato made sure you kept your swords?”

Zuko had called him an idiot. *Him!* When the idiotic ex-prince was planning on tagging along across the Earth Kingdom, all because of a damn sword. What kind of logic is that? Sokka knew his dad wouldn’t expect Zuko to do this, if anything he’d probably hope both of them would stay out of the line of fire, but that isn’t possible now.

“They’re the last thing I have from home.”

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

hope you enjoyed the chapter. Sokka's letter will hopefully be sent off soon, though how long it will take to get to Katara after such devastation at the North Pole, we'll see.

as always comment, kudos, etc. are awesome, love to see what you guys are thinking about the updates.

until next time (probably Wednesday lol)

## Chapter 10

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

the fact that it is Wednesday is nuts to me... I've been working on some animation practice this week as well as my actual work stuff and time just isn't on my side.

anyways, no big notes for this chapter, its mostly just a nice kinda fluffy chapter, a bit longer than the norm so that's cool.

### Text

The two of them ended up deciding to head out only a few days later, after they got some decent rest. Tek and Teo sent them off with plenty of provisions to last the two of them a while. Siew had given them medical supplies and instructions on caring for Zuko’s still healing injuries, she wasn’t too happy that they were traveling so soon, but neither of them were willing to just sit around, it was going to be a long trek to Omashu.

The two traveled in a semi-content silence. Zuko didn’t seem at all bothered by their quiet walk as they carefully made their way through the mountains that extend well beyond the Northern Air Temple. Sokka, while *fine* with the silence, was also pretty sure he was gonna lose his mind. He’d never realised just how much noise his usual group made, not just with talking, but Momo’s chattering, the wind passing Appa as this flew through the air, the swishing of Katara’s waterbending as she practiced, Aang’s marble trick, something he did to entertain himself while guiding Appa. There was always some kind of noise. Zuko was different, he moved in near complete silence, even on the uneven terrain his steps were nonexistent. Even his breathing was quiet, despite the exertion from climbing down mountains while injured. He expected that Zuko would be more boisterous, that his presence alone would be deafening, but he was nothing like what Sokka thought. It was still early into their trip, and Sokka knew Zuko could be loud, he knew his presence could be demanding and aggressive if he so chose, he had experienced that version of the firebender. It seemed what Sokka had seen when he first met Zuko wasn’t him, unless he’d truly changed that much in a few months. The teen who’d come to his home demanding the Avatar was what he thought a prince may be like, brash and proud, full of himself, condescending. Sokka wasn’t sure much of that fit the Zuko he watched carefully descending the mountains.

“We don’t want to have to go around Ba Sing Se, it will take too long, if we continue directly south and look for a port village we might be able to get passage across,” Zuko suggested, breaking the silence after hours.

“You know more than I do, Aang did most of the flying and riding on Appa makes things seem a lot smaller. Whatever gets us to Omashu quickest would be my vote.” Sokka found he didn’t mind letting Zuko decide their path, it wasn’t wrong to say he knew more about traveling on land, looking at a map can only tell you so much, and his idea of how long things take is a bit skewed with Appa travel being his main for the past few months.

Zuko just nodded, continuing his silence once again.

They continued on for a few more hours when the silence was once again broken, this time by Sokka’s stomach. The sun was high in the sky, luckily it wasn’t very warm considering it was still winter. Zuko didn't react to the noise coming from Sokka, and he wasn’t exactly sure how to bring up stopping. It was another several minutes before Zuko stopped, they stood in a low valley, mountains surrounding them on all sides.

“We’re stopping?” Sokka questioned allowed.

“You’re hungry,” Zukko reasoned as he pulled off his large pack.

—-

Zuko plopped down next to his pack, legs crossed. He started pulling a few fruits that Tek had insisted they take, it would be best to start getting through them before too long. He soon noticed that Sokka’s gaze hadn’t left him, he could feel the boy’s eyes on him. He tensed slightly, not sure why he was being stared at. He looked to Sokka, eyes narrowed into a glare, trying to tell him to stop. Sokka seemed to understand the silent demand as he evaded his eyes, pulling off his pack before sitting down.

With the other’s gaze off of him he started slicing up the fruit he’d taken from the bag. He made quick work of the slicing, only at the end realising that once again blue eyes were on him, or more specifically his hands, he didn’t glare his time, letting the gaze linger as he split up the fruit between two plates and added some dried fish.

“We shouldn’t spend too long out here, we’ll cook something when we find somewhere to stop for the night.”

Zuko didn’t go further into why he thought they should keep moving, instead just handing Sokka a plate of food. He had a couple reasons he thought they shouldn’t stop for too long, mostly he just wanted to make sure they traveled as much as they could each day, but beyond that, mountains make good cover for ambushes. Zuko knew they didn’t have much, but anyone could recognize them from a wanted poster, and even if they didn’t neither of them looked Earth, and sometimes even that might be enough for people to fuck with them. It would be for the best to keep moving, finding a cave to hide out in for the night would be ideal, he was sort of hoping to stumble upon one instead of wasting time looking.

“You know, I could’ve cut my own fruit,” Sokka commented, though he didn’t actually seem bothered by Zuko having done so, proven by him following the comment with a “thank you.”

He only gave a hum in response before beginning in on his own food. Zuko hadn’t actually thought much about the fact that Sokka was perfectly capable of cutting some fruit, or even that cutting plum-apples and dragon-oranges wasn’t strictly necessary to begin with, he’d just done it because it seemed like the right thing to do. It had been something his mother would often do, even though she could order the cooks to make anything she wanted. She always seemed to appear with some fruit whenever Zuko was having a particularly rough time, she’d sit by him in a comfortable silence while she prepared it, and then they’d share the food. Sometimes, if he was up to it, they’d talk after, but oftentimes it was a mostly silent gesture. It never failed to make Zuko feel better, and years later when traveling with his uncle, the man would do a similar thing, just with tea instead. Both were simple acts to show they cared, even if he hadn’t always understood that at the time. Zuko couldn’t explain when he’d felt it was necessary to cut Sokka’s fruit for him, he couldn’t really claim he *cares* about the Water Tribe boy. All the same, it seemed Sokka appreciated it, even with his comment.

They ate a companionable silence; though he could tell that Sokka was not naturally a quiet person. The boy couldn’t seem to stay still, whether that meant tapping his foot, his knee bouncing, or just the way he’d fix his wolf tail even when nothing was out of place. Occasionally Zuko would catch him opening his mouth, as though he was about to speak, but then he’d close it again, going back to his food. It was the fifth time he’d done it when Zuko finally decided to say something.

“You don’t have to be quiet.”

“Oh thank Tui,” Sokka speaks with a relieved sigh.

Zuko's face pinched in confusion, trying to figure out why Sokka felt he couldn’t speak. Had he said something? Maybe did something that made it seem like he didn’t want Sokka to speak?

“You’re quieter than I expected, so I just figured… I mean I know we’ve talked some, but I kind of expected you to be– I don’t know– a little more demanding? After I found out– well I thought you’d be like when you first showed up in Wolf’s Cove.”

Sokka’s explanation didn’t help. He kind of figured that was an easy persona to see through, it definitely fell apart pretty quickly. Even his crew figured out he wasn’t what he pretended, that Zuko wasn’t the prince anyone expected, or *wanted*. He realised after a long pause that he should probably say something, as to not contradict his previous encouragement to speak.

“I was desperate, angry, and also very much trying to elicit fear, ” Zuko admits as he takes Sokka’s empty plate and wipes it before wrapping both of their plates and putting them back in his bag. He’s had a lot of time to think about how he’s changed since he left his crew, who he was in the three years on that ship, and how different that person was to the prince he’d been before. In any case he was pretty sure they’d had a similar conversation to this before, that he was *different*, Sokka has been one of the few people whose said that to him as something not definitively bad.

“Still, you’re a prince,” Sokka reasoned.

“Are you not?” He’d wondered before, if Sokka was considered a prince. He knew the Northern Water Tribe had a princess, but he was uncertain if that carried over to their sister tribe.

“Not in the same way you are. I’ve made the joke before, but that’s not really a thing in the South Pole. My father is the chief, but he isn’t the only one who holds that title. The South Pole is split up into multiple smaller tribes, all of them have their own chief, the council of them decides who acts as the Chief of the Southern Watertribe. While it is likely I’ll become chief of Wolf Cove, it doesn’t mean I’ll be the Chief.”

“They each run independently then?”

“Sort of, but we help each other out, when in battle my father is the one in command, and there's a council held frequently for joint decisions. We’ve been more separated since the warriors left*.*”

As they returned to walking, Sokka continued to explain the workings of the different tribes, how they worked together. And Zuko listened intently, not saying much beyond what he needed to urge Sokka to continue. At some point he started talking about how their military works, which was vastly different from either the Earth Kingdom or Fire Nation, both of which he was more familiar with. He also mentioned their elders, speaking of his Grangran for a while in relation, how they also had their own council apart from the Chiefs and were the ones currently in control of the tribes while their warriors were away. Zuko realised much later in the conversation that Grangran was very likely the woman he’d used as an example for someone who would be Aang’s age, as Sokka didn’t bring it up, Zuko decided not to as well.

—-

Zuko would not call himself lucky, mostly because he didn’t much believe in luck, but also that he’d been told he wasn’t for as long as he could remember. Whether it was that he isn’t actually that unlucky, or that Sokka maybe is just very lucky, though somehow Zuko doubts that, they do actually stumble upon a place to stay. Maybe unluckiness just cancels out, yeah maybe that was it, or this was all gonna go down hill fast. Either way the sun was nearly gone from the sky when they reached a small mountain town, houses built into the clift sides, most made of earth, nearly blending in with the mountains. There’s no sign of Fire Nation in the town, which makes sense considering they’re likely still too far northeast for that to be much of a problem. Likewise there doesn’t seem to be Earth Kingdom soldiers guarding it either. Zuko knows that this area isn’t safe from the war, but it isn’t a target either, given, probably because people don’t know of its existence. Of course a main reason the Fire Nation isn’t in the area is Aang, Katara and Sokka’s last visit to the Northern Air Temple. After hearing the story from Sokka and Teo at dinner the night before he wasn’t too surprised about the lack of Fire Nation in the mountains surrounding the Northern Air Temple.

“Do we even have money to pay for a place?”

“Well I took mine back when I was getting the rest of our stuff,” he decides not to mention that he also swiped a *healthy* amount of Zhao’s, it’s Fire Nation currency though, so it wouldn’t be useful unless they were closer to the colonies.

“I don’t think they’ll accept—“ Sokka doesn’t finish his sentence as Zuko opens the pouch in his hand to show that he has Earth Kingdom currency; what he stole from Zhao hidden away at the bottom of his pack instead of in the pouch.

“I have been living in the Earth Kingdom for months and was in it plenty in the last three years,” he comments matter of factly.

“I guess we see if they have an inn then.”

The pair reach the entrance to the lower part of the town in a few minutes, finding that for nighttime there seems to be a decent few people outside. The streets are lit with lanterns and there’s music playing somewhere in the distance. Zuko had expected there to be very few people in town but he couldn’t be more wrong. The place seemed to be bustling with people. The closer they got to the music the more recognisable the song became, it was something he used to hear his mother hum when they sat together in the gardens.

As the two of them reached when a festival seemed to be happening it was then that they got a few strange looks, well more specifically, Sokka did. He was dressed in his Watertribe attire, while they’d been given some clothes at the Northern Air Temple Sokka understandably preferred his own, even if it made him stand out more than they would otherwise. No one had said anything but Sokka squirmed a bit under their gaze.

Zuko after searching through those looking at them found that most people just looked surprised rather than suspicious. He grabbed Sokka’s wrist pulling him along toward a woman who looked about old enough to be either of theirs mom, and seemed to while yes looking at them, also keeping an eye on a group of children that were running around nearby, her own likely among them.

“Hi,” he started, keeping enough distance that him and Sokka weren’t threateningly close, “we’re looking for a place to stay,” as Zuko spoke he got a better look at the woman, he hair was dark brown, her skin closer in shade to Sokka’s than was common in the Earth Kingdom, and most notably, her eyes were a sort of blue-ish green.

“There’s not an inn, bit too far in the mountains for that, but I’ve got a spare room, as long as you two don’t mind my kids asking you a million questions about your travels you can have it,” she offers as a man walks up to the group of them, a giggling child on his shoulders.

“We having company tonight?” he questions, as he places the child down, silently gesturing for them to go play with the others nearby. Clearly this isn’t the first time they’ve put up people for the night. The man’s about the same height as the woman next to him, he looks earth but Zuko’s not seen many people with grey eyes in the Earth Kingdom, to be fair it isn’t common in general, the only person he knows with them personally is Ty Lee. It’s something unspoken, especially when it comes to Ty Lee, but grey eyes were common among the Air Nomads. Zuko knows it's uncommon knowledge, or more correctly, commonly ignored knowledge, for the best, all things considered, and he isn’t about to voice the thought.

“These two just got into town, they were asking if there was an inn, told them they could stay with us, hope that’s alright?” She didn’t actually sound worried about the answer, further confirming his assumption that they had done this before.

“Of course, we’re only down the road, you two can put your things down and then join the festivities,” he gestured for them to follow him.

——

Sokka didn’t miss the fact that the woman Zuko had chosen to talk to was likely Wate Ttribe, at least partly so. He debated asking the woman which tribe she was from, but before he got the chance Zuko and him were following her husband to their house. He didn’t mind too much, he was sure he’d get the chance to speak with her later. The man hadn’t been lying about how close their home was, the music hadn’t really faded in the few minutes it took to walk there, and many people were on the street where it was. Walking inside it seemed the bottom floor of their home was actually a shop, they were led up a staircase in the back to get to their actual home.

“Pretty rare that we get visitors, you two came in from the north side of town right? Only place up that way is the Northern Air Temple,” he made the comment in a way to give them the chance to share if they wanted to.

“We were up at the North Pole until recently,” Sokka offered for an explanation.

“Both of you?” He questioned, gazing back at Zuko for a moment.

He decided it was for the best not to go through even just the basic explanation of them being captured, they would only be here for a night anyway. Sokka wasn’t sure how the town would react if they knew the two of them were wanted by the Fire Nation, and he wasn’t about to risk the two of them getting run out of town when the sun had started to dip below the horizon.

“Yup, Zu*li* is decidedly not one for the cold,” he joked, just barely avoiding saying Zuko’s actual name.

“I would assume not.” He opens a door to one of the rooms, “you can put your things in here.”

Sokka was grateful the man didn’t ask anymore questions, and despite a slightly curious look at what probably seemed like an odd pair –Sokka would argue that the man and his wife were an odd pair too– he didn’t question them further.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

It wasn’t long before they were back to the festival, Zuko was looking over the town centre, noticing the interesting blend of nations in the small town. Suddenly Zuko was being dragged by Sokka, because the other boy saw a sign for stewed sea prunes. Zuko lets himself be pulled along, not really minding what they do now that they’re in a relatively safe place, though he kept his dao blades sheathed at his hip.

“You have to try them, Zu*li.”* He was really gonna have to get better with calling him Li, not that Zuko really minded being called Zuli, but it definitely didn’t help to hide that he’s Fire Nation. Soon they arrived at the food stall, which also sold imported seal jerky. He pulled out his coin pouch, handing it to Sokka to pull what he needed from it. They weren’t exactly flush with money, but seal jerky would keep for a while so Zuko didn’t mind the purchase. Sokka buys a serving of sea prunes along with a few bags of seal jerky for the road.

The two find a bench nearby to sit at. Zuko’s eyes are on the band playing on a small stage at the center of the festival, it was the same band that had been playing before but now they played a tune he’d heard a few times throughout the Earth Kingdom. Feeling something hit his shoulder he turns to Sokka, who’s holding out a spoon with a stewed sea prune on it.

“Come on, try it,” Sokka urges, offering the spoon to Zuko.

Zuko has never been very picky with what he eats, well really he was just never given the option, despite being a prince. He took the offered spoon without much need for debate, trying the food. It was salty, which isn’t exactly uncommon for Watertribe food, at least the bit that he’s had before. Despite the saltiness they taste a bit like ocean kumquats, something Zuko has eaten quite a lot of, being on a ship for a few years.

“They’re a bit like ocean kumquats, saltier though,” Zuko comments.

“What’s an ocean kumquat?”

“A fruit... It’s common on naval ships because they keep for a while, but they’re used in a lot of foods.”

Sokka just hums in response as he’s started in on his food. Zuko goes back to watching the festivities, looking over the booths for something to eat, since he really should probably eat. He figures Sokka will probably want to try something else as well so he defaults to waiting so he can decide.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“Are you boys enjoying the festival? It was a great time for you to stumble upon us here,” the woman from earlier commented when they happened upon her again, this time hand in hand with the same child her husband had been holding earlier.

Sokka was currently trying to breathe through the spice from the curry Zuko had offered him a bite of a few moments before. Ironically it had been Sokka who pointed out the booth that advertised having Fire Nation foods, he hadn’t thought the noodles he’d gotten were too spicy but Zuko’s order was a whole other thing, did firebenders not taste spice or something? He tries to nod in response to the question, but his gaze turns back to the firebender as he hears him stifle a laugh. Zuko assures her through half laughs that they are having a good time.

“It’s not funny, Zu*li!”* Sokka exclaims, once again nearly sliping up in calling Zuko by his name.

“Yuda always laughs at me too, when I tell him his food is too spicy for me,” she comments with a bright laugh.

After a few long moments Sokka doesn’t feel so much like his mouth is burning anymore, though he is vaguely bitter watching Zuko continue to eat the curry without any problems. Luckily he ends up distracted by conversation.

“We never properly introduced ourselves, Yuda is my husband, you met him earlier. This is Asmara, our youngest.” –She gestures to the child who’s probably no older than three, quietly snacking on what looks to be melon– “We’ve got three others running around here with their friends, you’ll probably meet them later. And I’m Kanae. It is rather rare that we get visitors out here, at least nowadays, even more so someone from one of the Water Tribes.”

“Sokka, I’m from the Southern Watertribe, but was up North for a little while.”

“Long way from home, did you go north to train?”

“It was for my sister to train actually, I was just being a good big brother and tagging along, she’s still up there.” Sokka decided it best to leave out the fact that the Avatar was included in that trip.

“A long way to train, though I’ve heard there aren’t very many waterbenders in the south anymore.”

Sokka shook his head a bit solemnly, a shaky breath leaving her lips, “my sister’s the only one.” He didn’t miss Zuko watching him as he spoke, though Sokka found he couldn’t bring himself to look at the firebender at that moment.

“My mother was from the North, a waterbender, though she was never adept at healing, she left when she was still rather young, met my father, an earthbender.”

“Katara’s pretty great at healing, but she’s more of a fighter too, she’s training under Master Paku now.”

“They’re allowing women to fight now?”

“Not until a few weeks ago when Katara challenged him to a fight because he wouldn’t train her,” Sokka spoke with a bright pride in his younger sister. He’d thought that the whole thing was a bit nuts when it happened, but he wouldn’t deny that she got results and that she absolutely deserved to train.

“I’m glad you sound proud.”

“Traveling has done me good,” Sokka admits, remembering his reaction to the Kyoshi warriors only a few months ago.

Kanae nods in agreement, seeming to get his meaning. “And you were both up north?” She directs her attention to both of them rather than mostly Sokka now.

Sokka glances at Zuko, unsure of what sort of story the two are going with, since him being in the North doesn’t exactly make sense. Luckily he doesn’t have to answer, the firebender taking over the explanation.

“We met further south, near Gaipan. I was traveling with my uncle to Ba Sing Se. He's likely there by now, but I’d rather travel than hide myself behind some walls. Sokka mentioned they were headed north and I figured it would be my only chance to see Agna Qel’a.” Zuko’s lie was smooth, referencing the time they’d seen eachother in the forest, when the firebender was escaping captivity a different time.

After a bit more conversation Yuda appeared with three kids, the oldest of them, probably twelve, had another kid, younger than them on their back asleep, while a kid who could be their twin was standing next to Yuda clearly trying to act as though they weren't tired.

“Looks like we should be heading back,” Kanae laughs lightly, “you two enjoy your night, just let yourselves in when you come back. There’s a key in the pot by the door.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

It was well long after they’d arrived in town, to when they were finally in their room for the night, successfully managing to get there without waking anyone. One thing that neither of them had really planned for was that the spare room offered only had one bed. When finding that out earlier Zuko had just planned to use his sleeping bag, but as he went to pull it out Sokka was giving him a look like he was crazy.

“What?”

“Just sleep on the bed idiot,” Sokka spoke in a somewhat bewildered tone.

Zuko shuffles on his feet slightly, eyes narrowing at Sokka in confusion. He hadn’t slept in the same bed as someone else… in a while. Not with any consistency since he was a kid, before he and Azula were forced apart by their father’s need to pit them against each other, but when they actually relied on each other.

“You’re sure?”

“Lay down, it's cold anyways.”

Sokka was already tucked under the covers, not seeming at all bothered by having someone he considered an enemy a few days ago sleeping next to him. Zuko found that it really wasn’t much of a concern to him either, he’d slept in the boat, it wasn’t as though this was much different. So he soon followed Sokka’s instruction, climbing under the blankets next to him. There was an awkward while where the two just sort of laid there, having not entirely thought through how’d they actually feel being this close. Eventually they both got more comfortable, Sokka first as he pretty quickly passed out, light snoring coming from him as Zuko stared up at the ceiling. He was never good about falling asleep quickly, it took a while unless he wore himself out, usually he’d travel until he was ready to pass out, but traveling with someone else he couldn’t do that.

Zuko found himself awake for a while after, thinking about the oddly good evening he’d had with Sokka. The town was pretty amazing, a clear blend of the different nations, music throughout the night had been a mix of songs he’d heard traveling and ones from home. There were so many different foods, and he’d finally gotten to eat something that reminded him of home. After so long it had been hard to remember what he actually missed from the Fire Nation, the bad memories often overtaking the good. So much of what he missed from home didn’t exist anymore, not without his mother, uncle, or cousin there to keep the actual cultural parts of the Fire Nation alive for him, rather than the focus on the militaristic aspect that his father had. Zuko couldn’t find his anger at the moment though, still too buzzed with a rare contentment after their night exploring a place that seemed to show what their world could be.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Sokka felt *warm*, incredibly warm. Something— no, *someone* was on top of him. Luckily the watertribe boy was never quick to wake, so he didn’t jump at the realisation. He slowly remembered the events from the night before, figuring out who the weight was. When he did look down he saw the expected mop of black hair. Zuko’s head was on his chest and limbs splayed over him, like this you wouldn’t think that the firebender was actually slightly taller than him. He could feel the other boy’s steady breaths, still asleep. Sokka, still rather tired decided to lay there a little longer, he’d woken unexpectedly early, the sun wasn’t even up yet.

Gazing up at the ceiling he found himself softly pulling his finger through shaggy hair, not paying much mind to the repetitive motion as his mind wandered. He hoped Katara and Aang would get his letter, he wanted them to know he was fine. Sokka knew what they probably thought right now, him not returning. He heart ached at the idea of Katara believing him dead, they had already lost so much. She has Aang though and she’s strong, she’ll probably be pissed next time they see each other. Sokka cringed at the eventual lecture he’ll get. He wasn’t sure how long it would take for the letter to get to them, with the battle that just happened, and the ships now littering the northern sea, it was anyone's guess. Best thing was to keep heading toward Omashu, hoping that the city was still safe.

A quiet muttering pulled Sokka from his thoughts, quickly removing his hand for the other’s hair, having realised it was still tangled in his strands.

He had golden eyes blinking up at him, slowly until they snapped open, Zuko quickly pushing away from Sokka, nearly tumbling off the bed in the process. He caught himself, his eyes focusing on Sokka again, his shoulders relaxing after a long few moments, like he hadn’t recognised him at first.

“I— sorry…” Zuko mumbles, sitting up fully now.

Sokka just shook his head, not understanding why Zuko apologised, sure it was an odd reaction but it could've been worse, he could’ve shot fire at him in his momentary panic. He kept himself from cringing at that thought, he had to hope Zuko wouldn’t turn his fire on him.

“We should head out soon,” Zuko comments as he walks to his bag, probably going to look for a change of clothes.

“Breakfast first? Probably a good idea to grab something before we had out,” Sokka reasoned, now also going to rummage through his bag.

They did end up getting breakfast, though it was Kanae and Yuda that had made sure they were fed, insisting they eat before leaving. She had been right when she said her kids would ask them a million questions, neither of them mind, well Sokka didn’t think Zuko minded. Luckily the questions were pretty much all about their travels, rather than about either of them personally. Sokka could guess that their parents had probably told them not to ask about certain things. In the end they were sent to continue their travels with what leftovers they could fit in their bags.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes
