## Chapter 11

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Posting a bit earlier because my dad's in town and I don't know if I'll have time tomorrow.

This is another kinda soft chapter, it's a bit shorter than the last one, but I think you'll still enjoy it.

### Text

It was still winter, even if nearing the end of the season, spring only around the corner, the northern Earth Kingdom has quite the weather to contend with, the most concerning being the storms. Dark clouds had been over them for days, and nights had brought downpours, leaving them to navigate muddy terrain as they entered the final valleys of the shorter hills, the coast they are making for wouldn’t be much further off now. A few days and they’d hit the coastal towns, hopefully able to secure passage going further south.

The clouds were beginning to be a concern, despite slick terrain, they’d been lucky that the showers had stayed to the later hours, when they could find cover and wait until morning. Today the clouds were black, the sky dark, casting a shadow over the world. Thunder and lightning rolled across the sky, rumbling and lighting up spaces between clouds as they walked. They hadn’t even stopped for lunch yet, meaning it was likely going to rain before sun down. They had been hoping to fully get out of the mountains by then, on to some flat land, where if it did downpour that wouldn’t have the worry about falling to their death off a slippery cliff. Sitting in a valley wouldn’t be much better though, they’d be standing in water within a few minutes, and they risk mudslides coming down from the mountain surrounding them.

“You think we can make it through before it starts raining?” Sokka questions, his gaze on the gloomy sky above.

Zuko only responded with a shrug, it was anyone's guess how long the rain would hold off. Sokka hummed, debating if they should at the very least, get to higher ground, the valley was still pretty soaked from the rain the night before, it wouldn’t take long to start flooding.

“Let’s keep going,” he decided. Zuko didn’t say anything as he started walking again.

The firebender had been quiet the last few days, since they left the town they’d stayed in. Sokka hadn’t worked up the courage to ask him what was wrong, mostly because he didn’t want the answer he knew would come. He was pretty sure Zuko was uncomfortable now, after waking up on top of him and subsequently freaking out. He hadn’t thought it was a big deal until his traveling companion seemed to close himself off with no vocal explanation and with nothing else Sokka could think of having happened, it was the only reason he could come up with.

He could see miles of plains below from their vantage point up in the mountains. No towns were in sight, only one or two houses, which were probably farms. They likely wouldn’t reach either of them today.

The sky above rumbled, angry cracks through the sky, lightning touching down in the plains with blinding light. He narrowed his eyes at the lack of coverage below. They didn’t have tents, only a tarp that would be useless if the ground was already soaked before they managed to figure out a way to put it up to shield themselves from the weather.

They kept moving as the sky continued to echo booming thunder. There were few trees among the plains, but they’d have to find one suitable to pin their tarp to, to attempt to create a cover, not that it would protect them from lightning.

“We have to stop,” Sokka spoke up after another hour of walking. They were lucky the rain had held off so long but the air was getting colder, damper, it wouldn’t be long now. Zuko just nodded in return, stopping to look over the available places to set up camp for the storm.

“Those trees are close enough that we could probably put the tarp over the lower branches. It’ll keep the rain off hopefully,” Zuko suggested, gesturing to a grouping of trees not too far off. It was the most Zuko had said all day. They couldn’t see the farms in the distance now that they weren’t in the mountains, they’d likely make it to them tomorrow, but for tonight this would have to be enough.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

They managed to set up their makeshift tent just as the rain started. They started unpacking their bedrolls, and what food they had that didn’t need to be cooked since a fire wouldn’t be safe so close to trees or in the small area under the tarp. Zuko was quiet once again, and frankly the lack of conversation was starting to drive Sokka a bit mad. He’d almost preferred when they were arguing, at least they talked then. Sokka wasn’t really sure what caused Zuko’s quiet bouts, as he clearly didn’t have an issue talking, at least he said he didn’t mind, but unless Sokka started a conversation it didn’t seem there would be one. Even when Sokka did speak he was giving either short or silent answers, and if they did end in a conversation, it was heavy. This was not like traveling with Aang and Katara, where either Aang mostly joked around and kept things light, Katara could be annoying with her nagging but was also just as open to goofing around as Aang and himself. Zuko was still very much an unknown, despite how much Sokka had managed to learn about him in their short time traveling together.

He thought it might sound annoying to bring up the lack of conversation again, he’d done so the day before last too. Sokka was a bit worried that this time wasn’t so much about Zuko just naturally not being much for conversation and possibly more about what had happened that morning. Not that anything *had* happened, obviously.

“You’re staring.”

Sokka was yanked from his thoughts, eyes wide looking at Zuko. He’d been staring? *Shit* *he’d been staring*. If Zuko wasn’t put off by him before, he probably was now. Sokka had managed to get himself called out for staring at the ex-prince multiple times now. Why did he keep doing that?

“You alright there, buddy?”

“Uh, yeah– yeah. Sorry I was zoned out. Just worried about Katara and Aang.” He tried to cover, though that wasn’t at all what he was thinking about. Sokka was aware he should probably be the one asking that question though. He was more focused on deciding not to dwell on the fact that being called *buddy* by Zuko kind of stung, beyond just sounding odd coming from him.

“Even if they didn’t have the North Pole defending them, Aang’s the Avatar, I’m sure they’ll be fine. And from personal experience, little sisters are usually pretty ruthless, I’m sure Katara can take care of herself.”

“*Ruthless.* Good way of describing them. Yeah, Katara is pretty strong, she managed to get them to train her even though they don’t usually train girls in the north.” Sokka is entirely for bragging about Katara, even if she can be *really* annoying, he misses her right now, plus it was easier to talk about this then try to figure out what’s wrong with Zuko. Until now the two of them had never been apart for very long, it was weird to not have her right nearby. And even though he was a newer addition to his family, not having Aang around is pretty weird too, but no one needs to brag about the Avatar.

“You’d mentioned something about that to Kanae, yeah?”

Sokka hummed in agreement, “They get healer training usually. Katara is actually very good at healing too, but she wants to learn to fight. The South Pole wasn’t like that, at least if you were a bender. If you aren’t, which besides Katara there aren’t any now, it’s about the same. I’m sure you noticed, there are only men in the Southern Water Tribe Fleet.”

Zuko nodded, “That isn’t really a thing in the Fire Nation. They’ll allow anyone to fight, encourage it really, probably because they want as many people fighting as possible. There is a focus on benders though, a sort of superiority.” Sokka noticed the way the firebender’s face scrunched slightly as though annoyed, “using weapons as a bender is looked down on, there are suspicions about being born in the wrong season, and about how strength is passed down through families. It can get messy. Skill with a weapon if you aren’t a bender is usually respected, but there’s always a sort of… *pity* for non-benders, training with a weapon *helps*.”

Sokka didn’t take offense to the explanation, being that Zuko didn’t seem to agree with any of it, “you use swords,” he prompted him to keep talking, as this was the most they’d spoken in days.

He got a soft hum of agreement before Zuko started to explain, “at first I just learned from my cousin, Lu Ten, I was about nine when I started training with Master Piandao. I didn’t bother asking my father, I knew if I did he’d say no.” Zuko’s hand went to the swords laying sheathed next to him, resting on the weapon as he let out a shaky breath, “he found out from my sister, she saw me sparring with my cousin. He was pissed, but I knew I would get in trouble from the moment I started training, I just didn’t care. About two years into my training my father finally forbade it, it was right after Uncle and Lu Ten left… for Ba Sing Se. I kept training on my own.”

“Didn’t you say your cousin was against the war? He still went to Ba Sing Se?”

“I’m against it and I still searched for the Avatar for three years,” Zuko countered with a sort of weak shrug.

Sokka couldn’t help but stifle a laugh at the comment. “Yeah, alright. Your family sounds way too complicated.”

“It’s even worse than it sounds,” Zuko groans slightly.

“If it helps, the guy who was refusing to train Katara was engaged to grangran, our grandmother.”

Sokka doubted the comment would help, but he was proven at least slightly wrong when Zuko was the one to try to stifle a laugh this time. He looked to Sokka who couldn’t help but smirk at the reaction, only for Zuko to properly laugh now. Sokka found the laugh infectious, the way his head swung back with the laughter, eyes pricked with tears. He didn’t even realise he’d begun laughing too, at something that wasn’t even all that funny to begin with, but Tui and La did it feel good.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Freezing rain crashed down from the heavens, thunder shaking the ground and lightning bathing them in cold bright light every time it struck the plains. The winds tore through the area, Zuko was grateful for the tarp and trees blocking the worst of it. They’d blocked off as much of the world as they could, their bags close, on their bed roll to keep them from the damp ground. Shivering with the harsh weather, the temperature dropped drastically as the sun disappeared below the horizon. He glanced over at Sokka, finding the Water Tribe boy was once again looking at him, he glowered, expecting an explanation to be spoken over the loud winds. Sokka just lifted his blanket slightly as a silent offer, likely noticing he was struggling with the cold. Zuko was still, his mind arguing with him, his stomach in knots he couldn’t explain, because Sokka was trustworthy and he shouldn’t be freaking out like this. *He’s just being nice.*

“I’m getting cold here Zuko, come on,” Sokka urged.

Only now did Zuko notice that the other teen had started shivering as well. He finally joined him under the blanket, layering his own on top of them as well.

“I hate winter,” he grumbled as he tucked his limbs into himself, trying to keep in what heat he could. He was still struggling after weeks away from the sun, being thrown into the mountains of the Northern Earth Kingdom during winter didn’t help, and the storm only worsened the issue. He wasn’t sure how long it would take for his bending or internal temperature to be back to normal, he knew he wasn’t helping himself with long days of traveling in poor conditions, but stopping seemed like it might be worse, like he might just give up.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Zuko wasn’t sure when they’d fallen asleep, but he’d woken up to the same sensation as a few days prior, back in the village. He didn’t open his eyes, at first because he had to remember where he was, who he was with, but then because the feeling of fingers carding through his hair was nice. Zuko wasn’t sure if Sokka realised he was awake, the boy not stopping the repetitive motion. A few minutes later Zuko finally opened his eyes, gold didn’t find blue though as Sokka's eyes were closed. He didn’t end up having to say anything to get Sokka’s attention, the other likely feeling his gaze.

“Your hair’s nice, long…” Sokka mumbled, his eyes blinking as if he was just waking up, maybe he’d almost fallen back asleep.

“It’s getting longer,” Zuko agrees, though his hair isn’t as long as it once was, “I’ll probably let it keep growing,” he wasn’t sure how awake Sokka actually was. He gave him a sleepy smile before turning away slightly to yawn, though his fingers never left his hair.

The quiet moment broke when Sokka’s stomach grumbled, the boy going red at the sudden noise.

“I think that means it’s time for breakfast.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

They didn’t talk about the morning, once again getting on with their travels. Just because they didn’t talk about the morning though, doesn’t mean they didn’t talk. Thankfully something seemed to change, maybe Zuko was less uncomfortable, or maybe he was just starting to heal more from his injuries. That thought reminds Sokka, they should probably make sure the bandages are cleaned next time they get somewhere that would be an option.

“You actually went through the Great Divide?”

“Yes, we nearly got eaten by Canyon Crawlers!”

“Why didn’t you just fly over it on Appa?”

“I wish we would have–” Sokka commented before he began the explanation of what all had happened.

“Sounds like a bad play,” Zuko chuckles.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“You’d really like Suki, she’d probably love to spar with you.”

“Well if I ever end up on Kyoshi island, though considering they almost fed you to the Unagi I’m not sure I want to take my chances,” Zuko half jokes.

“I’ll go with you, I’d like to train with them again.”

“You sure she won’t feed *you* to the Unagi?”

“Suki would never!”

Zuko nodded with a light smirk, making it obvious he didn’t believe Sokka.

“Hey! She *likes* me!”

“Does she?” Zuko teased, ignoring the odd sting he felt at Sokka’s claim.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“The apple was on fire and I thought Mai was gonna end up getting burned. It was so stupid, I tackled her into the fountain to put out the fire!” Zuko exclaimed, groaning slightly at how stupid he’d been.

Sokka let out a great laugh, shaking his head at the embarrassment Zuko still seemed to feel about the whole situation.

“It’s not funny, Sokka” –Zuko tried to claim as he was barely keeping from laughing himself– “Azula and Ty Lee teased us about that for months!”

“You did kind of fall for her trick,” Sokka reasoned, smirking through his laughter.

“It wasn’t just that time, Azula and Ty Lee were convinced we *liked* each other, pulled stuff like that so often.”

Sokka ignored the sting he felt at the mention of Zuko possibly liking someone.

### End Notes

## Chapter 12

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

I'm a bit sick so if you notice more mistakes in my proofing this week sorry. I'll look over it again in a few days. I terrified my mom yesterday cause I collapse in her kitchen but I'm doing alright today so you get the chapter even if it is poorly edited.

See you again at the end of the chapter.

### Text

They ended up traveling far that day, passing by the farms they thought they might stop at and stumbling upon a small town. Zuko didn’t miss the soldiers posted around the town, guarded by Earth Kingdom Military, the first of them they’d seen since traveling together, they were getting closer to the coast.

“We should keep going.”

“It’s getting late Zuko, we’ll get stuck in another storm if we keep going” Clouds rumbled as if to make Sokka’s point for him, “and we need supplies. We don’t know when the next town will be, almost none of the smaller towns are on our map.”

Zuko sighed, eying the town gates nervously, “fine.”

Sokka shrugged, continuing on toward the town, Zuko now trailing slightly behind him. As they neared the gate the soldiers finally took notice of them, the closer they got the more he saw their suspicion. The two posted at the gate they approached looked them over, before they decided who they would talk to, they both focused on Zuko, who was very pointedly not looking at them, his face mostly shrouded by his long hair.

“What’s your business here?”

“Just looking for a place to stay,” Sokka tried to respond, but neither of them paid attention to him, acting as though he hadn’t even spoken. Spirits, they weren’t even looking at him, it was like he was a ghost.

Zuko looked up slightly after a few moments, likely noticing the lack of response. The cloudy sky combined with the slowly lowering sun and cover from his hair made his eyes look less glaringly gold than usual.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Zuko held back his scoff when he realised the soldiers were ignoring Sokka, clearly favoring him because they assumed he was an Earth Kingdom citizen. The opinion of the Water Tribe throughout both the Earth Kingdom and the Fire Nation was not favorable, mainly seeing them as uncivilized. There were some pretty outlandish stories about them, many that were still believed, even in the Earth Kingdom despite technically being allies.

“Just like he said. We’re looking for a place to stay,” Zuko reiterated.

“Rare to see people coming south, where are you headed?”

“Kyoshi Island, I have family there,” he lied.

“What’s he doing with you?” the soldier that hadn’t been speaking before questioned, gesturing to Sokka.

“We met up north. I was working on a trading ship until recently. He’s heading south too, seemed better to travel together,” Zuko covered convincingly.

He simply got a nod, and the quieter guard didn’t say anymore.

“How long will you be in town for?”

“Only tonight, we’ll get supplies in the morning and head out early.”

“Names?”

“Li and Sokka.”

“Alright, you can go. There’s an inn at the south end of town, won’t take long to get there, they should have a room.”

Sokka let out a harsh breath when they left the soldiers’ earshot.

“We still have to get a room,” Zuko commented, not sure how well this would work out when they weren’t in the dreary light of a stormy sky.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Luckily they didn’t have much trouble getting a room, it was likely that most of the people in town trusted that anyone who got past the soldiers were safe, most people barely gave them a second glance. Even so the two of them opted to stay in their room rather than explore, not chancing someone looking too close at Zuko, or deciding to give Sokka trouble.

After a while in their room Zuko noticed Sokka was oddly quiet, something that, because it was Sokka, was pretty jarring. Zuko didn’t say anything as he prepared some tea in the pot that had been supplied in their room, though opting not to use the spark rocks as the window shutters were closed so no one would see, it felt good to use his bending again, even for something so small. After a few minutes he set down a cup on the table next to Sokka’s bed.

“You gonna tell me what you’re thinking about or is it just your turn to be the brooding one?” Sokka looked up from his focus on the floor, glaring at Zuko rather than speaking.

“Drink some tea,” Zuko urged, in a similar way to how his uncle would when trying to get Zuko to settle down and talk to him.

“I don’t want tea.”

“You’re angry.”

“Yeah I’m angry! What even was that!?” Sokka yells, leaping from where he was sitting on the bed. He started pacing the floor between the two beds, fist clenching and unclenching as he did. Zuko had a feeling he’d known when Sokka’s sudden sullenness was about, the reaction just confirmed it.

“There are…” –Zuko sighed– “some pretty nasty stories about the Water Tribe, many people still believe them, even in the Earth Kingdom.”

“We’re supposed to be allies! My father, my people are fighting alongside men like *that!”* Sokka shouts. He throws himself back on the bed, blue eyes looking at Zuko as if he had some other, better, nicer explanation to how the soldiers had acted. Truth was, he didn’t, there wasn’t anything he could say, nothing justified it.

“Sokka…” Zuko wasn’t sure how to tell him to lower his voice before someone else heard him.

“Everyone’s been fine so far. I mean yeah I’ve overheard some people, got some stares, but he *ignored* me, it was like I wasn’t even there,” Sokka raked fingered through his hair, accidentally pulling out the tie that held his wolf tail, hair falling over his expression like curtains.

Zuko thought it might be best that Sokka knows this might not be a one time thing, or that even if being ignored is, that something else is likely to happen. Truth was most people probably left him alone before because of who he was with, “not everyone is like that, but you were traveling with the Avatar before, it’ll be different now.”

“They didn’t even blink at you,” Sokka grumbled.

“I hid my eyes; it was too dark and my hair helps,” Zuko explains simply, most of the time he avoids looking at someone directly, best case is to hide his eyes if he can, but as a general rule it only works for a short period of time.

“Your hair?” Sokka questions, and finally picks up his tea from the bedside table.

“It helps cover my eyes, but also I wear it down, rather than in a Fire Nation style.”

“So what, you just pretend?” He tone on edge, like he might turn his anger at what happened into an argument.

Zuko would humor him if he didn’t feel like passing out right now, plus it was probably best for them to keep the peace they’ve had the past few days. “Yes,” he answers in an even tone. Sokka grumbles slightly but doesn’t say anymore as he drinks his tea.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Waking the next morning was odd, without any weight on his chest, or the warmth of the firebender that, despite his current issues with bending, still runs exceptionally high. In the past several days traveling they slept with their bedrolls set up to be one so they could share their blankets and have the tarp over them to block rain. He sighs, willing his eyes open after a few minutes, finding that light is shining into the room, the shutters open with the firebender sitting on his bed mediating. Sokka doesn’t speak, watching as the flame of the candle on the bedside table rises and falls slowly with Zuko’s breathing. Several minutes go by before Zuko opens his eyes, and immediately Sokka can see a difference, or maybe it's just the way the morning sun reflects off golden eyes, but Zuko looks… calm?

“Morning,” Sokka greets, getting up now that the firebender is done and he doesn’t have to worry about disturbing him. As Zuko puts out the candle Sokka packs up the few things they’d taken out the night before when figuring out what supplies they would need, and if they would be able to carry a small tent, as the rain didn’t seem to be going anywhere.

Zuko hums in response to the greeting, combing fingers through his hair, which the longest part now reached a bit past his shoulders. “We should head out, get what we need before most people are up,” he says, which only then Sokka realises he’d been sort of staring *again.*

After getting packed up they quietly exit the room, making their way out of the small inn, luckily without drawing attention. The pair head toward the market that’s just started opening for the morning, many of the shops and carts not open yet, but what was available would have to do. Their shopping goes smoothing for the first few carts, a few surprised looks but not comments.

“Looks like this shop has tents, we can see if they have one that won’t be too difficult for us to carry,” Zuko comments as he stops in front of the shop, a sign outside mentioning traveling supplies, including tents.

“Might as well try, it’s still early and it is the last thing we need.” With Sokka’s agreement Zuko walks into the shop and he follows behind. They made their way to the counter, an older woman sat behind it, who’s green eyes narrowed from the moment they walked in.

“What do you want?”

Despite what Sokka would describe as a pretty aggressive tone, Zuko speaks politely with a falsely calm tone, “we’re looking for a tent, your sign outside–”

“I know what my sign says. I wrote it boy.”

Once again Zuko ignores her harshness, “do you have one light enough to carry? We don’t have an ostrich horse and the rain is beginning to be a problem.”

“Yes, I suppose *you* wouldn’t know the weather here.”

Zuko doesn’t respond this time, and Sokka takes it to mean he shouldn’t either. Silence hangs over the three for a long few moments before the woman finally speaks again, “three silver pieces.”

Zuko’s eyes narrow but he takes out their coin bag, “one, that’s already over charging.”

“The price is three silver pieces,” she reiterates, now rising from her chair.

“I heard what you said, but I’m not paying that. One piece or it can sit here until you eventually sell it, which then you’ll have to wait and make at most twenty copper pieces. If your pride is worth losing that much money by all means don’t sell us the tent.”

Sokka can’t help but stare at Zuko, his tone taking on more of a formal sound, commanding but calm. For once he sounded like a prince, not in the yelling and intimidating way that he was months ago, but more in the way that he actually seems like a leader. Sokka can see the anger, as Zuko always has an underlying sort of fury to him, but it doesn’t come through in his voice, not like Sokka is sure his would if he attempted to speak right now.

After another several moments Zuko silently places the silver piece on the counter.

The old woman doesn’t speak again as she pulls a tent bag from the pile of various items behind the counter, she puts it down as she takes the coin, “you will leave now.”

Zuko takes the bag, slinging it over his shoulder as he turns to Sokka with a satisfied and slightly smug grin. Walking out of the shop.

“Tents aren’t twenty coppers, they’re usually closer to fifty, sometimes a silver piece,” Sokka comments as he takes the tent bag from Zuko, since the firebender is still hurt and was already carrying two bags.

Zuko hums in agreement, “she probably hasn’t sold a tent in years, even Earth Kingdom citizens avoid the military. I've met a few people who are traveling to get out of conscription, been asked plenty of times myself.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“I’ve been meaning to ask, what happened with Jet?”

It took a minute for Zuko to realise who Sokka was referring to, his brows knitting together at the memory of that day. It was the last time he’d seen his uncle. He didn’t want to think about his uncle, knowing the man would probably feel rather betrayed by his disappearing act, but it was for the best, after everything that has happened, he still believed that, maybe even more so than he had before.

“Ran into him and his group in the forest, they thought I was a spy,” he snickered, remembering how ridiculous that sounded to him even then.

“You’d make a terrible spy,” Sokka comments, thinking that even without his scar Zuko would attract too much attention.

“That’s what I said, but they didn’t believe me. I don’t blame them for being cautious, but throwing me in their storeroom was unnecessary. Not to mention *stupid*, I could’ve burned down their whole camp. They secured someone they thought was a Fire Nation spy with *rope,”* Zuko nearly shouts, still baffled by the stupidity of the whole thing.

“He’s too full of himself. Had both my sister and Aang believing his bullshit, it was infuriating,” Sokka complains with an exaggerated sigh. “Katara was obsessed with him, it was so *weird.*” His face scrunches in disgust at the memory of how smitten his sister seemed with the boy.

The comment reminded him of someone, someone he should’ve brought up a while ago since he’d asked him to, though he was pretty sure he wanted him to say hi to Katara for him more so than Aang or Sokka.

“What's that face for? Don’t tell me– *not you too!?*” Sokka dramatises, obviously joking, though in any other situation Zuko might admit that Jet at the very least is *attractive* if it wasn’t for his ego, and the whole imprisoning him thing. The face was very much not about Jet though, or anyone he found attractive.

“Considering the guy literally had me captive, definitely not.” Zuko assures, trying to get back to what he meant to talk about, “Actually, I forgot to mention someone I ran into. Haru?”

“You ran into Haru? When?”

“Sometime after you did. They got their town back, had dinner with him and his parents, they let me stay over. He just told me to say hi and let you all know he’s okay. Honestly I think the message was meant more for Katara…” Zuko noted with a side eye to Sokka.

“Why does everyone hit on my sister?” he mumbles before sending a glare toward Zuko, which he quickly understood.

Zuko put his hands up in a mock surrender, “I promise I have *no* interest in your sister.”

Sokka let out a relieved breath, probably because Zuko was the last person he’d want his sister going out with, even after Jet.

——

As clouds began to roll in, well before sun down they made plans to stop. The sky darkened quickly, thunder rolling through the clouds, shaking the trees around them. They didn’t waste time being picky about their camping spot, only making sure it wasn’t down hill or too close to water. They just managed to get their tent up and tarp secured over it when rain broke through the clouds. The pair quickly threw their things in the tent, following in after immediately, lucky not to be drenched. The got their bedrolls unfurled, the tent not big enough for room between them, but they were use to close quarters by now, with having to use the tarp, this at least had room that their bags didn’t have to be on the bedrolls, instead placed at the secured opening of the tent.

“This rain is making it impossible to cook,” Sokka comments as he pulls out the dried fish from their food bag.

“I’d rather the first time I try to do a controlled burn after so long that it isn’t in a tent, lunch tomorrow hopefully, if the weather lets up.” And the universe seemed to decide to make a point as lightning lit up the world outside their tent.

They split dried fish and rice cakes, long lasting provisions weren’t exactly in high supply in a town that was essentially a military outpost, most of those items were likely going to the soldiers stopping through, not being put for sale for civilians. It probably didn’t help that despite being allowed in, in the light of day neither of them looked Earth.

After sharing their small meal they found themselves restless, as despite the darkness outside because of the clouds, it was likely just reaching sunset, they were a bit ahead of their usual schedule. Okay maybe it was just Sokka who was restless, because Zuko looked near passing out, gold eyes closing for seconds at a time before slowly opening again, as if trying to pay attention, despite the fact that Sokka wasn’t speaking.

“If you want to sleep I don’t mind, you should be getting more rest than you have, it’ll help with healing.”

“I’m” –he yawns– “fine.”

Sokka attempts to stifle his laugh, but fails miserably as an amused chuckle escapes, “at least lay down, don’t want to fall asleep sitting up.” Zuko concedes nearly immediately, shuffling under the doubled up blankets. “You alright with the cold?” he questions, having noticed the firebender wasn’t shivering as much today.

“Yeah, it's fine,” Zuko responds in a vaguely stilted tone.

*Fine.* That world was gonna drive Sokka nuts, because Zuko didn’t exactly seem fine.

“You lit the fire yesterday with bending, when you made tea, are things getting better?” Sokka questioned, hoping he might get some sort of actual answer.

“I didn’t realise you saw that, I won’t—“

“I don’t care if you firebend. Katara always says waterbending is a part of her, I don’t really get it, but I wouldn’t ask you not to bend, unless you know there’s like Earth Kingdom soldiers around, that would probably be bad.” —he notices a sort of shock in Zuko’s expression— “Anyway, I just know Siew said you should still be resting, and we haven’t really got to stop much, I wasn’t sure if you bending was a good sign, or if you just don’t know how to use spark rocks.” Sokka lightly teases, earning a lazy glare from the firebender.

“It’s coming back slowly, the cloudy days are making it harder, slower to return, but it will… is,” Zuko speaks slowly, eyes closed as he does. “I'd rather keep going,” he mumbles, voice falling off at the end.

“We can…” Sokka sighs, realising he’s asleep. A few minutes later he decides to lay down as well.

## Chapter 13

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

thanks to everyone who wished me to feel better, I appreciate it. I'm doing better, getting some rest (by that I mean I still have work but I mostly work from home so I can take it easy).

anyway, on to the chapter!

### Text

“How long till the coast do you think?” Sokka questioned as they packed up their tent.

The sun was finally out today, though the morning air was still cold. The wind picked up more in the last day, meaning they were certainly near water, the channel through the Earth Kingdom hopefully not too far off.

“A few days probably,” Zuko guessed, he couldn’t find the town from the other day on their map, making it difficult to tell for sure where they were, but the rain had been slowing them down, making them stop earlier and travel slower.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

The sun was high in the sky by the time they decided to stop for lunch soon. No clouds littered the nearby sky as they had the past few days, instead the storm clouds were far in the distance, darkening the southern horizon. As grateful as Sokka was for the reprieve from rain currently, the direction of the clouds didn’t bode well for any watercraft they might find passage on; one escape from a sinking ship was enough.

They didn’t get to stop for lunch before they came across an older man who seemed to be having trouble with his wagon. He noticed them nearly as soon as they did him, waving them over.

“Mind giving me a hand?” He called as they neared him.

Sokka noticed the problem as they got closer, one of his wheels had come loose and broken off. The wagon tilted awkwardly, the ostrich horses connected to the front of it had laid down, one even looked to be sleeping, so the man had probably been there a while.

“I can fix it, just need someone to keep the wagon steady,” he explained, gesturing for them to do as he said.

After a quick glance it was decided and the two of them got to the side of the cart that was broken, lifting the side so the man could reattach the wheel. It didn’t take more than a few minutes for the repairs to be done. As the old man got up he dusted his pants and pushed the long mostly grey brown hair from his face. Carefully the teens lowered the wagon, the wheel thankfully seeming secure now.

“Say, you boys headed south?” His tone was jovial, seeming entirely relaxed with the two of them. Sokka would assume the man was an earthbender considering his lack of defensiveness with two people who could’ve robbed him, but from everything he’d heard in the past earthbenders nearly always have green eyes, the man’s were a warm brown.

“Yes, sir,” Zuko responded politely.

“Great, I’ve gotta make a delivery at one of the port towns, I'll pay you a few pieces if you help me unload it once we get there. You two can ride in the back, sound good?” He offers with a soft smile, though considering he immediately turns to wake the ostrich horses Sokka is pretty sure he knows what decision they’ll come to.

Zuko hesitates, but Sokka is getting impatient and it will take them another two days if they walk but they’ll probably be to town by tonight with the wagon, so he’s quick to agree, “Sounds good to me. Are you alright with that Li?” he questioned with a slight elbow to Zuko’s side to get him to go with his plan. Zuko just nods.

The man getting into the cart with a simple, “we’ll get moving then,” confirmed Sokka’s assumption that he already figured what answer the two would land on, even if Zuko wasn’t entirely a fan of it.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

The two end up eating their lunch in the back of the wagon with crates of Spirits know what next to them, neither really care to ask. It was about an hour into their journey when the man struck up a conversation.

“I didn’t get you boys names,” he starts.

“I’m Sokka. The grumpy one is Li.”

“Nice to meet you, I’m Lao. I’m originally from Ba Sing Se but I wanted to travel so now I make deliveries across the northern Earth Kingdom. How bout you two, where you headin’?”

“Hell of a time to travel,” Sokka quips, chuckling. “We’re headed to Omashu.”

“Omashu? Now why would you want to go there? You’ll be lucky if it ain’t overrun by the time ya’ll get there. Been a battle goin’ on down there the past few weeks, Fire Nation tryin’ a take over. From what I hear they’re winning. Seemin’ like Ba Sing Se gonna be the last major city left soon.” The man didn’t sound particularly bothered with how the battle was turning out, a rare sight, someone so nonplussed about the war.

“My dad’s fighting there, so, with all respect, I’m hoping you’re wrong.”

“Southern then? Northern Water Tribe don’t fight, ya?”

“Yes sir,”

“You quite the ways from home.”

“I was visiting the North, my sister is up there training,” Sokka explained with a half truth.

“That so? Didn’t know the South had benders, good for her. How’d you two meet?”

Sokka looked to Zuko, seeing if he’d come up with some story, but he realised his traveling companion had fallen asleep, probably for the best considering he was still injured, he should be getting more rest than they have been, sleeping in a tent during a rainstorm hardly counts as rest..

“Up in Agna Qel’a,” Sokka started, a lie, but barely, Sokka wasn’t sure their *meeting* at Wolf Cove could really count.

“You met a firebender in Agna Qel’a?” –he seemed to sense Sokka tensing– “don’t worry, I don’t care much about that, though I’d like to know why a Water Tribesman is traveling with a Fire Nation Soldier.”

“He’s not a soldier, he was a captive on one of the Fire Navy ships.”

“Deserter?”

“Nah, pissed off some commander– admiral actually,” Sokka tried to make it seem like he only knew parts of the story.

The man glanced back now, eyes going to Zuko, he still remained asleep. Sokka watched the man’s expression soften, he couldn’t figure out exactly why, he hadn’t mentioned anything specific. The man spared a short glance to Sokka before his eyes were back on the path.

“Glad you two managed to get outta there. Can’t imagine the admiral was very happy ‘bout that.”

“Kind of hoping I don’t find out,” Sokka responded with a slightly nervous laugh, cause he really hoped he, but especially Zuko, would never see Zhao again.

“That’d probably be for the best.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

The sun was lowering toward the horizon when they were pulling into town, the guards didn’t stop Lao as he pulled in, seeming to know him based on the way they simply waved him in.

“We’ll be heading straight toward the docks, if you wanna wake your friend, shouldn’t be a few minutes.”

Sokka followed the suggestion, “Hey Zuko, time to wake up,” he went to shake him slightly, hand on his shoulder. Almost immediately he grabbed his wrist, gold eyes narrowed into a glare.

“Just me buddy,” Sokka spoke, in a tone he’d learned after his mother’s death, soft and assured, he used to use it when Katara woke from nightmares.

Zuko blinked a few times, eyes softening before he slowly loosened his grip on Sokka’s wrist. It took him a few long moments to fully let go, finally rising properly from his slumped position. Gold eyes quickly moved on from Sokka, instead staring toward the front of the wagon.

His brows furrowed watching Zuko, it hadn’t been the first time he’d woken up in such a state, on guard and suspicious of his surroundings. Probably left over from his weeks aboard Zhao’s ship. Was he having nightmares? Sokka had, of the charred remains of the men he’d lead to their deaths. Occasionally it would be the charred remains of his mother, Katara, Aang, his father. Nightmares of their deaths, despite three of them being alive, hopefully. He was terrified that he’d never see them again.

The wagon came to a halt, jerking Sokka out of his thoughts.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

They finished unloading the wagon’s cargo just as the sun left the sky, giving way to the light of the moon. It was only when they were done unloading that they noticed the Earth Kingdom soldier walking over to them, or more specifically Lao. The old man gave a pleasant smile, the kind you give when dealing with someone unpleasant who could cost you your job.

“I’ll have my men take it to the ship, Mr. Lao,” he assures, glancing over to them, they were attempting to mind their own business, standing by the wagon. Zuko ignored the man, more focused on the ostrich horses that had seemingly taken a liking to him. Sokka stayed a bit further from the two animals, pretending like he didn’t notice the soldier’s eyes stay on them long enough to make it clear he didn’t trust them, it wasn’t hard as he was watching Zuko pet the ostrich horses that had tried to bite Sokka when they first got to the docks.

“Fine with me,” Lao commented with a passive shrug before taking the pouch of coins the soldier handed him.

“With eyes like those, you’re lucky he didn’t blow up your wagon, and yourself,” the soldier spoke somewhat quieter with this comment, Zuko made no sign that he heard him, but Sokka couldn’t help tensing. He realised what was in the crates, blasting jelly. The soldier was right, even by accident if one of those boxes were set off… well they wouldn’t be making it to Omashu. He shuttered slightly at the thought.

“I wouldn’t have brought him along if I didn’t think it was fine. I was hoping you’d be more understanding, these two are looking for passage across the water–” Lao begins, earning a knowing glare from the soldier. “Don’t give me that look Jian, these two boys are the only reason I made it here, I would’ve been stranded with a broken cart if they didn’t come by.” Sokka decided it was best not to mention that they did very little to help, and that Lao hadn’t seemed like he would take them just ignoring him too well. It was a good decision, even with the current awkwardness.

“Where are you two looking to get to?”

“I’m tracking down my father and his men, they were going to Omashu to help out the forces there,” Sokka explains at least somewhat truthfully.

“Your father? Southern then. I don’t mind giving *you* a ride, what’s *he* doing here?”

“Looking for a friend,” Zuko responds simply, finally looking away from the ostrich horses.

“You won’t find any *friends* here.” His eyes narrowed looking at Zuko.

“I promise he’s fine,” Sokka tries to jump in, a bit shocked at himself for jumping to Zuko’s defense when not long ago he believed him to be an enemy… he hadn’t even realised that thought was completely gone, the idea that Zuko might be playing him, that he would turn on him, it just didn’t seem like someone he’d do.

“I won’t have an *ashmaker* on my ship,” he states plainly.

“We’ll just find someone else to take us then.”

Sokka finds himself standing straighter, and more importantly in front of Zuko, glowering at the soldier as he pulled his gaze from Zuko to meet Sokka.

“Jian, please, we’ve known each other for how long? And you heard the boy, his father and *his* men. You’re really going to risk pissing off the Southern Water Tribe Chief because you didn’t give his son and his friend passage?”

Sokka’s eyes immediately went to Lao. He hadn’t realised the way he’d said it, or more so that anyone would take it as his father being Chief. It wasn’t untrue, but Lao was kind of overselling what his father’s reaction would be, he might not be happy to hear about someone denying him passage, but he wouldn’t actually *do* anything, there are a lot more important things to focus on. Plus Sokka was pretty sure his father would have Zhao a little closer to the top of his list than some random Earth Kingdom ship captain. Of course he wasn’t going to say any of this, especially if it got them on the boat.

“That true boy?”

“Chief Hakoda is my father, yes.” Sokka confirms, then decides to add, “I’m his oldest son.”

“And the ashm–*other boy…* he’s your friend?” Jian catches himself, seeming to think better of insulting Zuko now.

“He is.” There isn’t a moment of hesitation.

Jian sighs, glancing past Sokka to look at Zuko, who’d been quiet since Sokka had spoken up earlier. Sokka watched as the man seemed to be trying to find any reason not to let Zuko aboard.

“I know a– *firebender* when I see one. A single spark and I’m throwing you overboard.” He didn’t wait for Zuko to respond as he started talking to Sokka again, “he doesn’t leave your side. I don’t want him walking my ship alone.”

“That’s fine,” Sokka agrees, glancing back at Zuko to check that he wouldn’t argue, luckily he didn’t look bothered. They’d been traveling together for a while, it wasn’t like this was going to be any different, plus Sokka didn’t really want to leave Zuko alone on a ship of people that weren’t going to be his biggest fan.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

After both payment and goodbyes from Lao, the two boys were headed onto the ship with their packs. Zuko kept his head lowered as they walked on, doing his best not to draw attention, best case, the Captain would keep his mouth shut and no one would bother him. Zuko knows how news gets around a ship quickly though, and he didn’t have much hope of keeping under the radar the entire time they’d be on the ship.

Jian gestures for them to follow, heading under the ship. The halls are empty as the crew is above loading cargo from the docks and getting ready to sail. Zuko only raises his head when Jian finally speaks.

“I won’t have you sleeping among my crew, it’ll cause a distraction and I don’t trust your *friend* when my men aren’t on guard. I’ve got an empty storeroom and some extra cots, that’ll have to do. Also, your weapons are to be left in your room at all times.”

Sokka just nodded, deciding not to argue with Jian, even with having to leave his boomerang behind. Zuko knew just going along would be for the best so he responded similarly with a silent nod.

Soon enough they made it to the storeroom he’d been talking about. With the promise that he’d send someone by with two cots they were left to their own devices, though directed not to leave the room until they’d been told to, as he wanted to inform the crew of their two *guests*. So much for not being noticed.

“We could’ve been stowaways and had a bigger space than this,” Zuko comments.

He wasn’t so much complaining as stating a fact, he wasn’t sure two cots would even properly fit in the room, it was really more of a closet, and the reason it was empty was probably because it wasn’t even big enough to store much.

“Didn’t you mention hiding in vents when you were on that Fire Nation ship?”

Zuko nodded, “not the only time I’ve done it though.”

“When?”

“Pirate ship, about a year after I was banished.”

“Didn’t you have your own ship?”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

*Two Years Ago*

They’d stopped at a neutral port, a need for supplies. Zuko rarely ever left the boat back then, unless it was Avatar related, but his uncle had convinced him to get some air for once. So a fourteen year old Zuko was begrudgingly exploring the port town. He’d forgone his armor for a simple red tunic and black trousers, his hair down from its usual hairstyle, though he kept his blades at his side even then. Because of the neutrality of the port no one blinked much at seeing a young Fire Nation teen walking about.

In his exploration of the town he’d come across an unexpected friend of sorts, a bright green iguana parrot. The animal had decided to follow him around most of the day, perching on his shoulder or more annoyingly his head.

The town was bustling with people, carts and tents littering the streets as people tried to sell their wares. The town was still heavily Earth, it was on an island off the coast of the kingdom so that wasn’t particularly surprising. It was rare to be somewhere so packed, the closest he’d come to the packed streets was his grandfather’s funeral, and even then he’d not been in the crowd. He allowed himself to get lost, the anonymity oddly comforting.

When Zuko finally headed back toward the docks he was met with something else unexpected, though definitely not friendly.

From his position down the pier he could see Zhao’s ship, and more importantly he could see Zhao and his men waking up the gangplank to *his* ship. At the time Zuko was still having trouble even producing flames, the memory of what had happened still felt too fresh.

The reptile-bird on his shoulder squawked to get his attention before starting toward a different ship, circling around, trying to get Zuko to follow. With one last look toward his ship he ran to follow the bird. He couldn’t face Zhao, not yet.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“I hid on a pirate ship, small crew so they were easy to avoid, plus the bird helped out, not sure why. Stayed hiding out on it for a few weeks, until I saw my ship at the same port as us again, no Zhao in sight. Uncle was pretty upset with me.”

“You disappeared for weeks, after *Zhao* showed up, of course he’d be upset!” Sokka nearly shouted, eyes wide in disbelief. “You’re lucky you didn’t get found out, who knows what they would’ve done.”

“Probably try to hold me for ransom, well if they found out who I am. Not sure otherwise, probably nothing good, but it was better than *him*, I’d probably still do it.” Zuko didn’t care if it made him a coward, if he never saw Zhao again it would be too soon. He knew his fear was exactly what Zhao wanted, but he didn’t care, he was scared and he was going to stay away from him if it was at all an option. Revenge didn’t matter, confronting him didn’t matter, he just wanted to be free of the memories of the ship.

Sokka just sort of *looked* at him for a few long moments, blue eyes trained on him, but in a way that was soft, like he was trying to ease Zuko with his gaze alone. He nodded slightly, in a sort of understanding agreement.

### End Notes

## Chapter 14

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

No big note today, enjoy the chapter friends

Author fun? fact: I'm still sick. I hate it here (:

### Text

“You asleep?”

“Not now,” Zuko sighs.

“Good, let’s go up top, I feel like I’m gonna suffocate in here.” Sokka was nearly climbing over Zuko to get out of the cots, which had to be directly next to each other to fit in the room. they’d ended up laying their tarp and bed rolls over the two to make it more like a larger one.

“You don’t have to climb over me, I’m getting up,” Zuko grumbles, having been half asleep. He pushes himself up, lifting himself from the cot with lethargic movements. He winces as he bends and stretches the stiff skin of the burns littering his body. Slowly he gets moving, grabbing his long sleeve shirt from its place draped over his bag.

“It shouldn’t be *that* cold.”

“I grew up somewhere that never sees snow, cold to me and cold to you are very different things.”

Sokka just shrugs, seeming to be planning to just wear his sleeveless tunic. Zuko wouldn’t argue it, if Sokka wanted to freeze, let him. Zuko pulls on the shirt, cringing as even putting on a shirt still messes with his injuries, he knew that traveling so soon had been a bad plan, but his body was really starting to prove it to him.

They quietly made their way up to the deck, both of them not having much trouble finding their way through a ship. Sokka breathed in the cold midnight air just as they left the confines of the hull. Zuko quickly noted the patrol walking the ship’s deck, passing by not far away, they only gave the two of them a short glance before moving on while whispering to each other. Zuko let out a relieved sigh that they just ignored them.

Zuko had dealt with the Earth Kingdom Navy, the nation wasn't known for their power on the water, because of their element, but Zuko knew they were fierce, even if small in number compared to the Fire Nation or Water Tribes. The ships rarely had benders, instead their men trained with various weapons. And most importantly, because of how few went into the Navy, their men were overworked, at sea constantly, always together. Each ship was like a brotherhood, they backed each other up, and if someone did one wrong, they would deal with all of them. Zuko wanted to be ignored, because he wasn’t looking for a fight that he knew Sokka and him wouldn’t win.

He followed Sokka to the railings along the ship’s deck, the other boy leaning against the rails as his eyes gleamed looking up at the stars. “I haven’t seen my father in years,” he started, blue eyes shifting to Zuko as he settled next to him. “I don’t know what he’ll think of me. I left Katara behind, I was supposed to protect her.” Zuko recognises the mix of anger and hope in Sokka’s eyes, “what if he hates me?”

“The man I met would *never* hate you. And you didn’t leave Katara, you were sent on a mission and captured, he’ll be glad you’re even alive,” Zuko reasoned. Hakoda had defended *him*, even after he taunted that his children were wanted by the Fire Nation as well, he still defended him, tried to get them to let him stay until he healed, he saw him as a kid instead of an enemy or a soldier. Zuko knew Hakoda could never hate his son, he wasn’t sure Hakoda had it in him to hate, not really. He did what he had to out of a need to protect what and who he still has, not out of vengeance or hatred, Zuko’s seen enough to be able to see that. Sokka had nothing to worry about, not really, but that doesn’t always stop you believing that the worst could happen.

“I *failed*. How am I supposed to face him?”

Zuko’s silent, because it's a question he’s asked himself a million times, and he’s never had an answer, not really. But Sokka’s father is not like his own, he is not cruel or sadistic, he’s kind and protective. Zuko smiled slightly, “with your head held high, because all any *good* parent wants is for their child to be safe.”

Sokka seems to take Zuko’s words differently than he’d expected, his expression saddening, eyes wide and light pulled down into a frown, “I– sorry.”

“Sokka, I wasn’t– my point is that your father is a good man and he’ll be happy to see you. It is understandable to worry though, it’s just natural.”

After a few long moments Sokka’s expression lightens, though not happy, he looks more content, his lips settling into their natural slight smile.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Zuko woke with the sun, as he nearly always did, though in a rare turn of events since they started traveling together, Sokka was still asleep. He carefully moved to sit up in the makeshift bed, wincing slightly as his wounds stretched. He pulled open his bag that sat on the small floor space, shuffling through it to find the medicines stowed at the bottom. He set them to the side before carefully pulling off his shirt to get to the bandages that covered his torso. He slowly unwraps the bandages, luckily this time there doesn’t seem to be any new blood. While the wounds still pulled they were finally starting to heal, though after weeks of going mostly untreated he knew he’d bare more scars than he had before.

It was a few minutes later that Sokka woke up, “what time is it?” he mumbled, still buried in blankets when Zuko turned to look back at him. The Water Tribe boy’s eyes weren’t even open yet.

“A little after sunrise,” Zuko answered simply as he went back to rewrapping his ankles, the wounds from the shackles were still pretty bad, and despite thinking they could use some air he didn’t want someone else seeing them. Zuko felt as Sokka shifted on the cots, but he didn’t bother turning around again, focused on his task.

Sokka didn’t speak for a while, but Zuko could feel his gaze on his back, he’d realised belatedly that trying to sort out the wounds on his back himself wasn’t going to work, so he’d moved on to his other injuries while waiting for Sokka to wake. He decided not to dwell on the fact that he’d gotten comfortable enough with the other teen to show weakness willingly, it wasn’t like he had someone else to go to.

“Still have the burn salve out?” Zuko handed the small jar back to Sokka in response. He carefully pulled his hair out of the way, the length of it now meeting the burns on the top of his back. “I have an extra tie if you want to put your hair up,” Sokka offered as had carefully treated the burns.

“I look less intimidating with it down,” he declines, finding comfort in hiding some of himself behind his hair. He chose not to mention the fact that the damage to his ear and, by association, his eye is a lot more obvious with his hair up, and he’d rather people not know his weaknesses right away. Plenty of people don’t assume his vision has any issues, but the actual damage becomes a lot more obvious with his melted ear.

“Trying to get peoples’ guard down?” Zuko could hear that it was meant as a joke from the light laughter in Sokka’s tone, though he wasn’t entirely wrong.

“Something like that… I’m also just pretty used to it down now.” Beyond all of it, accepting it, a hair tie that would certainly be Water Tribe blue, seemed wrong somehow.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

The pair was up on deck, doing their best to stay out of the way but also not really wanting to stay in a closet for the next few days. Luckily no one seemed to pay them any mind, beyond a few curious looks no one said anything, all going about their duties in the winter sun. Sokka stared out at the expanse of water, he was oddly quiet today, Zuko didn’t press why, assuming it was due to suddenly being around so many people. Unlike Sokka, he didn’t watch the ocean, instead focus on the crew going about their day. He leaned against the ship’s railing next to Sokka, trying not to look so much like he was observing them. He didn’t want both of them to have their back to the unknowns that were the Earth Kingdom’s marines. While no one seemed to be paying attention to them he still didn’t feel comfortable letting his guard down.

As time went on he made a few mental notes on the crew, most of them were older, at least notably older than himself or Sokka. The youngest of the crew looked to be in their thirties, and everyone worked as if they’d been doing this for the better part of their lives, which was very likely the case for most if not all of them. There was constant chatter amongst the crew, all idle and generally unimportant from what he could hear.

Hours dragged on and it became more apparent that something was wrong with Sokka, because if he wasn’t talking something had to be wrong. Zuko moved so his right side would be toward the deck, making sure he could still keep an eye on what was going on while speaking with Sokka.

“Are you alright?” he asked tentatively, golden gaze looking at Sokka just long enough that blue eyes met him. Sokka only hmmed in response before turning back to the water.

“Do you want to go back inside?” Zuko offered, maybe he felt he couldn’t talk here.

“You two, lend us a hand,” someone called from a small group of the crew. Sokka finally turned from the ocean at the call. Looking over Zuko could see a pile of crates behind them probably from a different load in of items after they got on the ship the night before as they hadn’t actually left the port til the morning.

“Maybe they’ll like you more if we help,” Sokka commented quietly. Zuko could guess it was meant to be slightly joking but the light sarcasm was missing in his voice, further confirming that something was wrong. Zuko didn’t get to ask again though as they headed toward the group.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

They didn’t end up going back to their *room*, even after finishing, the group of sailors, who were actually pretty pleasant, invited the two to eat with them.

Now the pair were sitting among a larger group of sailors on the deck, though they stayed closer to the group they’d been working with, just in case. Sokka had seemed to brighten up, telling stories from his travels as well as ones from home. Zuko stayed largely quiet, glad to see that whatever had been bothering Sokka was forgotten, at least for now.

“Usually you go ice dodging but we were in the Earth Kingdom so ended up rock dodging instead,” Sokka explained at the end of his story.

“*Rock dodging,* sounds like when I annoyed my sister as a kid,” one of the sailors jokes with a light laugh.

“I'm constantly getting splashed by mine,” Sokka says in a sort of agreement, and as if seeing a way to draw Zuko into the conversation adds, “Li has a sister too, right?” he prompts some sort of comment.

Zuko debates for a moment going along the lines of the other two in making a joke about what happens if you annoy his sister, but frankly getting burned by her was the princess on a good day. As a general rule Zuko did his best not to annoy his younger sister. In the end he just opts for agreement, “yeah, younger sister.”

“She part Fire like you?” one of the sailors –he said his name was Qianfan earlier– asks, shockingly sounding like curiosity rather than an attempt at malice.

“*Part,*” another man scoffs, earning a glare from Qianfan. “What? Like I’m wrong?” he argues.

“It's fine Qianfan, I know what I look like. No, my sister’s not Earth,” he responds to the earlier question.

“Oh, unexpected. I assumed she’d be,” –Qianfan pauses in his comment as if he’d realised something, brown eyes focusing more intently on Zuko– “you’re the one of the posters, you aren’t Earth at all are you?”

“No, though my sister did always love to joke that maybe I could get an Earth Kingdom family to adopt me,” Zuko says with a slight shrug. He can’t see Sokka to his left, but he can feel the other teen tense at his confession, likely expecting Guo or some other sailor to react badly.

Instead Guo lets out a laugh, and not one of shock, but a full bellied laugh, as though he actually thought Zuko’s comment was funny, “never met a deserter, they usually go after you lot.”

“They definitely try.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“What was that? I thought you’d lie! What would we have done if they threw us overboard?” Sokka whisper yells as they get back to their room.

“They’re sailors, there’re posters of me at nearly every port, if they found out after I lied then I’d definitely be thrown overboard,” Zuko reasons.

“You’re crazy.”

“It runs in the family.”

For a long moment the room is silent, Sokka staring at him with wide eyes. Slowly the Water boy’s expression breaks, first a smile and then a laugh. Zuko can’t help but join in his laugh, the sound delightfully infectious.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Zuko wakes with a start, someone screaming. There’s a strong grip on his forearms, short nails digging into his skin. Who screamed? He finds Sokka next to him, sat up with his eyes wide and full of tears. The teen struggles to take in breaths, his breathing quick and shallow, his grip tightening as he hyperventilates. Zuko ignores the mild pain from the grip, instead focusing his attention on blue eyes.

“Sokka–” blue eyes darted to him, but there’s little focus in the gaze, his mind clearly not all there. “It was a dream. Whatever you saw, it didn’t happen. It isn’t happening now. I need you to look at me–”

“I am…” Sokka mutters in response.

“No. Actually look at me, you’re focused on something else, I need you to *look* at me,” Zuko emphasises, his voice direct rather than soft, trying to pull Sokka out of his thoughts.

Fuzzy eyes still look back at him, and Zuko sighs, “Come on Sokka.” He goes to move, though Sokka’s grip on his arms makes it difficult. He manages to pull his arm from his hold, scratched marks are left behind and it causes the other teen's eyes to leave his, instead on his own hand. Zuko shifts to his knees on the cot next to Sokka, “Look at me,” he demands, grabbing Sokka’s face to direct his view to him, immediately he grabs Zuko’s bandaged wrists, tight but not forcing away his hands.

“We’re on an Earth Kingdom ship. It’s our third day on the ship. It is a full moon tonight. We are in our room.”

Slowly blue eyes come into focus, the fog over them beginning to fade. “Zuko.” Embarrassment falls into his expression, alive eyes widening, cheeks going flush. “Sorry I just– It was– I didn’t mean to wake you.”

“I don’t mind being woken up.”

“Still…”

“How often does this happen?” he questions, trying to curve the subject.

“Not– It isn’t usually that bad.”

Zuko hmms, “you want to talk about it?”

“Not right now,” Sokka sighs, “mind if we go up to the deck?”

“I don’t.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

The sun warms the ship’s deck enough that most of the crew seems more content rather than shivering while they work. Zuko was grateful that the clouds they’d seen over the water days before hadn’t caused them trouble. After helping with a few daily chores around the ship the pair are free to watch the ocean, well Sokka was watching the water, Zuko found the habit boring, but it seemed to calm the boy and it wasn’t like there was much else for them to do.

The day ticked on, and when the sun was high enough for it to be about midday they joined the crew for food. Sokka joined the conversation as he had the past few days, seeming fine despite the mentally exhausting night. They never did talk about the dream, Sokka didn’t seem keen to and Zuko wasn’t going to push it. He joined in on some of the conversation, but kept his input pretty minimal.

Zuko didn’t see it as the creature came from his left, only noticing the animal when the weight of it landed on his head. He looked up, seeing a familiar face, “Jaw Long?” Gold eyes widen as he understands the reason for the birds' presence when they are still far from land.

“Are you friends with the iguana parrot?” Sokka questions, laughing a bit at his own question.

“There’s a pirate ship nearby. This iguana parrot belongs to its captain,” Zuko quickly informs, “where’d you fly in from?” He directs his question to the reptile bird, who immediately turns southeast and squawks.

“Wait, you actually know them?”

“Remember when I told you I stowed away on a pirate ship?”

“Yeah but that’s—“ Sokka doesn’t get to finish.

“We have to let the Captain know, you should come with us,” Qianfan cuts in, already up from where he was sitting, Guo next to him. Zuko is up along with Sokka a moment later, following the two sailors to Jian’s quarters.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“I’m telling you, I know who the iguana parrot belongs to, I’m not trying to mess with you here. What would I even get out of that?” Zuko tries to argue.

“Making us look like a bunch of fools for listening to some ash—“ his eyes went to Sokka, though he was probably imagining a much different person, namely Hakoda– “kid.”

“I’m pretty sure I’ve seen—“ once again Sokka is interrupted.

Luckily it means Zuko doesn’t have to argue anymore either as someone knocks on the door, and without even waiting for the clear to come in, pops his head in to speak –that wouldn’t have ended well for him if he was in the Fire Nation Navy– “Ship coming in from the south. Looks like a pirate ship.”

Jian looks at him with an annoyed glare before heading to the door, he stops before leaving, “both of you are to stay in your room. I’m not having you get in the way.” He doesn’t wait before continuing out to the deck.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“I hate this!” Sokka exclaims as his paces, if it can even be called that in the small room, as he speaks. “Why make us just sit here?! We could help! We’re old enough to be soldiers in the Earth Kingdom so why are they acting like we’re children!?”

Zuko sat on their cot, leaned against the wall on the side he usually slept. He had a few ideas why they didn’t want them involved, and he was pretty sure it didn’t have to do with their abilities, but he elected to shrug in response, figuring Sokka doesn’t actually want an answer. They’d been below for likely less than half a candle-mark and there was still nothing that sounded like a fight happening above. Jaw Long laid on the cot next to Zuko, curled up without a care in the world as he lightly stroked the reptile bird’s feathers.

“How are you so calm? Jian called you a liar when you were just trying to help!”

“I already knew he didn’t trust me,” he supplies.

“I don’t get you,” Sokka mutters as he stops his pacing in front of where Zuko is sitting on the cot.

“I’m not that complicated.”

### End Notes
