## Chapter 15

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

I had some work stuff to do and totally spaced on posting, I'm trying to catch up on a lot after being sick. this chapter is a bit short but a decent amount happens so hopefully you won't mind. I should be back Wednesday, this next chapter will be fun, so look forward to that.

### Text

Heavy footsteps thundered above them, dust and sand shaking from the ceiling as the sounds of metal on metal rang through the air. Shouts and curses were muffled through the floorboards. Sokka looked up, as if he could see anything going on above, his foot tapping on the wooden floor board while he sat at the edge of the bed. He didn’t know how long it had been but it felt like an eternity since they’d gotten down there.

Zuko was sitting cross-legged on the bed, swords laid next to him, with his eyes focused on the door. He didn’t react to the noises above, fingers stroking the iguana parrot’s head seeming entirely calm, though Sokka could see he was at least alert.

No footsteps neared their door though, and despite how long it felt trapped below deck, unable to see or help, the fight was over quickly.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

The ship had been quiet for a while, occasional footsteps above, some inaudible talking, and more noticeable, the sound of something impacting the water. There had been three of those splashes.

“Too much to hope those were the pirates?” Sokka questions after a long silence tells them that it was stopping at three… hopefully.

“Where would Jaw Long go then?”

“You’re worried about the bird?”

Zuko just shrugs, and Sokka has to stop himself from laughing, because how was he gonna explain to anyone that the Fire Prince is friends with an iguana parrot, who would believe him?

“They like you well enough,” Sokka reasons, gesturing to the reptile bird curled up in Zuko’s lap.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

It is what feels like hours before someone comes to their *room*. It's Guo, he looks tired, wet hair brushed back. His eyes go to Jaw Long, but not for long, returning to the two teens, “there’s food, come on.”

“What happened? Were you able to chase off the Pirates? Is everyone okay?” Sokka started with the questions, his nervousness showing. Guo just shook his head, a sign for the teen to stop. Zuko watched Sokka’s face, a cringe in dejection and then a transfer into concern, his eyes widening and then hardening, expecting the worst.

Soon they got to the deck, where much of the crew was gathered, eating with minimal conversation. The air was tight around them, and very few bothered to look up from their meal as the three arrived.

“Go back home,” Zuko whispers to Jaw Long, who was perched on his shoulder. The reptile bird tilts their head in question, or maybe just looking for confirmation, “go,” he urges. This time the bird takes off into the night sky.

It hadn’t been night when they’d gone below before, the sun was high and it was around midday. Zuko knew the sun had set, he had felt it, but it did feel odd that the day was gone. Why had they taken so long to get them? The sounds of fighting had stopped pretty quickly after they started, but neither of them dared to leave the room without permission. Now Zuko wanted to be the one asking what happened.

Dinner was a quiet affair, since no one around them was talking, they didn’t either, it seemed as though the silence wasn’t meant to be broken. The deck cleared of most people after a while and eventually Sokka gestured that they should leave too.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“I didn’t see Qianfan,” Sokka says the moment Zuko closes the door to their room. “Did you?”

“No.” Zuko shakes his head, letting out a shaky breath, Sokka echoes the action, they know what that means.

Jian hadn’t been there either, but there was the possibility he was in his quarters. For now they couldn’t assume, but it would make sense why it took so long to sort everything out, if the Captain had been killed. It seemed like he’d been Captain of the ship for ages, if he really had died, that would’ve caused quite the upset.

“We’ll figure more out in the morning, we should try to get some rest,” Zuko suggests, not wanting them to dwell on this topic. They didn’t have the time to mourn someone they barely knew, no matter the kindness they showed. They’d be off to port sometime tomorrow, getting back to traveling by foot rather than sitting around a ship would hopefully help.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

The ship made good time, getting into port just before midday. The crew had been nearly as quiet today as the day before, but Guo had been helpful enough to explain what happened. Jian and Qianfan had been killed in the fighting, as well as one of the pirates. As was the rule in the Earth Kingdom Navy, all three bodies were disposed of by being thrown overboard into the ocean. As the crew had been traveling along the mostly peaceful canal for a while now the sudden loss came as quite the shock. They only said goodbye to Guo before leaving soon after the ship docked.

“Should we stay in town for the night?” Sokka questions, breaking the silence they’d been stewing in since leaving the ship. When Zuko didn’t reply right away he spoke again, “we probably won’t get far before nightfall.”

Zuko pulled the pouch which Sokka knew carried coins, and seemed to be checking what they had, “okay, we have enough to stay.” Sokka took that as an end to the conversation, and started looking out for anything that seemed to be an inn. It didn’t take long considering they were in a port town, and by the number of boats that had been at the docks, a popular one.

They walked into the inn, the bottom floor was a restaurant, packed with travelers, bits of conversations overlapping each other, partly muted by the band playing on a small makeshift stage farther into the room. They made their way to the check-in counter, it was luckily close to the entrance so they didn’t have to weave through tables.

“Welcome to the Badger-Mole Bed and Breakfast!” The woman at the desk gave them a cheerful, bright smile. She was probably a bit older than them, and by her bright green eyes, likely an earthbender. “How can I help you?”

“A room, please. If you have any available, it looks pretty busy in here.” Sokka gives a light smile, glancing over the room for a moment.

“Our restaurant is very popular,” she agrees as she pulls out a key from somewhere behind the counter. “I’ll just need you to sign this book, your name and how long you’ll be staying, please.”

Sokka quickly jots down their names and that they plan to just stay the night, returning the book back to the woman.

“Perfect! Your room will be just up those stairs, third floor, second door on your right. That’ll be twenty copper pieces, we also accept other nations' currency if that works better.”

Zuko is quick to shuffle through his bag, pulling out a dark red bag before handing the larger bag to Sokka for the moment. He pulls out the equivalent in Fire Nation notes, now handing them to Sokka while taking back his bag. The innkeeper doesn’t blink at being handed Fire Nation currency, tucking it away behind the counter.

“I hope you two enjoy your stay.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

After tossing their bags into their room and a quick wash with the provided water in the room, they were back outside, not wanting to stay cooped up in a room after days spent mostly in what amounted to a closet. Knowing they’ll probably eat at the restaurant downstairs before they leave the next day they opt to find food elsewhere as the town is filled with market stalls and carts.

The streets were packed with people, half sentences and calls of names from the crowd was all that could really be made out. Children weaved in and out of the mess of people, chasing each other without much care. People on break for a midday meal line up at the various carts, both locals and sailors. locals are easy to spot by the fact that most of them have mixed colors in their clothing rather than the common earth tones of the Earth Kingdom. Reds and pinks are the most common addition, but Sokka notes the occasional blue. Living in a port tone likely gives them access to more variety in dyes or even premade clothes from other nations.

“Damn it,” Zuko suddenly curses, coming to a stop in front of Sokka, who promptly crashes into him. The firebender doesn’t even turn to him, instead he’s looking at a board with various flyers pinned to it. It doesn’t take long for Sokka to find what he’s looking at, there are two new posters of the both of them. Zuko’s poster is much closer in appearance to him now, rather than the ones before that were hardly recognizable as his current self. Sokka reaches past him, yanking down both posters, stuffing them in his tunic. “He’s alive,” Zuko whispers, and Sokka knows it is probably not meant to be heard, but he still nods.

The poster and the news of Zhao’s likely still alive status put a bit of a damper on their day. After finding whatever line for food wasn’t terribly long, which ended up being an actually pretty amazing soup dumplings, they headed back to the inn, now worried about the risk of being out in the port town with so many people.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Sokka laid in bed staring at the wooden ceiling above, eyes increasingly heavy as the hours ticked on but unable to get himself to fall asleep. Blowing out an annoyed sigh he let his gaze move from the ceiling to the second bed in the room, only a few feet from the one he was laying in. Blue eyes found the still frame of a firebender tucked under the provided covers and the two they use on their travels, the night had brought a rather harsh cold front, though it was nothing compared to home. Zuko’s back was to him, instead facing toward the door, Sokka watched as his shoulder moved slightly with his breaths.

He wasn’t sure how long he’d laid there, carefully trying to match the firebender’s breaths to see if it would help him sleep, the focus made him realise something was wrong instantly. Zuko’s shoulders tensed, his breaths becoming short gasps. Sokka waited to see if it would pass, not wanting to wake the other teen if it was unnecessary. He jumped from his bed as the firebender started tossing and turning, tangling himself into the mess of covers.

“Zuko,” Sokka spoke in a sort of whisper yell, as he stood beside the bed, debating if he should try to shake him awake. “Zuko, wake up.” He jumps back as Zuko’s eyes snap open, his fists clenched. Gold snaps to Sokka, gaze sharp, dangerous, terrified. Sokka holds himself from flinching at the sight, “you had a nightmare.”

The burning gaze cools, the firebender letting out a shaky breath. “Sorry, I didn’t mean to wake you.”

Sokka shakes his head, “you didn’t.”

The room falls into a heavy silence, Sokka sitting on the edge of his bed. Zuko doesn’t say anything as he gets up, going to his bag. After a few minutes he speaks up, “I’m gonna go get some air.”

He walks out before Sokka can offer to go with him.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Sokka’s once again laying with his eyes focused on the ceiling when the door opens, looking to the doorway the light from the hall illuminates behind Zuko, shadowing his face as he walks into the room. He pads past his bed, not saying anything as he sits on the edge of Sokka’s. The Water teen pulls back to blankets, a silent gesture which Zuko this time immediately accepts, sinking into the mattress as Sokka puts the covers over both of them.

### End Notes

## Chapter 16

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

I know I'm a day late again, sorry my dudes. Hopefully you guys like this chapter, I have a feeling you will...

### Text

They woke late in the morning, it was inconvenient, but they had needed the rest. Sokka couldn’t pull his gaze from Zuko as they got ready to go out, he hadn’t looked away from him for more than a few moments since he first saw his eyes that morning. Sokka had recognized those eyes, eyes he’d thought they left behind weeks ago. His stomach plummeted the moment he’d saw him, in a moment that they’d had so often shared these past several weeks that it almost felt odd to think he’d ever woken up without the first thing he sees in the morning being Zuko’s black hair, before the firebender eventually looks up at him. He didn’t have to ask about the nightmares when he saw those eyes, he knew Zuko wouldn’t talk about them. The eyes that had looked at him when they woke, the eyes of the Fire Prince that slowly moved around the room in a silence so quiet it was even odd for him, they were empty, empty in the way that they had been on that ship.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

The two never talked about what happened the night before, not a single time Zuko woke in a panic, waking Sokka each time, and not when they woke up that morning. He seemed to understand, or at least be resigned to the fact that Zuko didn’t want to talk about whatever his nightmares were about. Zuko didn’t want to think about it, let alone *talk*, so he was entirely fine with Sokka not mentioning any of it. They originally had wanted to leave as early as possible, but after the night they had they ended up sleeping in rather late, and had never gone to pick up more supplies before the long trip they’d have through the Earth Kingdom’s largest forest. So now it was after midday and they were just getting to going out for supplies.

The weather was less than favorable, a light rain constant with low rolls of thunder in the distance. Despite it not being great for walking around town, it did mean the streets were quieter, most people hiding out inside, so less of a chance that either of them would be caught based on their wanted posters. Port towns were hotspots for bounty hunters, and neither of their bounties were something to scoff at, beyond the possibility of danger to one's life that it implied. Zuko was just glad the Blue Spirit wasn’t mentioned on the new posters, maybe Zhao didn’t know, his belongings didn’t seem to be tampered with when he got them back, not that it helped greatly, his face was nearly as recognisable as the wooden mask.

It was arguably dangerous for either of them to stay in town any longer than they had to, more time to see them, see where they were going, or even overhear them talking. The longer they stay in one place the more likely it would be that someone would be able to point anyone who came asking in the right direction. Their supply run would have to be quick and discreet, keep from pulling attention.

&#95;&#95;&#95;

“You’ve got to be kidding me…” Sokka mumbles the moment he sees the recognisable mop of brown hair. Zuko hadn’t seen him yet, and frankly he didn’t exactly feel like *him* seeing Zuko, so he grabbed the firebender’s hand, pulling him down a side street to hopefully avoid the homicidal teen.

“What–” “Jet, I saw him and some of his group.”

Zuko looked confused for a moment, as if he forgot who Jet was. Sokka almost laughed at the thought, knowing it would certainly hurt Jet’s giant ego to know someone he had captive barely remembered his name. Sadly, at least in Sokka’s opinion, it didn’t take long for realisation to dawn on him. “What would they be doing here?”

“Probably trying to steal blasting jelly or something, not our problem. I just don’t want him yelling about you being a spy or something with a bunch of Earth Kingdom sailors around town,” Sokka reasons, starting to walk down the new street. Zuko went along without complaint, only giving a hum of agreement as they went. Truth was, he hadn’t really thought what he would do about people who know the both of them, he especially didn’t think what he’d do if they ran into Jet. He tried to keep Jet out of his head as much as possible, because of the split second expression Zuko had the first time they’d talked about him, even though he said it wasn’t about Jet, for some reason Sokka couldn’t separate the two.

Sokka knew if they got into an actual fight with Jet here that they would be the ones blamed, even if he started it. He didn’t trust them to care who was actually right or wrong, the two of them aren’t Earth, and that very well could be enough. It would be best to avoid the three, if they didn’t want to end up arrested, or worse, at the wrong end of a boulder.

&#95;&#95;&#95;

Sokka’s expression didn’t soften as they got further away from Jet, eyes set in a glare as they walked. Zuko looked from his face to their still connected hands, but after a moment of thought decided not to do anything about it. He didn’t mind the way their hands felt entwined. Zuko couldn’t remember the last time he’d held someone’s hand, it was a simple thing, but something about it felt so *important.* For a moment someone else appears in his mind, but he hadn’t been one for holding hands, quickly he pulls away from the memory.

“You’re really not a fan of his, huh?”

“He flooded an entire village, would’ve killed hundreds,” Sokka says, sounding as though he’s expecting an argument, Zuko didn’t plan on giving him one, not this time. He’d been trying to avoid arguing with Sokka so much, something about it felt off. He didn’t have the capacity to try to figure out why arguing with the Water teen made him feel like his inner fire would go out, especially when he’s never been known for a cool head, but it had started feeling so wrong that he wanted to avoid it as best he could.

“I’m not saying I *like* the guy. Capturing me kinda put a wrench in any possible favor I might’ve had,” Zuko reasons.

“What, you would’ve if he *hadn’t* captured you?” Sokka bites out.

Zuko stops walking, causing Sokka to come to a halt as well, “don’t do that.” He feels the way his voice wants to shake. Why is he so upset by this? What is wrong with him? Why does he care so much about what Sokka thinks? Arguing, or even just yelling had been so commonplace for Zuko, and yet now the idea of it made a pit in his stomach.

“Do what?” Sokka asks, his voice suddenly not as harsh, his eyes aren’t focused on Zuko’s, but instead their hands.

“Whenever you’re mad you try to start an argument,” Zuko explains, trying to keep his voice steady.

“Sokka?”

They both turn at the voice, and as Zuko goes to pull his hand away, Sokka tightens his grasp. He’s not sure if the other boy even realises it, as his eyes are entirely focused on Jet and the two others with him. Zuko doesn’t remember either of their names, not that they were exactly jumping at introducing themselves that last time they came across each other. None of the three were focused on him at all though, and Zuko found that he was grateful for the dark skies, and his hair covering much of his scar now. Could they make it through this without him being recognised?

“Are Katara and Aang with you?” Jet speaks again when the only response he gets is a glare.

“We split up at the North Pole, I’m heading south to meet up with my dad,” he gives a very truncated explanation.

Zuko turned to look at Sokka, his burned side full away from the three now. He lightly squeezes Sokka’s hand, trying to get him to calm down, because he can see the anger in his eyes. It isn’t the burning kind of anger, that flames up and fizzles out quickly, no, it’s cold, the kind that freezes you in place. From how quiet Jet becomes Zuko thinks he may have noticed, now trying to choose his next words more carefully.

“We’re going to Ba Sing Se.” Zuko notices his eyes flicker to him, almost seeming like he's looking for someone to back up what he’s saying, why he thinks someone that he doesn’t know would, Zuko’s not sure. “I lost sight of what really mattered, back in the forest. I was supposed to help, I wanted to help.” And Zuko understands what is going on, with the way Jet makes his voice sound less confident, pleading. He’d seen it before, he grew up surrounded by people like this, it was manipulation. Zuko had to admit it was almost good, Jet’s sorrowful act was nearly believable. But then there were the two behind him, it was quick, just a short glance, a silent conversation, just the fact that it happened at all was enough. Jet had expected Sokka to fold, to forgive him, maybe even to help him in some way, he didn’t truly believe what he’d tried to do was too far. Zuko had been manipulated so much of his life, Agni, he was pretty sure Azula could show up now and he’d be more likely to believe her than Jet, but Sokka was different, would he see through it? Without Zuko conceding to it, it was more likely.

“Lost sight… you wiped out a village Jet. You destroy hundreds of people’s homes! You would have killed them! You *lost sight*, I think it was a bit more than that. You wanted vengeance, and you didn’t care who got hurt for you to get it!” Sokka’s yelling sounds almost like scolding disappointment, nearly like he wants to try to reason with Jet, convince him, but knows it is likely futile.

“Sokka, it isn’t worth it. Let’s just go,” Zuko tries to urge quietly, as now they are drawing attention, something that neither of them should be doing. He flicks his gaze to Jet, who’s false sorrow has burned away, flaming anger replacing it, which Zuko wouldn’t comment on the irony of. He can’t hear the two behind him, but with the way the smaller of the two pulls at his arm it seems they’re trying to get him to leave as well. The firebender almost thinks they’ll manage it, until Jet rips his arm from her, nearing Sokka and him instead. His eyes are once again focused on Sokka, who meets Jet’s anger with his own. Zuko is smart enough to keep his head somewhat down, allowing bangs to shadow his features, luckily being the Jet is a bit taller, it blocks him more from view up close.

“The Fire Nation is a stain on this land, all they do is destroy! It was one village to save multiple, and instead the Fire Nation still has control there, because of you! We were hunted down because of what you did, we had to abandon the forest because of you!” Jet retorts in a booming yell, looking down at Sokka. Zuko can’t see his expression well with the way he tries to keep himself unnoticed, but he can feel the contempt. He can feel more eyes on them, the quiet street suddenly feeling very busy.

“How many towns would it have been? How many people would you have killed?” Sokka retorts, not yelling now, but biting like frost in the winter.

“As many as it takes to rid us of those ashmakers, it's an honorable sacrifice.”

Zuko stifled a laugh, a scoff escaping his lips. It was a bitter sort of laugh, the kind that comes out when a sick sort of realisation hits you. He can feel the gaze on him now, not just Jet’s, but Sokka’s too. He curses that he can’t look to see Sokka without revealing himself, his blind side to his companion. He doesn’t notice it, as Jet is further toward his left side, but he tilts himself down slightly to find his gaze, and Zuko knows when he sees him it’s too late.

The taller teen rises, taking a step back, echoing the kind laugh that tried to escape Zuko a moment ago, “oh I get it now,” he sneers, “it was you then.” He expected an attack, not more talking, but this was preferable. “So what? You meet a pretty ashmaker and suddenly they’re not all that bad?”

Zuko nearly mocked him for clearly thinking he hadn’t escaped on his own, considering he hadn’t exactly made it difficult to do, but Sokka spoke up before he could.

“We didn’t know each other then.” It’s a lie, but only sort of, and Zuko isn’t about to refute the comment right now. “And this isn’t about him, you weren’t just attacking soldiers Jet, you were attacking civilians.”

“They’re Fire Nation, Sokka! They’re all the same! How long till your little pet ashmaker burns you? You think because you *saved* him he’s loyal to you. They’re killers. All. Of. Them.”

And Jet must have been real confident, because he was too distracted to see what was coming. He was laid out on the floor, sent there by what was possibly a knuckle shattering punch from Sokka. They were both pulled from their focus on the now bleeding Jet when shouts come from one end of the small street. “Okay, time to go.” Sokka starts running without any more warning, Zuko quick to match him so he doesn’t end up being pulled behind.

“Go left, we’ll get back to the inn quicker,” Zuko directs, having walked a decent amount of the town the night before. Sokka follows his word without question, both of them quickening their pace for fear that whatever form of law the town has would now be after them.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Zuko nearly slams the door behind them when they get into their room. Both of them sink to the floor against the door, trying to catch their breath.

“Your– hand– okay?” Zuko manages to ask between breaths.

Sokka takes a long few moments to respond, “I’m offended– you– don’t think– I know– how to hit– someone.”

“Sorry,” he responds, not trying to sound all that sincere as Sokka doesn’t actually seem offended.

“It’s fine. Definitely better than Jet’s face.” Sokka lets out a breathy laugh, and Zuko finds himself joining in.

After a while their laughter dies down, “you surprised me,” Zuko comments, and it’s not a lie. Sokka had been angry, of course he was, but it wasn’t lost on the firebender that Sokka had been resigned to use his words until it was *him* that Jet was talking about.

“Had to defend your honor.”

### End Notes

## Chapter 17

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

you guys are gonna fucking hate me after this chapter... or you'll love me. I'm actually not sure tbh

### Text

“We’re gonna have to pay for another night,” Zuko comments as he wraps Sokka’s bruising knuckles. It looked like Jet had gone down hard, so he wasn’t particularly surprised by his injured hand. Zuko wasn’t exactly one for punching people, and generally it didn’t seem Sokka was either, they both have weapons they fight with, but that wasn’t a fight, it was a reaction, a response. He holds the bandaged hand in both of his own, gaze focused on it rather than on Sokka who he could feel looking at him, apparently distracted enough by something that he wasn’t listening to Zuko. “Thank you,” he managed to mumble, before releasing Sokka’s hand.

“What?” Sokka suddenly questions a belated few moments after Zuko got up from his spot on the bed.

“I’m going to pack so we can leave quickly. We should get a couple hours of rest and leave before sunrise,” Zuko replies, picking up a few things lying about as he gets his bag.

“You’re not worried about them coming to search for us?”

“If we go now we’ll be seen trying to leave town, things will probably calm down by tonight.” Zuko tosses the packed bags on one of the beds, where they’d already left their weapons when they returned.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“I’m gonna distract them.”

“You’re gonna what?! How? We’ll just find another way out of town.”

“It’s almost sunrise if we wait, people will be around and we’ll be caught for sure.”

“I’m not just gonna leave you, Zuko.”

Zuko isn’t listening to him, instead he’s rummaging through his bag, apparently looking for something. After a long while he pulls out a mask, the *Blue Spirit* mask. He pulls off the bag it had been in, handing it to Sokka while securing the one that is still slung across his torso.

“No. No way. You are not doing this.”

Zuko pauses for a moment, eyes narrowing as he just slightly tilts his head as if he’s confused about something, “you knew,” he comments in a slightly surprised whisper.

“About you being the Blue Spirit? I figured it out a while ago, well I didn’t know for sure, but Aang mentioned that the Blue Spirit helped him out, and you seemed to know more about Aang than made sense. Plus you both fight with Dao blades, and you keep a book of Love Among the Dragons in your pack. This isn’t the point. You can’t distract them, there’s like five guys there.” Sokka gestures to the group gathered at one of the gates out of town.

“I’ll be fine. Just wait until they’re distracted and slip past, I’ll meet you in the forest.” Zuko pulls the mask down covering his face and without giving Sokka a chance to try to convince him not to throw himself into a fight heads straight for the guards.

“Aren’t I supposed to be the plan guy?” he mumbles to himself, watching as the guards notice the Blue Spirit upon them. He can’t see the guard’s faces clearly in the dark but based on how slow they were to act, they didn’t think the Blue Spirit was a possible enemy. Sokka couldn’t blame them for being surprised as generally the Blue Spirit attacked Fire Nation Soldiers or stole from those much richer than what guards having to work in the mild of the night would be. The shock combined with what Sokka had to assume, based on his limited swordsmanship knowledge, was quite a bit of skill, meant the fight didn’t last long, three of the five down before they seemed to realise what was going on. Sokka had to snap himself out of his distracted state, as the Blue Spirit led the guards further away from the gate, leaving the three down behind. He did a quick check for anyone before dashing through the gate, only pausing a moment to glance back in the hope that Zuko was already behind him, he wasn’t. Sokka shook away the worry, racing into the treeline before anyone was able to spot him.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Zuko was careful not to permanently damage anyone, well at least not too much, who's to say what a decent knock to the head with the hilt of his blade would do. The first three were simple, the group clearly surprised by his presence as well as his plan to attack them. The Blue Spirit mask was not common for him, really the major story came from what happened to the Pohuai Stronghold. He’d actually only dawned the mask four other times, including tonight, many reports of the Blue Spirit seemed to be a way to save face when someone fucked up. It was easier to blame a faceless vigilante than take responsibility for failure. It didn’t matter much to Zuko though, let people believe what they want, if anything people’s hesitation made his job easier.

The last two took longer, both earth benders, but he’d fought harder battles, so eventually the two fell into a sudden nap after harsh blows to the head. He didn’t waste any time, running back toward the gates, making it through just as he started to see some movement from the three he’d knocked out before. He pulled off the mask, stuffing it into his bag despite the bag being quite full as it hadn't been the one it was in before. No time to stop any longer he raced into the forest, only realising then that he hadn’t been very specific where they should meet.

Zuko found himself flat on the ground, someone over him. His vision blurred from the impact, his first instinct was fire, immediately going to shoot fire at the person on top of him. As if they knew it would be his reaction he felt fingers around his wrists, holding his hands away before he could do damage.

“It’s me Zuko!” Sokka's voice came from the blurry figure and Zuko suddenly realised he hadn’t been breathing, a heavy breath gasping out. Slowly Sokka’s face came into view over him.

“Spirits Sokka,” he sighed out in relief, having assumed the worst. It was a few moments later that he remembered what he’d almost done, gold eyes widening as he looked up at him. *Fuck*.

“What? Are you hurt? Did something happen?” Sokka scrambled off of him, pulling him to sit on the way up, blue eyes searching over him for injuries.

“I–” Zuko felt like everything had crumbled, he’d nearly done it, done exactly as Jet had said, exactly what was expected of him, he defaulted to fire, in his fear he nearly burned someone who trusts him, “almost burned you.”

Sokka was silent for a long moment, bewilderment taking over his expression, eyebrows knitted together, face scrunched in confusion, “that’s what you’re worried about?”

“My first instinct was to burn you,” and he could hear how his voice shook at those words. If he burned someone who trusted him was he any better than the rest of his family? Than his father? Than Zhao? How could he say he wasn’t like them if the moment he believed he was in any real danger lost it and went straight for causing irreparable damage to someone? He almost *burned* Sokka. *How can I know you won’t burn me?* Words he’d long since forced from his memory now weigh on him.

“Zuko, what are you talking about? Of course it was. You clearly didn’t even know who I was, you thought you were being attacked.” Sokka’s voice is fast, like he needs to get the words out as quickly as possible.

“You’re supposed to be able to trust me.”

“I do trust you.”

“But I almost burned you!” Zuko yells, feeling the way his fingertips tingle at the energy wanting to be released. What in Agni’s name is wrong with him? Why can’t he get over whatever is making him feel this way? *You're scary when you look at me like that.* The voice echoes in his mind.

“Zuko why are you upset about this, you did what anyone would have done.”

And Zuko can’t explain it to him, because what is he supposed to tell Sokka? He’s not stupid, it's not his first crush, but *this* is so different. He knows it would’ve been an accident, he wouldn’t knowingly hurt Sokka, not now, but the possibility of it eats him. The idea of doing anything that might make him be what people expect, what Sokka expected in the beginning, is absolutely terrifying. Being good is hard, and frankly he’s not sure he’s good so much as just not as bad as he could be. Zuko *knows* it’s more than a crush; a crush is him realising Sensu is cute, a crush is kissing a visiting noble boy when he was thirteen, a crush is his quiet feelings for one of his crew, and a crush is stuttering over yourself on ember island trying to talk to some boy he can’t even remember the name of now. No, this isn’t a crush, but he can’t say what it *is.* So crush will have to do, and he absolutely can’t tell Sokka because he knows how it will end, how it ended the last time.

He had to get over this, to brush off the fear that creeps up when he feels how cold Sokka’s hands are as the Water Tribe teen takes his hands to try to get his attention. The fear that burns deep when he sees depths of worry in Sokka’s gaze, “you’re burning up.” And one hand lets go, instead feeling his forehead, his cheek, his neck, stopping there with blue eyes still holding his gaze.

“We need to keep moving for a bit, but we can’t travel long, you’ve got a fever.”

“I’m a firebender, Sokka, we run warm,” and Zuko knows it’s a lie hidden in a truth, because he’s never ran as warm as other firebenders, and he can feel himself that he’s freezing and burning at the same time.

“I’ve slept in the same bed as you how many times now? I know how warm you run,” Sokka retorts in a sort of muttered scolding as he unhooks his water pouch from his belt, offering it to Zuko. “Drink while we walk, we’ll stop whenever we hit water, rest for a bit and then keep going if you can.”

“I’ll be fine,” Zuko sighs, but takes the water.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

They continue south, and after several hours it becomes obvious that they need to stop despite having not come across a water source, odd considering the density of the forest. They don’t have a map detailing the forest, so finding water that way isn’t an option. Their pace is getting slower and slower and as it nears midday it is getting more likely that they’ll have to stop before finding water.

“I think we might be walking parallel to the river,” Zuko comments, stopping to lean against a tree. His breaths shallow, cheeks flushed. Sokka hums in though looking around them for a sign of water, he’d been so focused on making sure Zuko didn’t keel over behind him that he hadn’t been paying a great amount of attention to their direction beyond making sure they were going south still. Crouching to the forest floor he looks for any noticeable dips in the terrain, most of the area is flat, the lack of any major slope making it hard to be sure, but he notices a slight slope to the west.

“We’ll try going west a bit, if we don’t see water soon it’ll be best if we stop for a while.” Sokka waits for Zuko to acknowledge he’s said anything, but his eyes are closed, and he looks like he might fall over. He carefully shakes Zuko to get him to pay attention, “stay with me, I’ll help you walk, come on.” Sokka takes the other teen’s arm, putting it around his shoulder while he locks his own arm around his waist to try to keep him steady.

It only takes a few minutes of walking west that they find a river cutting through the forest, the water is slow moving and clear, probably safe to stay by. He helps Zuko to the ground, his skin tingling from the heat that was coming off the firebender. He pulls out one of their bed rolls and the blankets first, laying this out next to Zuko, “get some sleep. I think we should stop for the day, you’re too sick to keep going.” He doesn’t get a response beyond Zuko slowly moving to the bedroll and climbing under the covers shivering despite the high sun and the heat coming off of him.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Sokka's eyes blink open, looking up at the dark sky through the trees, he sighs, trying to figure out what woke him. He looks down to find Zuko still asleep, mop of black hair splayed across his chest. Some snaps in the distance, probably a branch. He peers into the darkness where the noise came from, hearing more footsteps but not seeing anyone. “Zuko, wake up,” Sokka whispers, shaking him despite the usual outcome of doing so. The firebender barely reacts, only shifting so he’s no longer laying on him. The Water Tribesman is up quickly, keeping his movement quiet as he hears footsteps suddenly stop, though certainly still not upon them. “Zuko get up, we need to go now,” Sokka whisper-yells, pulling the blankets from the firebender and stuffing them in the bag they usually go in. He quickly starts rolling his bedroll as Zuko slowly wakes.

Gold eyes slowly gain focus, looking at Sokka, “what are you doing?” he grumbles quietly. He moves off the bedroll as Sokka pulls at it, rapidly tucking away the two rolls in the same bag as their blankets. Luckily Sokka had foregone the tent, as it was warm enough out, and there hadn’t looked to be rain. Zuko looks toward the noise of footsteps as well as voices, and Sokka notices his eyes widen, as though he recognises something. He’s up quickly, grabbing Sokka’s shoulder to steady himself. “Fire Nation,” he whispers, and Sokka isn’t sure how he knows but if anyone would it’s Zuko. Sokka hands him the lighter bags, knowing he probably shouldn’t even be up.

Zuko gestures to head north, and as much as it sucks to backtrack the steps of coming from the southeast. As always the firebender’s steps are silent across the forest floor, Sokka attempting to match him as quietly as he can. He moves slower than usual, good for keeping up while staying quiet, not so much for keeping ahead. He points west in Zuko’s view, as the river turns toward the west, and following along it will at least keep their direction consistent in the dark. He nods in agreement and they keep to the river, following it as quickly as they can, the footsteps begin to fade as they move. Zuko gestures to a large tree that had fallen over, crashed against another tree creating an alcove covered by its branches and trunk, it should be enough cover in the dark of night. They make their way into the small covering, bags gathered between them. The footsteps started again, close but not so much they’d likely be seens, but they were getting consistently louder. Zuko pulled their muted green blanket from their pack, wrapping it around them, only pointing to Sokka when he gave him a questioning look. He realised a few minutes later that he’d meant his blue clothes, which would stand out much more against the dark trees than the green blanket would.

They don’t move as the footsteps get closer, knowing they were close enough that any movement could get them caught. Sokka could catch the soft glow of someone holding fire for light as they walked, and he could only pray to Tui and La that they wouldn’t be caught in the glow. As the footsteps began to fade once again, heading north but not west, Sokka calmed, only then realising that Zuko was leaning against him. Blue eyes, having well adjusted to the dark by now, focused on the firebender, who’d slumped against him, gold eyes closed. He found himself tucking away black hair to look at him, shockingly the movement not causing him to stir. A smile tugged at his lips, as he carefully shifted them so he was lent against the still standing tree, and Zuko was still tucked into his side.

Looking up at the moon through the trees he came to a realisation, and it felt like waves crashing against a rocky shore, frozen water blasting them. His gaze moved from the moon back to Zuko, a soft sigh leaving his lips. What was he gonna do now? Now that he understood what was going on. He couldn’t tell him, and he knew now that this was going to end horribly.

### End Notes

## Chapter 18

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

you may have noticed an addition to the tags... nothing bad luckily lol

no long note here, enjoy the chapter

### Text

“We really need a more detailed map,” Sokka sighed, trying to figure out where they should be going, but the forest on their map is essentially just a blob of trees, not a single town listened, or even the river they are walking along. If they were going to find a town the river would probably be their best option. Zuko seemed to be doing better today, quiet as he often was, but at least not looking as though he’d fall over any moment, but Sokka would still feel more comfortable if they managed to find a town to spend a day or two in. “Where do you think those soldiers were heading last night?”

“I don’t know of any occupied towns within the forest, and we haven’t come across anything that looks like a battle took place. I’m not sure, not exactly up to date on Fire Nation war plans.” Zuko shrugs, stopping for a moment to fill the water skin from the river. “Maybe a supplier is out here, sometimes they meet people who are willing to supply companies too far from the colonies.”

“Willing seems unlikely,” Sokka notes, remembering what Teko had explained when they found out he was creating weapons for the Fire Nation.

“It depends,” Zuko says simply, and Sokka knows he still feels off because he would usually explain what he means. He thinks about Lao though, who didn’t seem to care much about the outcome of the war, and while he was delivering to the Earth Kingdom Navy then who's to say he wouldn’t also supply the Fire Nation.

“Any idea where in the forest we are?”

“I didn’t hit the forest last time, I caught a ride with a family going around the great divide. They told me to avoid the forest because of bandits,” Zuko comments with a shrug.

“Bandits. And you just thought to mention this now?” In Zuko’s defense, he’d been sleeping or very much out of it that entire time, Sokka’s brain supplied automatically. He didn’t mention the Great Divide, really hoping there was a way they could get around that, he wasn’t sure there would be anyone to bring them through.

“It has been months, I doubt they’re still around.”

“I wish I had your optimism.”

And Zuko actually laughs at the comment, and Sokka ends up joining in after realising what he’d said, because they both know how much of a joke that is. And Sokka watches in between laughs, the way he covers his mouth as his laugh and, how Zuko’s eyes scrunch showing enough emotion that you don’t have to see the smile that comes with it. He watches too as the laughter dies down, the forest’s noises taking over, and he’s less pleased seeing how the light in Zuko’s eyes is temporary, having vanished as he came back to their reality.

Sokka wondered how he looks to Zuko, what emotions he can see that Sokka tries to hide, just how Zuko tries to hide the emptiness. He wants to know if the firebender spends as much time trying to understand his reactions as he does for his. And he worries if Zuko can read him, can he see how twisted up he is inside, can he see his guilt, his sorrow, and can he see his love? Or do his eyes look empty as Zuko’s often do? Has he adapted the same sort of mask or can his emotions be read clearly?

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“Can I see the map?” Zuko asks suddenly as the forest begins to become more sparse, breaking the silence that Sokka had decided not to fill. Sokka hands over the map, coming to a stop as Zuko does. The firebender lays out the map over a flat enough rock, clearly looking for something, “There,” he points to what looks to be a lake a bit south of the forest, “Poyang, it’s one of the largest lakes in the Earth Kingdom. I think we’ve been following the river that flows into it.”

“Okay…” Sokka says in the sort of way that he’s trying to make it obvious he needs more information.

“The people I traveled with last time, They work in a town on the west side of the lake. It isn’t well known I guess, mostly a high-class vacation spot for Earth Kingdom nobility and people high up in their military,” Zuko explains.

“Hate to break this to you Zuko, but we are neither of those things.”

Zuko shook his head, rolling up the map before handing it back to Sokka, “obviously. That won’t be an issue though, it wasn’t all that uncommon to see people who are part Fire there.”

“What, like that town up north?” Sokka tries to get any real answer about this place, because right now it seems like they’ll be walking themselves into getting arrested.

“No. It’s not some idealistic town where the nations still get along,” his tone snaps slightly, but quickly returns to normal, “My point is that people are used to seeing people who don’t look Earth there. You might come as a bit of a surprise, but there was some Northern family living there when I passed through.”

And Sokka felt like Zuko wasn’t exactly explaining everything, a sort of pit forming in his stomach. He followed Zuko anyways, going along the river. They still weren’t exactly close to the town yet.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“We should go in the morning,” Zuko comments as the lake comes into view just after the sun lowered below the horizon. And Sokka wasn’t sure how confusing this was going to get because so far Zuko had seemed to prefer sneaking into places at night rather than walking in broad daylight.

Sokka listened though, the two of them stopping for the night at the northernmost part of the lake, it would probably take till midday to get to the town, as he couldn’t even see the end of the lake from where they were. After getting their tent set and a fire going, because Zuko said there wasn't likely to be anyone around to see it, they sat down for rice and dried fish.

“So how exactly are you expecting us to get into town? If it’s a bunch of nobles and high-ranking military there’s bound to be a lot of security,” Sokka breaches the subject, hoping for an actual answer.

Zuko rummages through his bag for a while before eventually pulling out a rather official looking envelope, green with the Earth Kingdom crest. Opening it he pulls out what looks similar to an Earth Kingdom passport, Sokka had only seen one once. “The family I was traveling with works for the Beifong family, it was their daughter’s birthday while I was in town, she gave me this just encase,” he held up the paper, embellished with a gold flying boar.

“Are you gonna tell me you flirted with some Earth Kingdom noble girl?”

“Agni no! She’s twelve!” Zuko yelled, looking like he might throw up. “They were on vacation in town, they’re the noble house of Gaoling in the south.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

*A few months ago*

It had been an unexpected encounter, when trying to find a way around the Great Divide, rather than through it. He’d been sitting looking at his frankly less than helpful map, when a large wagon stopped a few feet from him. A woman looked down from her spot holding the ostrich horse raines. He was surprised to find amber eyes when he finally looked up, her dark brown hair pulled into a low bun. Almost immediately after she stopped two girls, probably no older than eleven popped their heads out to see what was going on, they looked rather similar to her, and nearly identical to each other.

“Fire or Earth?” she questioned simply.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“You should rest for a few days, until we find you someone who’s heading north of the city,” Nara comments as gates come into view on the horizon. “I’ll say you’re my younger brother, they won’t care, Poyang is always looking for new hands,” she explains further.

“I didn’t know there was a city by Poyang Lake,” he comments, as the town wasn’t listed on maps, instead only the lake.

“Well kept secret. There are large estates along the west side of the lake, vacation homes for Earth Kingdom Nobility and Military Officials. The city is mostly those who work for the families when they are in town and take care of the estates when they are gone. I work for the Beifong family, they’re from Gaoling in the south.”

“No problems not being Earth?” Zuko questions.

“Not when mostly everyone in town is at the very least part Fire. They won’t hire you to work for them where someone can see, but this war keeps most of them rich, they don’t care that we’re Fire for the most part. Anyone there is a traitor, enough Earth that the colonies wouldn’t be a good place to live for them, or enough Fire that most won’t hire them.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

After helping deliver the supplies for the Beifongs’ daughter's birthday party, he was able to explore the town neighboring the estates. The town was mostly how Nara had said, and Zuko was shocked to see so many people who looked Fire. It was a bit unnerving, not knowing if anyone here may know who he really is. He feels something shift under him, quickly jumping away to catch his balance, watching as the earth settles back into place. The moments he touches down there’s movement again. “Who’s there?” he calls out, light enough on his feet to avoid the attempt to trip him. They were likely trying to be subtle enough to go unnoticed by people other than him. When the earth moves this time he doesn’t avoid it, and instead of trying to trip him it pushes him toward a side street.

“You’re the Beifongs’ daughter,” he comments, slightly surprised to see her out in town.

“My name’s Toph,” she comments, her tone seemingly annoyed, despite the fact that he hadn’t been told her name.

“Okay…”

“We need to talk,” and she turns away from him, walking further down the street, clearly expecting him to follow. He complies, despite the confusion, as he doesn’t want Nara to lose her job because he pisses off the young noble. Eventually she stops, and he realises why quickly as he observes the area, no one is around. He decides not to question how a blind girl knows this part of the street is empty. “Your name isn’t Zuli.”

“I know it is an uncommon name but—”

“You also aren’t Nara’s brother.”

“She might not have mentioned me before–”

“I can tell that you’re lying. What I don’t know is why. Are you threatening her? Making her lie for you? You move like her, meaning you’re probably a firebender, so why would you?” Toph keeps a sturdy stance, and Zuko knows she’s prepared to attack him if he says the wrong thing. And if this ends up in a fight he’s at the luckiest going to be arrested, because she’s a noble and as far as anyone here is concerned he’s some Fire Nation deserter.

“Nara doesn’t know my real name. No, we are not siblings. I’m not threatening her, I just needed a ride north.” He opts for the truth, while not entirely believing what she says, he knows he’s never been a good liar.

“What’s your name?”

“Li.” He answers simply, and he has the jump out of the way of a rock being pelted at him.

“Try again. I told you I can tell you are lying. Don’t underestimate me.”

“I go by Li usually.” Another rock comes his way, and even though she’s kept her moves small he can see the skill behind her movements, he narrowly dodges, though he suspects he only manages to because the rocks are a threat not an attack. “Fine, it’s Zuko.” No rocks come flying this time.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“Zuli,” Nara calls from the entryway of her apartment, he answers by appearing because he was already up, he’d been stressed all day that Toph would say something. “The Beifongs’ daughter had a final dress fitting in town, they’re requesting that someone accompany her, but all of us are busy preparing for the party tonight.”

“You want me to go? They’re okay with that?”

“They trust me, and I vouched for you, so yes. Will you?”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“Earth to Zuko, are you even listening?” Toph’s voice sounds, catching Zuko off guard because he hadn’t heard her talking over the other people in the street.

“Sorry, I didn’t hear you,” he comments, moving so his right is to her instead.

“I’m not exactly quiet,” she retorts. He’s rather certain she thinks he’d purposely ignored her.

“I’m deaf on my left side, it was too loud to hear you,” he explains simply.

“Oh. I didn’t know.”

He notes what seems like relief in her voice, and he realises now why she’d sounded so annoyed. She’s used to being handled, and just like he did, she hates it. He stifles a laugh as her voice interrupts his thoughts.

“Were you born without it?”

“No. I was burned a few years ago, lost the hearing in my left ear and most of my sight in that eye.”

“I’ve never been able to see, well not like you at least. I can feel movement, with my earthbending.”

“That’s how you knew the street was empty yesterday.”

A proud smile forms on her lips, “and how I could tell you were lying.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“Ugh! That sucked! I mean really I’m not a baby, they don’t have to talk to me like one,” Toph complains, kicking a rock as they walk.

“To be fair you’re younger than my little sister soooo…” he comments hoping she can hear the smile in his voice and tell he’s joking. “Ow!” He rubs his arm, “it was a joke.”

“I know,” she smiles. They walk in silence for a little while before Toph speaks again, “so you actually have a sister. Where is she?”

“Ah…” he looks around checking if anyone is paying attention to them, but the streets are quiet the closer they get to the lake and estates, “she’s back in the Fire Nation.” He realised belatedly that maybe Toph thinks he’s not actually from the Fire nation, but she doesn’t seem surprised by the answer.

“Yeah, I guess she would be, being Princess and all.”

Zuko stops, staring at Toph as she continues a few paces in front of him before finally stopping too, “I am the daughter of nobles. I’m their only child so even if they think I’m weak and helpless I’m still the Beifong heir. I might think all this high society stuff is dumb, but I have learned about world leaders. So, Prince Zuko, what are you doing in the Earth Kingdom, pretending to be a servant?”

“I’m not allowed home,” he admits simply, not really wanting to get into the whole story.

“So that whole story is true then? About you being disowned.”

“How—“ Zuko is unsure how to keep speaking, it feels like walls are squeezing in around him. What would happen now? Would she tell someone? Would Nara and her daughters be hurt because of him? Would he be killed, he knew logically that eventually yes, but first they’d get what information they could. After a moment Toph’s voice filters back in.

“—rumor at some party a few years ago. Supposedly the Fire Nation Prince had betrayed his country and was punished for it. I wasn’t sure how common the name Zuko is in the Fire Nation, but then you mentioned your burn and now your sister. It wasn’t hard to put together”

“You’re the first person to figure it out, I’ve stayed in an Earth Kingdom military camp, met the Chief of the Southern Watertribe, and to them I was just some kid.”

“Well you can’t lie to me, also how are you even still alive? A military camp? Seriously?”

“Well neither time was exactly by choice.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“It was nice meeting you Toph,” Zuko comments, actually meaning it, as she seems like a slightly more sane little sister. Still mildly violent tendencies like Azula, but not cruel.

“You’re not so bad yourself Zuzu,” she grins, punching him in the arm. He's pretty sure he’ll have a bruise on his arm for weeks with how many times she’s punched him in the past few days. She proceeds to pull out an envelope, handing it to Zuko. “Don’t open it now, but that’s for emergencies or for if you return here. It will get you past the guards.”

He’s almost certain he knows what it is, and he knows she says not to open it because giving him something like this is rather unprecedented, especially considering who he is. He tucks the envelope into his tunic, “Thank you. I hope I won’t have to use it, but it is appreciated.”

“So formal, I forgot I was talking to a Prince for a bit but then you act all diplomatic. Lighten up Zuzu, hopefully next time you see me it’ll be at Earth Rumble Six kicking some ass,” she grins, letting out a boisterous laugh. After a few moments her expression calms, “come down to Gaoling after your trip up north, yeah?”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“Wait, so she knows who you are?!?” Sokka nearly yells in shock after the story.

“I mean, she found out, and I couldn’t lie to her. She’s the only one who knows exactly who I am, well besides you. Sensu thinks I’m some noble’s kid, but he doesn’t know the actual truth,” Zuko explains, and he doesn’t miss how Sokka’s face seems to scrunch when he mentions the Earth Kingdom soldier. “It’s not like I wanted to lie–”

“It isn’t that,” Sokka interrupts. Zuko waits for him to explain further but he doesn’t, leaving the firebender to wonder what exactly is going on in the Water teen’s head now. “We should get some sleep.”

### End Notes

## Chapter 19

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Could I have put this with the chapter from Wednesday? Maybe. But I didn't, so here's a slightly short update. Hopefully you like it.

### Text

Sokka woke to nails digging into his chest, covered in sweat from the heat radiating off of the firebender latched onto him. Muffled murmurs came from Zuko, who’s grip hurt even through his tunic. He grabbed Zuko’s wrist, pulling his nails out of his chest. Sokka should’ve expected the reaction, as things have only been seeming to get worse, but it still knocked the air out of him when Zuko kicked him away. He went crashing back, head hitting the hard ground next to their bedrolls. The firebender was several feet away by the time his blurred vision focused again, a dull ache in his head staying though.

“Zu–” “Leave me alone!” “Zuko…”

There’s a long silence, “please, just give me a minute,” his voice nearly begs, head against his knees.

Sokka sits back down, staying several feet from the firebender, trying to give him space but not wanting to leave him alone entirely. He looks up at the still dark sky, though he can see light just barely starting to creep out in the distance, this was not a good way to start their day..

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Zuko keeps his eyes on the grassy terrain below him, the green muted in the early morning lack of light. He rakes shaky hands through his black hair, shoving it back from his face only for it to fall forward again. Flashes of his dream, his memories, keep appearing. It’s been worse since the Earth Navy ship, and he can’t figure out why. He’s unsure if Sokka’s noticed, at least until now.

His own screams echo in his ears, but he knows they aren’t real, they aren’t from now, Sokka can’t hear them. It’s all in his head, he knows where he is, can see the grass beneath his feet, but still the sound keeps going, only broken by *his* voice. *You deserve to burn after the* *dishonor you’ve brought to our nation. You are no prince.* And this time he swears he feels the sting of burning skin, swears he smells flesh melting. This time he knows he actually screams, because in what feels like an instant he’s looking into blue eyes, and can feel cool palms holding his face to look at him. Sokka is silent, but Zuko can see how clouded his gaze is. He focuses on it, on stormy blue eyes, and slowly the screams fade, his voice goes with them.

“Sorry…” he mutters when he finally believes he can speak.

“I think you need to talk about what happened,” Sokka comments, slowly removing his hands.

“I will as soon as you do,” Zuko retorts, almost biting out the words, knowing it sounds rude, but also knowing Sokka has barely processed anything since Zhao’s ship either. Zuko has spent too much of his life reading people for his own safety not to notice. He knows Sokka blames himself for what happened, that he’s been ignoring it in favor of tending to his injuries. Zuko isn’t one to push someone to talk, not usually, but he’s not the only one keeping things to himself. He’s also banking on the fact that Sokka doesn’t want to talk, giving him an excuse not to, at least not about the ship.

“I don’t want to…”

“Neither do I, let’s just move on alright? We’ve got somewhere to get to.” Zuko can almost feel the way his heart doesn’t like how he’s reacting, and despite knowing the likely cause he decides to convince himself it’s just because he’s still sick, and not because he feels guilty for being harsh with Sokka.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

It is nearly mid-day when they reach the gates of the city, mostly due to Zuko having to take it slow. More correctly, Sokka making him take it slow. He probably would’ve been there in half the time usually, but he didn’t feel like arguing with the other boy.

“State your business,” the guard says, and there is only one, just as the last time.

Zuko simply pulls out the Beifong seal, handing it to the guard without further explanation. He feigns annoyance, as if waiting for the guard to let them in is some massive hassle. Glancing at Sokka who is keeping a rather stern expression, and Zuko has to keep himself from laughing at the way he’s purposely trying to stand taller than usual, despite the fact that the guard dwarfs both of them by a heavy margin.

“Sorry for the inconvenience, I did not realise you are friends of the Beifongs’.” Zuko can see what looks like anger in the man’s eyes, and probably because they aren’t people who would usually be considered *friends* to a noble house. He hands Zuko back the document. “You may enter,” and it is through near gritted teeth that he says it. He is much less friendly than the guard he met on his last trip here, but he doesn’t dare to be outright rude to them either.

When they finally get out of ear shot Sokka lets out a massive sigh, “that guy was terrifying! He’s bigger than Pipsqueak!” A seems to realise that Zuko has no idea who that is, “one of the old Freedom Fighters.”

“He’s huge but his name is Pipsqueak?”

“Yeah, Aang thought it was hilarious,” Sokka smiles a bit. “So, where to?”

“I don’t think Nara will be home, but Akahana and Himari should be. They’ll probably like you.”

“Should I feel offended that you think I’ll get along with eleven year-olds?”

“Isn’t Aang like eleven.”

“I think he’s technically one-hundred twelve, but I see your point.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“Zuli?” Akahana quirks an eyebrow at Zuko.

“And his boyfriend,” Himari says, popping up behind her sister with a devious smile.

“He’s not— what would make you think?” Zuko starts, feeling somewhat lucky that being sick means he was already pretty flushed, “you know what, nevermind. Is your mom still at work?” Zuko quickly changes the subject, oblivious to the Water Tribe boy’s flushed face and red ears behind him.

Akahana nods, moving for them to come inside. The two girls followed behind them inside. “So your trip up north was to find a date or..?” Himari questions.

“Maybe introduce yourself before you get started,” Zuko groans, giving Sokka a *sorry about* *her* look.

“Sorry,” she rolls her eyes dramatically, “I’m Himari. The quiet one is Akahana.”

“I’m not quiet, you're just loud,” Akahana deadpans.

Himari waves her off, looking expectantly at Sokka. He gives her a bright smile, apparently not bothered by her teasing, or at least hiding it well, “Sokka, nice to meet you Himari, Akahana.” He puts out his arm, probably from habit, to greet them in the Water Tribe fashion. The action receives a confused look from Himari who glances at Zuko for some sort of hint. He doesn’t get the chance before Sokka explains, “it’s a Water Tribe greeting,” he turns to him, looking at Zuko to help him demonstrate. He follows along, grabbing Sokka's forearm as he does the same. “Just like that.” Sokka smiles and turns back to the two girls, of which Akahana has her arm out to greet Sokka now.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

A while later the front door opens, the two girls immediately running to the door. “Okan! Zuli is here, and he brought his boyfr-just friend. He’s from the Southern Water Tribe, his name’s Sokka. They’re on their way to Omashu. Also I think Zuli isn’t feeling well, Sokka keeps asking if he’s okay,” Himari talks a mile a minute probably while Nara is taking her shoes off.

Sokka looks down at Zuko, seeming slightly confused, he decides to take pity, “okan means mom. Nara’s home.” He goes to push himself up from his position, as he’s been laying his head on Sokka’s lap, so he could lay down on the small couch instead of having to sit up. “Don’t bother sitting up on my account, kid,” Nara comments as she walks into the room, “put these away, please,” she directs, handing Akahana and Himari the bags in her hands. Her attention is quickly back to them, amber eyes flicker back and forth between the two of them.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Sokka wants to squirm in his seat as the amber eyed woman walks into the room. He knew it was irrational, but he wasn’t exactly used to being around so many firebenders without being attacked. He had to remind himself that Zuko wouldn’t let anything happen, even in his current state, and he certainly wouldn’t have chosen to bring them somewhere dangerous, he could’ve never mentioned the town if he wanted. He finds himself paying attention to something other than amber eyes when he hears his name, “I assume you’re Sokka?”

“Yes, uh– ma’am.”

“Nara is fine, I’m not that old,” she laughs lightly. Her attention mostly goes to Zuko though as she kneels down, “I’m gonna check your temperature.” She first places her hand on his forehead, then his cheek. He can feel him tensing and when her hand goes to Zuko’s neck he flinches, hitting into Sokka. He’s too focused on the interaction to notice the subtle impact, seeing the almost analytical look in Nara’s eyes at Zuko’s reaction. Her expression hardens after a moment, “you idiot. Who was it?”

Zuko stays silent, and Sokka can’t entirely see the expression from where he is, but he knows it’s probably Zuko’s angsty *everyone stay away from me* face. And Sokka couldn’t have assumed what the follow up comment would be, “Fine. As long as it wasn’t him.” Her head made a slight gesture toward Sokka. He is able to see Zuko’s eyes narrow, clearly not liking what Nara was insinuating. After a moment Zuko turns around, putting his back to the woman.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

A while later Sokka finds himself, well not cornered, but definitely purposely caught in the kitchen. Nara covered a pot of what looked like some sort of stew before turning to Sokka. He smiled nervously, seeing a sort of analysing look in her eyes. She only returned his expression with a sort of placating smile.

“Do you know how I met Zuli?” She asks, and the use of the fake name in the scenario tells him she likely only knows him by that one. He only nods, expecting her to continue. “I lied for him, I don’t regret it, deserters have to look out for each other, and he clearly had experienced more than someone his age should. He’s different now though, I’ve seen that look he has before. You don’t have the same look though, and I need to know that’s not because you caused it. He didn’t look-”

“Empty,” Sokka decides to fill in, and she seems to be ready to let him talk now. “We were captured by Admiral Zhao. Zuli was there when I got there. It was during the siege of Agna Qel’a. I was part of a small team planning to assassinate Zhao, but it didn't go as planned. I’m still not sure how they got found out, but they–” Sokka can’t continue the story, opting to end that part of it, “ well I’m the only one not at the bottom of the ocean.” He decides to sit at the table, sitting cross-legged on one of the cushions. “Zuli was in a pretty bad state when I found him.”

“Agni,” Nara hisses under her breath. “Of all the people.” She looks to Sokka again, this time giving him a much more serious expression, “I’m sorry I assumed. I’m just not sure he would… understand.”

Sokka has to take a minute to process when she means, because he knows she doesn’t know the full story of Zuko, who he is or what happened to him even before Zhao. She’s trying to protect him, a boy she knew for a week at most. The teen lets out a heavy sigh, “that someone who loves you shouldn’t hurt you? He does, though I think it’s a process, sometimes he talks like he believes he deserved everything that has been done to him, but he does know it was wrong.”

“Do you?” Nara questions, but quickly realises it needs clarification, “love him, I mean.”

Sokka doesn’t realises he’s said anything until the words are already out, “I think I’m starting to.” He laughs slightly, “it’s terrifying.”

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

sokka admitting something... also these idiot need therapy.
