## Chapter 20

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Imma be honest, you might hate me this chapter...

TW in the end note if you want to check that out. I'll say the the tw is not for something graphic, it's for basically one line.

### Text

“I can’t believe she gave this to you, you must have made quite the impression.” Nara slips the seal back into the envelope before returning it to Zuko’s bag. “I wouldn’t use this too often though, it’s odd enough for a non-family member to have one, let alone an ex Fire Nation soldier.”

“I know, getting into town was the first time I’ve used it. I’d rather not use it at all if possible.”

Nara nods, “seems you at least understand the importance.” She takes the now warm cloth from his head, pulling new one from the cold bowl of water. “I wanted to talk to you about something else though, if you’re feeling up to a chat right now.” They’d ended up staying for longer than expected, after getting to safety Zuko’s illness hit like a boulder from an earthbender, he’d barely been conscious the past few days, at least he was pretty sure it had only been a few days. He only nods, a bit hesitant on agreeing, since they’d been talking the past few minutes, so clearly this was about something she thought he wouldn’t want to discuss.

“Sokka told me you’d been captured, not long after you left here I would assume, since you’d been heading up north then.” Zuko avoided Nara’s gaze, not wanting to see what was in her eyes. Disappointment? Fear? Disgust? Or the worst, pity? He just waited for her to continue. “Have you at least been treating your wounds?”

“I'm fine Nara, barely anything happened anyway, Zh–” he can’t get the name out, the distant sound of screams echoing in his ears, “*he* was too busy being obsessed with destroying the moon.”

“I’d prefer it if you didn’t lie to me. You’re likely sick because your body has been trying to heal but you’ve been pushing yourself to keep moving. Even if your wounds heal you’re letting your health decline while it does. And I know the look in your eyes–”

“You don’t know what you’re talking about,” Zuko bites out, glaring.

“I do, because I’ve seen it before. You haven’t told him have you? Sokka. About what happened to you on that ship? You haven’t talked to anyone, not really.” Nara keeps her expression schooled, Zuko can’t find pity in it, just concern.

“Sokka saw plenty, he doesn’t need to hear me cry about it.” “You don’t want him to see you as weak.”

“He already knows I’m weak.”

“I think you are far from weak. Look, you don’t need to talk to him, not now if you don’t want to, he clearly went through quite a lot too, and he seems to have the same idea to hide away how he’s struggling. You need to talk though, and I know what Zhao is capable of, what he does to break people.”

“He didn’t *break* me,” Zuko sneers. Nara doesn’t flinch at his tone, she only sighs slightly, looking at him with a sort of expectation. “I can’t Nara. I *can’t* talk about it. I thought it would get better, that the memories would stop being so *real,* but they’ve only gotten worse. Being sick the past few days is the only time I’ve managed to sleep through the night.”

“At the very least tell me if you need anything for wounds, if anything isn’t healed yet,” Nara concedes.

“I’ll be fine.”

“Zu*li,* please.” Zuko catches the odd tone at the end of his false name, the same way it comes out when he nearly says Zuko. He meets amber eyes with a narrowed gaze, he finds not hatred or fear, but concern, and something else, something brighter that he can’t entirely make out. “I did some research while you were away. Your secret is safe with me, I promise, Prince Zuko.”

“I’m no prince,” he sighs, taking her at her word because honestly he’s still too tired to even sit up.

“I knew your cousin, we were at Ba Sing Se together. He’s part of the reason I defected.”

Zuko was never perfect at telling when someone is lying, not someone who is good at it, but Nara doesn’t seem like she’s lying, even if everything doesn’t line up, because she has two children who are too old for her to have defected during the siege of Ba Sing Se, “but Himari and Akahana?”

“Are not my children.” Zuko gives her a look to explain. “They are my older sister’s children. She moved to the colonies with her husband a little over a decade ago. It’s a long story but she and her husband were killed for treason. I smuggled Himari and Akahana out. They were too young to remember much, it is easier if they don’t know. This all happened after the siege, after Prince Lu Ten was killed.”

Zuko tries to find something comforting to say, but he can tell from her truncated explanation that she probably doesn’t want to stay on the topic of her sister. It makes sense now, that Nara hardly looks old enough to have two eleven year-old children, she probably isn’t. He takes her mentioning his cousin at the end as a hint to move the conversation to him, “you said he was part of the reason you defected. I know he didn’t agree with the war, not really, but I didn’t think many knew that. He only mentioned it around my mother or I.”

“It was more well known than you might think. I know you were young, but there were a lot of questions around your cousin’s legitimacy. General Iroh wasn’t married, and while that is not a requirement in the Fire Nation, it raised questions when he returned from an extended trip to the Earth Kingdom with a son. We met in training when we were thirteen, my parents sent me to study at the Royal Fire Nation Academy, they were disappointed that my sister chose to move to the colonies rather than continue a military career, and wanted to make sure I didn’t do the same.” A sort of bitter smile appeared on her lips, “Lu Ten and I, we shared similar beliefs, that there were better ways to share the Fire Nation’s prosperity, of course we still bought that was even the purpose, we were still fools, but we had the right idea. He wanted change, wanted the war to end, and he was going to do it, as soon as he became Fire Lord. Then we went to Ba Sing Se, and we learned just how far gone the Fire Nation is. Lu Ten was not killed by the Earth Kingdom military, he was not killed in battle, he was assassinated.”

“You think someone in the Fire Nation ordered him killed because he wanted the war to end?” Zuko closes his eyes, thinking back to his odd conversation with JongJong when they’d met. Had he suspected the same thing? Nara sounded confident though, as if it wasn’t just a theory but a fact.

“I don’t think someone ordered it, I know someone did. I don’t know who. I have my guesses of course, but I know who was tasked to assassinate him, and that everyone who knew besides me is dead.” Nara lets out a shaky sigh, “Zuko, it was Zhao.”

“Then I know who ordered it, my father.”

“I didn’t want to–”

“He’s been after his favor since I was a child, he’s *always* been around, doing everything he can to gain power.” Zuko has to open his eyes so as not to drown in the screams that have begun again. “He doesn’t care about serving the Fire Lord, he just wants power to lord over people, the opportunity to do as he pleases because of his station. If he thought it possible I’m sure he’d try to usurp my father.”

“Zuko,” and the way his name comes out sends a shot through him, reminding him of someone he hasn’t seen in years, but it does comfort him, instead it reminds him of his loss, and of what has happened since her disappearance.

*A hand pushes against Zuko’s shoulder, burning his flesh as he’s held down against the floor* *of the cell. He grits his teeth as he feels another hand nearing his back, gasping as a sharp* *heat slashes across his back. He readies himself before the next, only wincing this time.* *Another, he keeps himself from reacting now. And as they multiply tears build in his eyes, his* *back stinging in pain from burns layering over more burns.*

*“The Prince of the Fire Nation crying over a little burn, pathetic.” Zhao spits out as he* *catches Zuko’s eye in his, a smirk breaks onto his expression. “I much prefer if you’d scream,* *like you did then,” he says almost wistfully.*

*Heat slices across the back of his thigh, and he bites down on his lip so hard it starts to bleed,* *but he doesn’t scream, and that's the closest thing he can get to victory he can get here. He* *won’t scream.*

Despite the memory Zuko can hear his own screams, because his *victory* only lasted so long. He feels a hand on his shoulder but it doesn't hold him down, just trying to shake him softly, this person is worried about hurting him. Zuko finds blue eyes looking back at him now, instead of amber.

“We lost you there for a bit,” Sokka says it lightly, but Zuko recognises the stormy look in his eyes. “Nara came and got me,” he explains. He struggles to give a smile, and Zuko can help but blame himself for the other teen’s state. He looks so tired, eyes clouded with worry, heavy bags under them. He notices after a few long moments that they’re red, like he’s been crying, and his chest hurts knowing Sokka is hurting.

“Are you okay?” Zuko asks with an unsteady voice, trying to push himself up to keep himself from falling asleep. Sokka is quick to help him, softly taking his arms to pull him up. Once Zuko is sitting against the bottom of the couch as the futon is on the floor next to it, repeats the question, “are you okay?”

Sokka tries to give him a reassuring smile, but it's hard to do so with bloodshot eyes. He sits next to Zuko, leaning against the couch, “I’m okay. Had a rough night, bad dreams,” he admits.

“Want to talk about it?” Zuko offers, trying to ignore the hypocrisy of his question.

He shakes his head, brown hair falling into his eyes, “not right now. I don’t want to think about it.”

“I think we should leave soon,” he vocalises, following Sokka’s want for a change of subject.

“You’re sick Zuko.”

“I’ll be fine, we’re wasting too much time here, moving will make me feel better,” Zuko tries to convince, earning him an unamused look. He knows why, traveling while not being well enough too is why he’s in the state he is now, but they can’t stay here forever, they have somewhere to be.

“Just give yourself a few more days,” Sokka urges.

Looking at him Zuko can see his hesitancy, being here isn’t helping them, because they aren’t ready to talk, but there’s nothing to distract them. Zuko’s lucky he’s sick, at least that can count as a distraction. Sokka is stuck with his thoughts, his memories most hours while the girls are at school and Nara is at work, it isn’t helping him. But Zuko nods, because arguing won’t help, he needs to focus on getting better so they can leave.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

It’s four days later, a week since they’d arrived, when Zuko is well enough to travel again. Luckily in the last two days he’s been lucid enough to be a distraction to Sokka again, talking about random things, mapping out their travels, discussing what they need to get before leaving. The distraction seems to help, Sokka looking better rested at least, and more like his usual self. The quick change doesn’t remedy Zuko’s worry, unsure how Sokka is really doing, but feeling unable to ask lest it cause him to become poorly again.

“Fine, you can come.” They can hear Nara’s voice from the kitchen. Himari and Akahana have been pestering her about coming along all day. The plan was that Nara would drop them off when she went to get supplies from a town to the south, she planned to leave the girls with a friend for the few day journey there and back, but neither of them seemed to be having it.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“I still can’t believe you’re a prince, I mean you don’t really act how I thought a prince would act,” Himari comments as they sit in the back of the wagon.

“I’m not exactly a prince anymore.” The comment earns a frown, spurring Sokka to try to make her feel better.

“He knows how to play the part, trust me.”

“He’s serious enough, isn’t he?” Akahana shrugs.

The wagon jerks as it comes to a sudden halt. Everyone’s attention turns to Nara at the front of the cart, “Zuko come look at these,” she calls back, though it’s quiet enough that Zuko doesn’t seem to hear her.

“She wants you to look at something up front,” Sokka explains. Zuko nods, getting out of the wagon to go around to the front. He doesn’t get into the back again, instead getting in the front with Nara.

“You need to head back, we’ll continue on foot.” He hears Zuko, making his way through the boxes to the front to join the conversation. “Nara, seriously, those are komodo rhino prints, and they’re headed south.” There were another set of prints partially covered by the group of komodo rhinos, were they chasing someone?

“I’m not going to let you two just head straight into danger on–” loud footsteps thunder against the ground, “you two, out of the wagon, into the trees, now,” she directs. “Don’t just sit there, they’ll let us pass, but if they see you two here we’ll be lucky if we’re just captured.

The two of them are quick to head for the trees, bringing their bags with them so if the cart is checked they won’t raise suspicion. They are quick into the brush, getting far enough away that they won’t be easily spotted, but it means they can’t really see what is happening either. The sound of komodo rhino footsteps come to a halt, Sokka struggles to listen to what is being said, but he can only tell that it’s a gruff voice, while unable to pick up the words. The can really only see one of the komodo rhinos, he notices Zuko observing him, eyes narrowed as he looks it over, trying to get a better look despite being so far away.

“Stay here.” Zuko’s tone comes out commanding despite being a whisper. Sokka, surprised by the sudden change, doesn’t speak until it’s too late, Zuko having dropped his packs, only bringing his dao with him. He doesn’t know what Zuko has found out, but before he even makes it to the group Sokka can see flames, and hear the voice of Nara telling the girls to run. Everything happens too fast, and he can hardly see any of it. There’s screaming and a smell that is all too familiar.

*He’s on the ship, forced to kneel, his eyes locked with Aoto. Screams filled the air alongside* *the smell of burning flesh.*

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Zuko knew it would be too late, those weren’t Fire Nation soldiers, and those other tracks weren’t from someone running they were from a tracker, he’d made a grave error, because now that he was closer he could see the shirshu. His gaze met amber, but it wasn’t Nara like he’d expected from the yelling, no it was Akahana, who’s shooting fire toward the Rough Rhinos while standing in front of Himari on the ground. He didn’t take time to try to find Nara, instead attacking the men from the other side, sending fire at their backs to pull their attention. He knew what the smell meant, the smell that while protecting him from the shirshu for now, also likely meant that Nara was dead. Zuko couldn’t dwell on the thought right now though, focused on distracting the group from Akahana, who based on the way they turned their attention to him was very much not their target. He couldn’t focus on the twins though, while they clearly aren’t going for the kill, he needs all his focus on the Rough Rhinos to avoid capture.

Fighting becomes a blur, five against one is not an easy fight. Zuko doesn’t pull his attacks like he often does, the Rough Rhinos are legendary in the Fire Nation, mostly for the fear they cause through their cruelty, he has no problem putting them down for good. He should’ve kept an idea on Akahana though, while he’d manage to take three of the men down, his ear rings hearing her scream.

He turns to where a woman with long black hair holds a knife to her throat. It takes him a moment to realise who she is, June. He’d met her a few years ago, while he was still hunting the avatar. She’s a famous bounty hunter, probably the most sought after in any nation. What’s she doing with the Rough Rhinos?

“Put down the blades or I slit the kid’s throat.”

“She has nothing to do with this June. Let her go.”

“You know who I am, I suppose I should be flattered,” her tone is dull though, not betraying any emotion. “Surrender and I’ll let her go.”

Zuko knows it's a bad idea, he knows she’s lying, but he doesn’t have another choice. He has to hope she’ll keep her word, hope that he’s wrong. He’s too far to make it to her in time. Zuko drops his blades, slowly raising his hands, “let her go June. I won’t fight.” The two men left grab him as it happens, pinning his arms behind his back. Akahana doesn’t scream, she doesn’t get the chance, but his own rips through the air as she falls to the ground, covered in her own blood. “You bitch!” He screams, tearing against the two left Rough Rhinos.

“A life for a life, Prince Zuko.”

“She was a child!” He screams as he feels his body go limp, the shirshu’s tongue whipping across his side.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

TW: I wouldn't usually put a non-graphic character death as a tw, but this time around since the deaths involve kids I thought I should probably say something.

## Chapter 21

### Notes

#### Chapter Summary

so I very much just forgot to post last week, sorry about that. we're back though, hopefully you all like this chapter.

### Text

“Wake up.” Zuko sputters out a cough as a foot meets his side. Gold eyes narrow into a glare at the man in front of him, a silent ‘what’. His arms are secured behind his back with metal cuffs, he’d commend them for not being stupid, but they’re Fire Nation, no accolades for knowing how to keep a firebender contained. He wouldn't be able to move his arms without them anyways, his body still paralysed from the shirshu’s tongue. The man doesn’t say anything, instead his boot just meets Zuko’s side again, this time hard enough to crash him to his side on the wooden floor. Okay, not entirely smart, though lighting the floor on fire likely wouldn’t do him any good. Another kick to his stomach, slamming him into the wall behind him. He raises his gaze to the man’s, his pride getting the better of him. Again, and again. He continues the assault until he’s coughing up blood, only then does the man stop, crouching down beside Zuko. “You’re lucky Zhao wants you alive.”

Zuko doesn’t even flinch at the name, his mind too clouded with pain, his vision blurry, unfocused eyes staring at the man. As his eyes start to close a hand yanks him to sit up by his neck, eyes coming back into focus as he is held against the wall. He can’t claw the hand away, can’t try to break free, still paralysed. Not that it would be much of any option even if he didn’t still have shirshu venom coursing through his body.

“If it was up to me we’d kill you for what you did.”

“Go fuck yourself,” Zuko spits out without hesitation. Death here would be better than being delivered to Zhao.

The hand squeezes against his throat, heating as it tightens. His screams come out as a whimper as his throat burns.

“Vachir,” a voice cuts through the pain, the heat vanishing immediately, “we can’t deliver damaged goods.”

“Zhao just asked for him *alive.*”

“Leave him Vachir, that’s an order.”

“Order? There are no Rhino’s anymore Mongke! He killed the others! Our brothers! I’m not taking any of your orders. I said this was a bad gig, that this would go wrong. Now we’ve lost Ogodei, Kahci, and Yeh-Lu. Plus we have to split the money with *her.”*

“They were weaker, it was their fault for underestimating the Prince.”

“*Prince*,” Vachir sneers, “he’s nothing but a murderous traitor.” Zuko would’ve flinched if he could move, his hand tightening around his neck. Mongke grabs his shoulder, getting his attention properly. Vachir let's Zuko fall back to the ground, letting go of his grip around his throat.

“Take a walk Vachir. Now.”

“Piss off,” the archer grumbles, but leaves the room anyway.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Sokka finds himself back to the brush, and only the fact that the sun is now setting tells him how long it has been. He runs up to where the wagon had been, but now all that is left is dried blood on the dirt road. It had been a little while after midday when everything had happened. He rearranged the bags, trying to even out the weight on himself. He’d have to travel through the night if he wanted any hope of finding Zuko.

There’s sets of komodo dragon tracks leading south, without time to waste he sets off, his pace slow from the extra weight. The change in what he’s carrying at least has his focus enough to keep his mind from roaming to worse things, for a short time at least.

His trip is not short though, and his mind begins to wonder after a while. He can’t remember what happened, not fully, but Zuko was gone, as were Nara and the girls. He vaguely remembers yelling but he can’t figure out what was said. He can remember Zuko screaming, what was it about though? Who was he yelling at? All he knows is that Zuko is gone, and he knows Zuko wouldn’t have left on his own, he was captured.

*What if he’s dead?* Sokka’s mind supplies. “No. He’s not dead,” he speaks aloud, arguing his mind, “he’ll probably get himself free before I even get there.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Zuko is leant against the wall, unsure how long it has been, but the sun had set a while ago, and outside the room had been quiet for at least a candlemark. The sound of rain pounded against the roof. He strained to look around the dark room for anything that might work to get his shackles off. He needed to get out, and soon, before they woke. He could move now, the shirshu venom having worn off, why they hadn’t paralysed him again he’s unsure. June hadn’t been around since they got to what Zuko was pretty sure was an abandoned farm, maybe she isn’t here.

His ankles not bound he struggles to his feet, he’d have to deal with his wrist binds later. He puts his ear to the door, listening for any movement, knowing the noise he’ll probably make breaking down the door will wake them, but counting on a few moments of deliriousness to get his swords, he’s not risking the key as he saw it on Mongke earlier. Hearing nothing from the other room he first attempts to open the door by the handle, though expects it not to open. The door creaks open, leaving Zuko slightly shocked, but he quickly shakes off the surprise when it occurs to him that it’s not very common for interior doors to have locks. He enters into a hallway, quietly making his way toward the main room, no sign of the Rough Rhinos. He finds his swords unsheathed on the kitchen table, as though someone had been looking at them but had to leave. After a bit of a struggle he manages to get them sheathed, attaching them to his hip.

Getting out of the house is easy, the door unlocked, and no sign of the komodo rhinos either. He glares into the darkness around the house trying to see through the downpour, broken down fencing and a barn hazy in the distance. As he nears the dirt road he hears the familiar sound of ostrich horse steps, quite a few of them, and they’re getting closer quickly. The foggy night hides them until they are nearly upon him, a group of Earth Kingdom soldiers. He doesn’t think to hide as they near, instead shaking his head to let his hair fall into his eyes. He knows it’s foolish, but it won’t be the first time he gets help from the Earth Kingdom military while running from people of his own nation.

“Help!” he tries to yell, but it comes out quieter than expected and the pain from trying is enough to cause tears to well in his eyes. He does his best to blink the tears away. Luckily despite the inability to yell, someone from the group must have seen him because they sped up until they came to a quick halt just before him.

The man in front drops down from his ostrich horse, he nears slowly, arms raised as if to show he’s not a threat. He looks a decent few years older than him, maybe in his late twenties, but it is hard to tell in the darkness of night. He isn’t looking at Zuko’s face as he nears, instead he’s eyes got to his neck, which he can only assume is covered in a nasty burn. “Spirits,” the soldier curses in a harsh whisper, only then seeming to realise himself, “what happened?” Zuko can hear how he’s trying to keep his voice calm, the anger under it.

He tries to find the least words to explain, knowing any talking is going to hurt, but before he says anything someone else speaks up, “captain, their wrists–” the soldier who has now also left his ostrich horse gestures to Zuko’s bound hands.

A muttering of curses leaves the captain’s mouth, “get them off.” The soldier that had come down is quick to follow the order, it doesn’t take him long to get the cuffs off, clearly not the first time he’d done so. Zuko rubs his wrists slightly, though is careful as they had aggravated his mostly healed wrists from his time on the boat.

“I was traveling south when–” his throat aches as he speaks, his fingers going to his throat, cringing at the recognisable feeling of a fresh burn, “attacked.” He closes his eyes trying to will away the pain, his mind fuzzy with it he says, “my family.” The image of Akahana comes into view, then Himari, an arrow through her stomach, and Nara burned so badly he could barely tell it was her. He chokes, quick to wipe away tears, they were gone, and it was his fault. He’d gotten them involved, they shouldn’t have been there. They were killed because of him, because they helped him. Zuko jerks back as someone grips his shoulder, looking up to find green eyes staring down at him, and a hand hovering where he’d been. He recognises a moment of surprise, the moment the man realises he isn’t Earth. Sudden shock vanishes for concern?

“Would you mind coming with us?” It sounds like a genuine question, but Zuko doubts he actually has a choice so he just nods.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

It’s a little past sunrise when Sokka is finally upon the first building he’s seen. It doesn’t take more than a passing glance to tell it’s abandoned. Looking for any sign of people being there he sees the sun glinting off something partially covered in mud. A lack of anything else to follow he picks up the partially buried item, he recognises Fire Nation steal cuffs, familiar from Kyoshi Island where he’d seen them hooked to a Rough Rhino’s belt.

“Of course he got free,” he nearly laughs. Would Zuko head south? He hadn’t seen him at all, so that means he must have. Throwing down the cuffs, his tiredness from walking all night having vanished with the appearance of new found hope, he starts off again.

He doesn’t have to go much further before he finds muddy tracks heading south, ostrich horses. Sokka breathed a sigh of relief at the lack of komodo rhino tracks, he’d continued south despite the disappearance of them with the rain, but he’d also hoped he wouldn’t see them after finding the discarded cuffs, it meant Zuko might still be safe. With nothing but a trust in his sometimes questionable instincts he begins to follow the tracks.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“Colonies or just very Fire looking?” The captain, who Zuko’s learnt is called Kenji, questions. He’d agreed to answer some questions, he knew he couldn’t give the whole truth, but considering he’d probably be face down in mud or recaptured without their appearance he was willing to say what he could.

“Neither. I’m from Hari Busan, I left three years ago, before I turned fourteen.”

“You’re from the Fire Nation capital?” Kenji didn’t look on guard so much as bewildered. “You left to avoid service?”

“No, I left because my father threw me out.” Zuko shrugs, taking a sip of the tea that had been provided. Honestly he was very much risking making himself a captive again, but they let him keep his swords, and it wasn’t like there was anyone he had to worry about protecting here, so getting out shouldn’t be too hard if it comes to that.

“You left the Fire Nation because your father threw you out?” Kenji’s expression seems curious, like he’s trying to map something out, but Zuko doubts it is the truth, “why come to the Earth Kingdom? Your mother came with you? You said you and your family had been attacked.”

“Is asking about my life story really pertinent?” He snaps, not realising how defensive he sounds until he notices the look he’s getting, almost a warning, but at the same time like Kenji is trying to placate him with the soft smile that doesn’t reach his eyes. Zuko takes the warning, calming himself before speaking again, “My mother had been *gone* for years. My *family* is– was– the people who took me in. It’s my fault we got attacked, they shouldn’t have been there, I was leaving.”

Kenji doesn’t reply verbally, instead he gestures for Zuko to continue, probably having too many questions at this point. He doesn’t get the chance to respond again.

“Sir, there’s some kid here, says he’s looking for someone called Zuli,” a soldier says after popping his head into the tent. Zuko will never get used to that lack of knocking among some of the companies he’s ended up with.

“I’ll assume that is you, Li?” Kenji looks at him with a slightly amused expression.

“Try to leave that first part off usually…” Zuko sighs, turning to the soldier, “is he Water Tribe?” The comment earns him a new surprised look from the captain, he is making quite the lore for himself right now. The soldier nods. “That’ll be Sokka, he’s Chief Hakoda’s son.” He decides to mention the later bit for assurance Sokka wouldn’t be treated rudely, though he doubts it with the way Kenji had been reacting to him.

“You in trouble with the Southern Water Tribe?” He sounds more worried for him than warry.

“He’s a friend, we were heading south to meet up with his father.”

Kenji just lets out a sigh, motioning for them to all go out to meet Sokka now. Zuko can’t fathom what the captain is probably thinking right now, seeming to just be letting whatever may happen, happen.

As they walk out the tent he sees Sokka, his blue eyes going wide. Bags dropping to the ground as he races toward him, a smile breaking onto his face. The arms he finds himself waking up in most days wrap around him, pulling him into a tight hug, which ends much quicker than expected.

“Thank the spirits you’re alive,” Sokka gasps out as he pulls back slightly, looking at Zuko for a moment that stretches on for what feels like an eternity.

He realises what is happening only a fraction of a second before he’s pulled in again.

### End Notes

## Chapter 22

Zuko is engulfed in a hug once again, though he doesn’t find himself returning it. *I’m an* *idiot,* he groans internally. Sokka pulls back, looking at him with a bemused expression, raising an eyebrow in silent question. Zuko hopes he manages to keep his new found disappointment internal. *How could I think– this isn’t some play, Zuko. Agni, what am I* *thinking?* He manages to focus on something other than beating himself up internally, shaking off Sokka’s question with his own, “you’re okay?”

“Am I okay?” Sokka’s eyes once again wide, “you’re the one who was captured by the Rough Rhinos!” His fingers hover near Zuko’s burned throat now, eyes shifted down to the damage, “I’m glad you’re–” Sokka cuts himself off, “Nara,” he gasps out, “and the girls…”

Zuko just nods, not able to find his voice again, both due to the pain and the subject. He’d been trying not to think about them, trying not to break, he couldn’t mourn them right now.

“I apologise for breaking up the reunion, but I would like to speak to you both.”

Zuko turns to find Kenji behind them, he gives a slight nod before the two are following behind him into the tent they'd been in before.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Sokka wants to pass out, after being up for Tui knows how long. He’s not sure how long this conversation has been going on, and despite how nice Kenji seems he wants it to be over if only so he can finally go to sleep. He’s barely paying attention when he hears the captain mention something about heading south.

“It shouldn’t be any trouble, you two coming along until we meet with the larger forces. I’d rather not leave you traveling on your own with that group still looking for you. I might not be a navy man, but even I’ve heard of that man’s cruelty, I won’t have you two risk being out there on your own. We’ll be diverting off before getting far enough south to be by Omashu, but by then we should be far enough off, or even better we’ll manage to take them out on the way.”

Sokka couldn’t help but be suspicious of the offer, when had they ever even mentioned Omashu? Had he really not been listening? Had Zuko said something? His mind was too fuzzy from the lack of sleep to remember much of the conversation.

“We haven’t got news about the troops down there, honestly more hopeful than hearing something, we usually only get told if they are losing or have already lost. Hopefully you’ll be able to meet up with your father again soon.”

Now it made more sense, he knew who his father was and they’d mentioned going south. “You’re the first person not to be all doom and gloom about the situation.”

“Hope is our most important asset in times like these, if you lose hope then you’re that much easier to defeat.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Zuko had to be glad for the distraction, even if said distraction was driving him a bit mad at this point. After Kenji was finally done speaking with them he got them sorted with a tent, he tried not to question why he’d automatically assumed to put them in a tent together rather than in one of the other sleeping tents. Sokka had promptly collapsed on one of the cots, and had now been snoring for the past hour; he couldn’t blame him after having been up all night. Zuko was somewhat grateful for his companion's choice to sleep immediately, because he wasn’t sure he could take sleeping how they usually do. As it was he found himself staring at the ceiling trying to grasp why he was so bothered by what had happened, or not happened, technically speaking. Zuko could admit it was rare that his feelings for someone haven’t been returned, both a good and bad thing, but that wasn’t the issue. Thinking back on the moment he could see it, the moment of hesitation, the way blue eyes flickered down slightly, he wasn’t fooling himself… was he? Zuko knows how to read people, he has always had to know, it was how he survived, even if just barely. He knew how to tell what someone is thinking, or he thought he did.

He wanted to yell at himself for dwelling on something so unimportant, so what if Sokka doesn’t like him? So what if he didn’t *kiss* him? He could barely speak right now. He’d gotten three people killed, three people he cared deeply about. Why was he so worried about what Sokka thinks of him? It won’t be long before they part ways. They likely won’t see each other again after. Zuko will go back to trying to fade into obscurity in the Earth Kingdom, while Sokka will fight alongside his family. He’d been down this path before though, been so sure as he was now. He can’t want this, it wouldn’t work. It is for the best that nothing happened. He *knows* it’s for the best.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Sokka wakes up *cold, too cold.* Shooting up on the cot, wide eyes staring down where Zuko would usually be, but he was gone. *Of course he’s gone. You let him get captured.* His mind chastises him. His eyes slowly acclimate to the darkness of the tent, why is he in a tent? Scanning the space around him he finds the shadowy figure of someone laying on another cot a few feet away at the other side of the tent. Scrambling to his feet he nearly falls over himself to check who it is. Memories from the day before some rushing back as his hand grasps the figure's shoulder.

A blur later he’s laid out on the floor staring up at gold eyes which fade from manic to worry before igniting in pain. Zuko collapses to the side, cradling his torso. “Fuck,” the firebender mutters, tears welling in his eyes.

“Zuko? What happened? Are you alright?” Sokka tries to get his attention, but he’d barely been with him to begin with. As he curls in on himself Sokka notices the bruising peeking out from under his shirt.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“Zuli I’ve got to stay late today. Akihana and Himari have a fire demonstration practice at school tonight, it’s for their culture festival coming up, I don’t want the two of them coming home that late on their own.”

Nara doesn’t have to actually ask before he’s offering to go, he didn’t want to just sit around for the days until they could find him someone heading north out of town, the only reason he was waiting in the first place was his uncertainty walking extensively with his still fresh injury. “When does their practice start, it would be nice to see.” He left out that it would be nice to see fire not coming at him for once, it had been a long while since he’d seen fire used for something other than fighting.

That was the day he’d ran into Toph for the first time, which did end up making him somewhat late to the beginning of practice. A few eyes looked back to him as he walked in, parents who’d probably come to help out with preparation. There was slight confusion, likely because he was certainly closer to the age of the kids practicing than to the parents, and that they likely didn’t recognise him as he’d barely been in town two days. Confusion seemed to pass when the twins waved him over to where they were sitting.

“We haven’t gone up yet,” Himari whispers as he sits next to them.

“We’re the only ones firebending in our demonstration, we usually go last because some parents don’t want to be here for it,” Akihanna is the one to explain, her voice just loud enough to hear.

Zuko finds he’s a bit taken back by the explanation, as he was under the impression that much of the town was part Fire at the least. He looked up at the small stage to see that the current child was performing an earthbending routine, he quickly went back to focusing on the girls worried his annoyance would show.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

He finds himself talking, well more like ranting, to Nara after the girls had gone to bed that night, “I’m sure plenty of them can firebend as well, nearly half of the families left just before their practice, stayed for everyone else. It’s meant to be a culture festival, bending is part of that. There were plenty of kids earthbending.”

“Zuli.” She places a cup of tea on the table in front of him before sitting down with her own, “I was shaken by it the first time as well. I talked with their arts instructor. It’s a mix of reasons, why people leave. For some people it’s because of bad experiences, many of them are here fleeing the Fire Nation, even if they are Fire themselves. I think for many it is shame, I’m sure you’ve noticed even in your short time here, firebending isn’t used very publicly. Many people don’t speak about it, whether because of regret of what they did before coming here or because they grew up with people who only looked at them with fear.”

It very quickly confirmed for him that this town wasn’t actually some haven for those who wanted to escape the war, it wasn’t a solution, not really.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“You’ve talked to her?” Himari gasped.

“Who? Toph?” Zuko clarified, though, that had been who he was talking about, commenting that they’d probably get along with the earthbender.

“We haven’t talked to her, I think her parents don’t really want her hanging out with us.”

“I don’t think it’s that they don’t trust us, she doesn’t even talk with the other Earth Kingdom nobles’ kids,” Akihanna finishes the explanation.

“I kind of thought she was stuck up, I mean her family is pretty important,” Himari comments, followed by rolling her eyes. Zuko had to keep himself from laughing at the irony of it, though his importance is pretty nonexistent now as far as he’s concerned.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Zuko shoots up from his spot on the couch, nearly falling off, if it wasn’t for someone stopping him he would have. He turns to find Nara who instead of saying anything simply makes sure he isn’t going to fall and then walks away. A few moments later he hears the sound of her filling a teapot.

By the time she returns with a cup for each of them he’s sat up properly, Nara taking a seat next to him. They don’t speak at first, sipping their tea in a comfortable silence.

“I didn’t mean to wake you,” Zuko starts.

Nara, shaking her head slightly, “You didn’t, even if you had I wouldn’t have minded. I’d rather you not be alone.”

“Being alone doesn’t bother me,” he tries to assure, knowing soon enough he’ll have to leave, and he’ll be alone once again. It was unavoidable for him, either people left him or he left others, no matter what he’d end up alone. He used to hate being alone, in the years after his mom left, when he was still in the palace, he was the most alone then. Now he doesn’t so much mind being alone, to most he’s just some traveler, whatever they think of him doesn’t matter much being soon enough they’ll part, but he’s slowly learnt to appreciate the lesson he learns in his travels.

“Just because it doesn’t bother you doesn’t mean it is what’s best. Honestly, you’re a kid, you shouldn’t be involved in any of this, and now you’re going to run straight toward the person who has a price on your head. I know I don’t have the right to tell you what to do, but I really wish you’d consider just staying here. This war isn’t yours to fight.”

Zuko can’t seem to string together a response, *stay here? She’s offering for him to stay.* For a moment he finds himself wanting to say yes, because that was the plan originally, to find a place in the Earth Kingdom where he could blend, where he could hide. He knows he can’t though, he’s not going to fight, not really, he’s just going to warn them, there’s no danger in it. He doesn’t answer but he can tell before she says anything that Nara knows his answer. “Alright, just know that the offer is there if you change your mind.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

Zuko manages to see through the blur of tears, wide blue eyes staring at him, storm filled and wet like his own. Sokka was crying? “Are you okay?” he manages to get out in a strained voice. Everything hurts, staying focused hurts, his mind foggy, what had he been thinking about before? He couldn’t seem to remember, but the pain in his chest was the worst of all and maybe that was enough to tell him he doesn’t want to know.

The sound of laughter fills the tent, it’s only for a short time, but it shocks Zuko all the same. Narrowing his eyes in confusion at the boy sitting in front of him. He doesn’t have to ask, Sokka sighing as his laugh dies down, “am I okay? You asked that before too.” Sokka pauses, almost like he’s expecting a response, but Zuko isn’t sure what. He shakes his head, letting out a sigh, “Zuko, shouldn’t I be the one asking *you* that?”

“I’m fine,” but his voice barely gets the words out, as if the spirits are trying to make a point.

For a second Sokka looks almost mad, but it fades to a sort of tiredness immediately, the look so quick that Zuko isn’t sure that there was actually any anger there. “Did you at least have their medic look you over?”

“I got burn cream from them.”

“Did you tell them about the bruising?”

Zuko is sure there’s underlying anger, he swears he can hear it, he knows the answer Sokka wants, but it isn’t the truth and he’d really rather not lie to him. He doesn’t have to answer, because Sokka really didn’t need to ask the question in the first place.

“Will you at least let me see, we still have some leftover medical supplies.”

“You aren’t mad?” Zuko can hear how his voice shakes when he asks, wanting to curse himself for it, he hopes Sokka writes it off as being because of his burnt throat.

“I’m not mad, well I am… but mostly I’m just worried. I mean what were you thinking? You just got over being sick and you decide to take on the Rough Rhinos? I’ve watched them nearly burn down a village. What made you think you could beat them? Do you realise how terrified I was? I thought they were going to kill you!” Sokka wipes away the welling tears, “Spirits! You are making me sound like Katara.”

“I thought I could save them.” Zuko breathes out, he’s not sure if he should admit it, not sure he should tell Sokka what he did. But he should know, right? It wasn’t even the first time Zuko’s killed someone. Sokka has a right to know, to decide if he really wants to care this much, because Sokka isn’t like him. Sokka doesn’t have to struggle to be good, he just is. “I killed three of the Rough Rhinos.”

“What?” Sokka looks confused, not surprised though. Zuko almost goes to explain until he speaks again, “of course you did. They were attacking you, you were trying to protect Nara and the girls.” Another few moments of silence before Sokka continues again, “Did you think I’d be mad about that? Zuko, I do understand how war works. I was literally on a mission to kill Zhao when I got captured.”

“I know, but… I’m not,” Zuko groans, knowing Sokka is going to disagree with him, “nevermind. Can we just go back to sleep?”

### End Notes

## Chapter 23

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

you might've notice the chapter count, this is the final chapter of this book, don't worry they'll be a second. more info will be at the end, but for now, enjoy the chapter.

### Text

They traveled with Kenji’s men for the next few days, with no sign of the last of the Rough Rhinos; they both calmed down, well, as much as they could traveling with Earth Kingdom soldiers. As it was they eventually would be heading in separate directions, there wasn’t a large amount of fanfare, three days after heading south together they decided to continue on directly south versus the company needing to travel east. The morning of the fourth day they were back on their own, nearing the mountains, the last thing that separated them from Omashu.

“Do you hear that?” Sokka questions, breaking the comfortable silence they’d been walking in for the past few hours.

Zuko paused, trying to figure out what Sokka was hearing, he’d admittedly been a bit zoned out, they’d talked a bit about what happened but it had still been less than a week since everything. Constantly he found himself stuck in his head, stuck in half memories, as if his mind was trying to protect him from them. It couldn’t block out what happened though, instead his memory of it was clear, watching as Akihana bled out as she tried to protect her sister who was likely already dead at the time. The sound of yelling echoes through the mountains, it’s faint, as if far off, but Zuko can hear it if he focuses.

“Hope they’re friendly?” Sokka says in a less than hopeful voice.

Zuko just hums in response, feeling about the same on running into people again, but they didn’t want to go around the mountains, not with how close they are, going around would take longer. They’d have to hope they wouldn’t run into anyone while they found a way through. He vaguely remembers a story his uncle had once told about the creation of Omashu, about a cave used by two lovers to meet while their people were at war. He would assume the hardest part would be finding the cave, before the Fire Nation finds them.

“You’re not listening, are you?”

“Sorry,” Zuko quickly apologises, realising he needs to explain what he was thinking, “I was just remembering a story my Uncle told me–” he’s quick to continue when he finds Sokka looking at him with knitted brows, “there’s a cave that should lead to Omashu.”

“You’re trusting a story from your uncle about a secret tunnel to Omashu?”

“My uncle is rarely wrong.”

“Your uncle laid siege to Ba Sing Se.”

“I said rarely, not never.”

An incredulous look leveled at him he feels the need to try to convince Sokka, but he’s talked enough already that his throat feels like it’s on fire, he ends up just glaring back at Sokka. The Watertribe teen seems to understand the issue, letting out a sigh, “you really believe this cave exists?” Zuko hesitates for a moment before he nods, not because he doesn’t believe the cave exists, but because he remembers the rule of getting through it. Sokka passes the water pouch to him, motioning for him to drink, likely hoping for more of an explanation.

Zuko can’t find his voice yet, and despite the lack of more explanation Sokka follows as he begins walking into the mountains, trying to piece together where the cave might be. Before they find it he manages to speak some more, “the cave was created by two lovers who wanted to meet with each other, their people were at war so they couldn’t meet publicly.”

Sokka didn’t respond vocally, almost seeming lost in thought, he only came back when they happened upon an entrance to a cave. Blue eyes narrow at the Earth Kingdom writing on the signs outside the cave, and Zuko can only agree with the sort of annoyed look that forms on his face as he concentrates, “that means curse, doesn’t it?”

Zuko, despite all the time in the Earth Kingdom, still find their writing difficult, mostly because some of their characters are similar or the same but mean different things than they do in the Fire Nation, making the written language confusing, unlike speaking where beyond their more specific terms, things were generally the same. He takes a minute to translate the writing the same as Sokka is.

“You didn’t say anything about a curse,” Sokka groans, he mumbles the next few lines written on the plaque at the cave entrance, “love will light your way…”

“It’ll be better than getting caught by the Fire Nation,” Zuko comments, gesturing to the smoke in the distance, “even if we could get through without getting caught, it will take longer.” He admittedly wasn’t so sure about the whole trusting in love thing, it hadn't even done him much good in the past, but they had supplies and eventually they’d find their way out.

Sokka was quiet for a long few moments, eventually looking back at Zuko. It was after another moment that he finally said something, though this one felt like it lasted an eternity, as Sokka’s gaze landed on him with a sort of questioning look that he couldn’t figure out the reason for. “We should get moving, before they figure out we’re here.”

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

The cave was more like a maze, a labyrinth that neither of them could seem to make heads or tails of. Zuko lit their path the best he could, holding a flame in his hand as they walked. Sokka wasn’t sure how long it had been, without any indication of time passing beyond his stomach growling he could only guess how long it had really been.

“I’m pretty sure we’re going in circles,” Zuko voices with a tired sigh.

“How can you tell? I don’t even know what direction we’re going,” Sokka complains, he’d attempted a map when they first entered the cave but it didn’t last long as it felt almost as though the caves shifted. Looking over the firebender his eyes linger on the way the flames reflect off his golden eyes . The moment seems to linger, was it a moment? Sokka wasn’t actually sure, telling what Zuko was thinking is annoyingly difficult at times.

“I marked the wall here,” he gestures to a spot on the cave wall, a small but purposefully dark streak as if the wall had been scorched, which Sokka had to assume that’s what it was. “We passed through this way a candlemark ago, also we’re not deep enough into the caves, I can feel the sun starting to set,” Zuko explains, accepting the water pouch when Sokka offers it. Neither mentioned it but his voice still sounds weak so he’s been subtly offering water every little while, and Zuko hasn’t been declining it.

“We’ll keep going after we eat, I’m gonna collapse if we don’t eat something soon,” Sokka dramatises, pulling off his pack.

Zuko turns his back to Sokka, “there’s some kindling sticks in the small pack, we can use it for light. Should be close to the top.”

After putting his packs down he sorts through the smaller of the two bags, quickly finding the kindling. A little while later they’re snacking on dried fruits and fish, forgoing rice as they only have so much water until they leave the cave.

“Any ideas where to go? I was going to attempt a map but I can’t tell east from west in here.”

“It’s hard to tell, I know the sun is set but I can’t figure out which direction is which either, we could keep marking our path.”

Sokka sighs, really not looking forward to spending the night in a cave, it wouldn’t be the first time but the fact they don’t know how to get out is disconcerting. Marking their path would maybe work, but it would still likely take well into the next day. The story echoes in his head, *love will guide you*. He’d tell the universe that Zuko didn’t seem to be guiding them anywhere but… he let out a sigh, maybe he was going about this the wrong way. It probably didn’t mean literally, besides could he really say he loves Zuko? He thinks he might, he’s thought so before, but maybe this is proof he doesn’t, or maybe just that his feelings aren’t reciprocated.

“Want to keep going?” Zuko’s voice breaks into Sokka’s thoughts.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

“We need to try something else,” Sokka says, coming to a stop at yet another intersection of caves, marking their path had only further added to Sokka’s theory the caves were changing, as they hadn’t happened upon a single marker in ages but were still seeming to get nowhere.

“You’re not about to tell me to trust in love, are you?” the firebender questions, the flame in his hands had been growing weaker in the last several minutes, with every yawn the cave seemed to get darker and they weren’t any closer to finding their way out.

“Maybe!? I don’t know. Just walking and hoping for the best isn’t exactly doing anything though, we’re no closer to getting out of here than we were an hour ago,” he reasons. Sokka isn’t exactly sure what believing in love was gonna do, maybe nothing, but maybe being stuck in a cave that might lead to their demise was the push he needed.

“So what exactly is your plan then?” Zuko queries, brow knitting in confusion partly behind his hair that had fallen forward throughout their walk.

Sokka let out a shaky breath as he stepped toward Zuko. The flame in his hand sparked with life for a moment, though luckily not enough to burn either of them. The fire calmed as Sokka’s hand raised to his cheek, brushing hair back to meet his eyes, a silent look of assent brought him nearer. The flame vanished just as he pulled Zuko in, the firebender’s arms wrapping around his neck, echoing his action, pulling him close as well.

Sokka hadn’t exactly done much kissing in his life, and he was surprised to find how well their lips fit together, considering how often they don’t understand each other. And in a way it wasn’t at all what he expected, because the kiss wasn’t harsh or filled with *fiery passion*, or any other fire pun he could come up with. No it was soft, maybe a bit reserved, but with an underlying comfort, like the warmth from a fire on a cold night. It ended much too quickly if you asked him, but probably because he was cursing himself for not doing it a week ago when he’d goose-chickened out.

As they pulled away they didn’t have time for longing looks or a quick pull back in, distracted instead by the way the cave lit, stones in the walls glowing a sort of blue, and nearly immediately after, the cave shaking viciously.

“We have to move,” Zuko spoke over the sound of crumbling rock. A warm hand took his, pulling him along after without another word. They ran through the caves as bits of rock rained down on them, following the glowing stones through winding paths. I was barely ten minutes when they ran out of the exit of the cave collapsing onto the ground as they stumbled away from it. Zuko shed his bags quickly, no longer caring about the still slightly shaking cave. Sokka followed quickly, collapsing back down the moment he was done.

He could feel Zuko next to him, watching as he hesitantly leaned over him, a slight pout as if debating something. Sokka grinned for once certain he knew what the firebender was thinking, pulling him down into another kiss. Returning the kiss in earnest Zuko didn’t seem ready to run this time, instead melting in with Sokka, his movement still surprisingly soft but this time with more certainty. Sokka tangled his fingers in his hair as he looped his arms around his neck, pulling him closer.

&#95;&#95;&#95;&#95;

It was unexpectedly easy to settle into the kiss this time, the knot that had been in his stomach for the past week gone. He didn’t pull away as he carefully moved, straddling his waist so he wasn’t twisted over Sokka.

Yelling echoed around them, yanking them from the moment as the sound of an explosion ricocheted through the mountains. “You gotta be fucking kidding me,” he mutters as he moves to sit up when Sokka does, not having much of a choice still being on top of him.

“All I wanted was to kiss him for a little while longer, it’s really not that much to ask,” Sokka echoes his sentiment, cursing the spirits in his own way.

“We should get moving,” Zuko said, gaze focused on the plumb of smoke not too far away, they managed through one side of the mountains without getting attacked, but it seemed they exited right into a battle. As he removed himself from Sokka’s lap he momentarily considered he should be embarrassed, but he couldn’t find the emotion in him, not due to distraction but because he would rather be back kissing him than running into some battle.

Sokka is barely up when they hear a voice yell out, “fallback!”

It’s familiar, to both of them, their attention to the direction the call seemed to come from. It wasn’t but a few minutes when the first sight of blue came around one of the peaks, followed quickly by what was likely the entirety of the Southern Watertribe force. In the commotion they didn’t notice the two of them still partly in the shadow of the cave, not before Sokka yelled out, “Dad!”
